论文对照阅读

基础模型的工具学习

Y. Qin et al.

导读 正文 评论

正式阅读前 · 先结论，再定点深挖

全文导读

这篇是工具学习综述，长、全、容易读成「又一本 API 清单」。下面先给出主张、机制、证据与精读路径。读完这一页，再带着一个具体问题进入正文。

一句话主张。 基础模型负责理解意图、拆任务、做规划；专用工具负责计算、检索、落地执行。二者互补才叫工具学习：从用户指令走到可执行计划，再靠示范或反馈把「会用一种工具」泛化到新工具。

和 AI 工程、智能体、Cordiverse 的关系

Cordiverse 把本文标成引用 [11]：运行时 harness 会组合多样工具套件与执行环境。它解决的是「模型如何学会用工具」——认知起源、界面分类、控制器 / 工具集 / 环境 / 感知器四件套，以及 18 种工具上的提示实验。

对 AI 工程的直接用处可以收成一句：把「会调计算器、搜索、浏览器」从散落论文收成一张可对照的地图，并提前点名造工具（§ 5.3）、知识冲突与安全治理。它和 ReAct / Toolformer 同属「用工具」谱系，但切口是综述与框架，不是又一个调用器。

它没有处理工具在运行时如何卸载、依赖如何声明——那是 Cordiverse 要补的时空可组合性。展开判断见独立的读后评论，此处不占导读。

问题从哪来

人靠工具克服身体限制；早期 AI 能力不够，谈不上稳定、可泛化的工具使用。ChatGPT / GPT-4 让意图理解、规划与多模态感知变得可行，但现有工作多钉在单一任务上，缺少统一框架与系统评测。

缺的不是再堆一个 API，而是：如何理解含糊指令、如何在上下文装不下全部说明书时先选工具、如何用推理做多步多工具规划，以及如何把接口统一到语义 / GUI / 程序三种协议上。

机制（四件套 · 从意图到计划 · 示范或反馈）

图 3（第 11 页）把系统拆成：

  • 工具集：功能各异的 API / GUI / 物理装置。
  • 控制器：通常是基础模型，负责选工具、填参、必要时拆子任务。
  • 环境：虚拟或真实，返回执行结果。
  • 感知器：把环境与人的反馈摘要回控制器，形成闭环。

流程上先做意图理解与工具理解（零样本说明书 / 少样本示范，图 4），再做带推理的规划：内省一次想完、外观边做边看（图 5；ReAct、Toolformer、Inner Monologue）。训练分从示范中学与从反馈中学；泛化靠统一接口，外加元工具学习与课程学习。

作者实际贡献了什么

  • 一般框架：控制器 / 工具集 / 环境 / 感知器，把散落工作接到同一张图上。
  • 从意图到计划的流程：意图、工具理解、内省 / 外观推理、多步多工具挑战。
  • 训练地图：行为克隆、半监督 / 自监督（Toolformer）、RL / RLHF，以及语义 / GUI / 编程三种接口统一。
  • 18 种工具实验（表 1）：简单提示即可用天气、地图、幻灯片；知识图谱、维基、三维建模即使少样本也不够。ChatGPT 并未全面超过 text-davinci-003。
  • 开放问题：安全与治理、大系统、从使用者到制造者、个性化、数字具身、知识冲突、科学发现。

章节地图

章节页这一章回答什么怎么读
摘要与引言 1、4–5 何谓工具学习；图 1 的搜书 / 画铁塔 / 做酸奶分工。 必读。先建立主张。
目录 2–3 章节与附录 18 个案例工具清单。 当地图，圈自己要去的节。
§ 2 背景 6–10 认知起源；物理 / GUI / 程序三层界面；预训练 + 提示范式；工具与模型互补。 关心「为什么是现在」必读图 2。
§ 3 框架与训练 11–21 图 3 四件套；意图与工具理解；内省 / 外观；示范 vs 反馈；三种统一接口。 关心机制必读。这是全文概念核心。
§ 4 实验 21–25 18 种工具；表 1；BMTools / LangChain / ChatGPT Plugins。 对证据时精读表 1 与第 25 页解读。
§ 5 讨论 26–34 安全治理；大系统；造工具（图 7）；个性化；具身；知识冲突（图 8–9）；开放问题。 关心 harness 必读 § 5.1 与 § 5.3。
结论与贡献 34–35 主张收束；谁写哪一节。 看作者承认还缺什么。
参考文献 36–49 书目（英文条目）。 回溯引用时再查。
附录 A 50–75 18 种工具的提示词、代码与调用轨迹（英文原文）。 复现或对照 harness 时按工具翻。

建议的三条精读路径

只想抓住思想

本导读 → 摘要 → 图 1（第 4 页）→ 图 3（第 11 页）→ 结论（第 34 页）。用白话列快翻，对上主张再展开译文。

核对实验数字

表 1（第 23 页）→ 第 25 页解读 → 附录里难工具（知识图谱 A.9、数据库 A.18）。盯「简单提示够用」与「哪些必须真训练」。

关心智能体 / harness

§ 3.2 外观推理（第 15 页）→ § 5.1 安全（第 26 页）→ § 5.3 造工具（第 28 页）。读完再看读后评论：会用、会造，不等于卸得干净。

读正文时的一条纪律

每一轮精读前，先写下这一轮只想搞清的一句话（例如：「内省推理和外观推理差在哪」「表 1 为什么 ChatGPT 没有全面赢」）。读完能用白话复述，这一轮就算过关；复述不出，再回到该页的原文 / 译文 / 白话三列，而不是从头重读。

英文「文本抽取」列支持划词查词（离线词库）。正文工具栏可开关列：先只开「白话解读」扫地图，再打开译文或 PDF 对读。

用顶栏三个标签切换导读、正文与评论。正文工具栏可开关列、翻页跳章节。

第 1 / 75 页

Tool Learning with Foundation Models Yujia Qin1, Shengding Hu1, Yankai Lin2∗, Weize Chen1, Ning Ding1, Ganqu Cui1, Zheni Zeng1, Xuanhe Zhou1, Yufei Huang1, Chaojun Xiao1, Chi Han3, Yi Ren Fung3, Yusheng Su1, Huadong Wang1, Cheng Qian1, Runchu Tian1, Kunlun Zhu8, Shihao Liang8, Xingyu Shen1, Bokai Xu1, Zhen Zhang1, Yining Ye1, Bowen Li1, Ziwei Tang5, Jing Yi1, Yuzhang Zhu1, Zhenning Dai1, Lan Yan1, Xin Cong1, Yaxi Lu1, Weilin Zhao1, Yuxiang Huang1, Junxi Yan1, Xu Han1, Xian Sun7, Dahai Li7, Jason Phang4, Cheng Yang5, Tongshuang Wu6, Heng Ji3, Guoliang Li1, Zhiyuan Liu1∗, Maosong Sun1∗ 1Tsinghua University, 2Renmin University of China, 3University of Illinois Urbana-Champaign, 4New York University, 5Beijing University of Posts and Telecommunications, 6Carnegie Mellon University, 7Zhihu Inc., 8ModelBest Inc. qyj20@mails.tsinghua.edu.cn

Abstract

Humans possess an extraordinary ability to create and utilize tools, allowing them to overcome physical limitations and explore new frontiers. With the advent of recent powerful foundation models, artificial intelligence systems have the potential to be equally adept in tool use as humans. This paradigm, which is dubbed as tool learning with foundation models, combines the strengths of specialized tools and foundation models to achieve enhanced accuracy, efficiency, and automation in problem-solving. Despite its immense potential, there is still a lack of a comprehensive understanding of key challenges, opportunities, and future endeavors in this field. To this end, we present a systematic investigation and comprehensive review of tool learning in this paper. We first introduce the background of tool learning, including its cognitive origins, the paradigm shift of foundation models, and the complementary roles of tools and models. We recapitulate existing tool learning research and formulate a general tool learning framework: starting from understanding the user instruction, models should learn to decompose a complex task into several subtasks, dynamically adjust their plan through reasoning, and effectively conquer each sub-task by selecting appropriate tools. We also discuss how to train models for improved tool-use capabilities and facilitate the generalization in tool learning. Considering the lack of a systematic tool learning evaluation in prior works, we experiment with 18 representative tools and show the potential of current foundation models in skillfully utilizing tools. Finally, we discuss several open problems that require further investigation for tool learning, such as ensuring safe and trustworthy tool use, enabling tool creation with foundation models, and addressing personalization challenges. Overall, we hope this paper could inspire future research in integrating tools with foundation models. Relevant codes and datasets are publicly available for further research exploration1. “It is not only the violin that shapes the violinist, we are all shaped by the tools we train ourselves to use.” — Edsger W. Dijkstra ∗Corresponding authors. 1https://github.com/OpenBMB/BMTools Author contributions are listed in § 6. arXiv:2304.08354v3 [cs.CL] 6 Aug 2024

基础模型的工具学习

Tool Learning with Foundation Models

Yujia Qin1, Shengding Hu1, Yankai Lin2∗, Weize Chen1, Ning Ding1, Ganqu Cui1,

Zheni Zeng1, Xuanhe Zhou1, Yufei Huang1, Chaojun Xiao1, Chi Han3, Yi Ren Fung3,

Yusheng Su1, Huadong Wang1, Cheng Qian1, Runchu Tian1, Kunlun Zhu8, Shihao Liang8,

Yuxiang Huang1, Junxi Yan1, Xu Han1, Xian Sun7, Dahai Li7, Jason Phang4,

Cheng Yang5, Tongshuang Wu6, Heng Ji3, Guoliang Li1, Zhiyuan Liu1∗, Maosong Sun1∗

1清华大学，2中国人民大学，3伊利诺伊大学厄巴纳-香槟分校，

6卡内基梅隆大学，7知乎，8面壁智能

摘要

人类拥有创造并使用工具的非凡能力，从而克服身体限制、开拓新的边界。随着近期强大基础模型的出现，人工智能系统也有望在工具使用上达到与人类相当的娴熟程度。这一范式被本文称为基础模型的工具学习（tool learning with foundation models）：把专用工具的长处与基础模型的长处结合起来，以在问题解决中获得更高的准确性、效率与自动化。尽管潜力巨大，该领域的关键挑战、机遇与未来工作仍缺乏全面认识。为此，本文对工具学习做了系统考察与综述。我们首先介绍工具学习的背景，包括其认知起源、基础模型的范式转变，以及工具与模型的互补角色。随后回顾已有工具学习研究，并归纳出一套一般性框架：从理解用户指令出发，模型应学会把复杂任务分解为若干子任务，通过推理动态调整计划，并选择合适工具有效完成各子任务。我们也讨论如何训练模型以提升工具使用能力，并促进工具学习中的泛化。鉴于先前工作缺少系统评测，我们用 18 种代表性工具做了实验，展示当前基础模型熟练使用工具的潜力。最后讨论若干仍需进一步研究的开放问题，例如确保安全可信的工具使用、让基础模型能够创造工具，以及个性化挑战。总体而言，我们希望本文能启发把工具与基础模型相结合的后续研究。相关代码与数据集已公开，便于进一步探索1。

「塑造小提琴手的不只是小提琴；我们都被自己训练去使用的工具所塑造。」

—— Edsger W. Dijkstra

∗通讯作者。

1https://github.com/OpenBMB/BMTools

作者贡献见第 6 节。

白话解读

本页是全文开场：作者把「基础模型 + 专用工具」命名为工具学习，主张模型应理解意图、拆任务、选工具。后面会给框架、训练方法，并用 18 种工具做实验。 这很重要，因为它把散落的「会调 API」工作收成一个可对照的研究纲领。 类比：像先给「学徒如何用工具」写一本总论，再分章讲认知、分类、训练与评测。

第 4 / 75 页

Introduction Tools are extensions of human capabilities designed to enhance productivity, efficiency, and problem-solving in human activities. Since the dawn of civilization, tools have been integral to the very essence of our existence (Washburn, 1960). Tool creation and utilization are motivated by a deep-rooted desire to overcome our physical limitations and discover new territories. More specifically, with advancements in tools, we can accomplish increasingly complex tasks with ease and efficiency, liberating time and resources to pursue more ambitious ventures. As such, tools have served as the crucial foundation upon which our cultural and social practices are built, transforming our modes of learning, communication, working, and entertainment, infusing these domains with new dimensions of accessibility and interactivity (Gibson et al., 1993). Throughout history, it is undeniable that human beings have played a pivotal role in the invention and manipulation of tools, which is a striking manifestation of intelligence (Shumaker et al., 2011). Given the rise of Artificial Intelligence (AI), one natural question is, does AI possess the potential to be equally adept and capable as its creators? The prerequisite of the manipulation of tools is a thorough comprehension of the tools’ functionalities, as well as the ability to understand user intents and perform planning and reasoning for tool use. Before the advent of powerful foundation models (Bommasani et al., 2021), conducting tool-oriented AI research was exceedingly challenging. While certain basic tools could be fitted using shallow statistical models or deep neural models (Pomerleau, 1988; Mnih et al., 2013; Akkaya et al., 2019), their performance and stability remained inadequate to meet the demands of practical applications, let alone generalizing across various tools. This is due to the limitations of traditional supervised learning in capturing the complex operations essential for tool utilization and the insufficiency of trial-and-error approaches like reinforcement learning in mastering the extensive decision space associated with tool use. In a nutshell, the fundamental limitations in tool use by earlier AI lie in the insufficient capabilities of the models. Recently, the emergence of more capable foundation models, characterized by significantly improved capabilities, has rendered tool learning practicable. They have shown enormous semantic understanding capacity in diverse tasks, spanning the fields of natural language processing (NLP) (Brown et al., 2020), computer vision (CV) (Ramesh et al., 2022), biology (Jumper et al., 2021), etc. Additionally, they have demonstrated superior reasoning and decision-making abilities in complex interactive environments (Nakano et al., 2021). By harnessing the extensive world knowledge garnered during pre-training, they can perform grounded actions and interact with the real world. Notably, the emergence of ChatGPT (OpenAI, 2022) highlights the potential of foundation models to understand human intentions, automate intricate processes, and generate natural responses; the advent of GPT-4 (OpenAI, 2023) offers immense potential for multi-modal perception, which is essential to the real-world grounding ability. Therefore, foundation models enable AI to harness tools, which can lead to more potent and streamlined solutions for real-world tasks. Foundation models are able to decipher complex data, simulate human-like planning capabilities, and generate a broad spectrum of outputs. 引言

工具是人类能力的延伸，旨在提高人类活动中的生产力、效率与问题解决能力。自文明肇始，工具就融入我们存在的核心（Washburn, 1960）。创造与使用工具，源于克服身体限制、发现新疆域的深层欲望。更具体地说，随着工具进步，我们能更轻松、高效地完成日益复杂的任务，从而解放时间与资源去追求更宏大的事业。因此，工具构成了我们文化与社会实践的关键基础，改变了学习、交流、工作与娱乐的方式，并为这些领域注入新的可达性与互动性（Gibson et al., 1993）。纵观历史，人类在发明与操纵工具上扮演了关键角色，这是智能的鲜明体现（Shumaker et al., 2011）。面对人工智能（AI）的兴起，一个自然的问题是：AI 是否有潜力像其创造者一样娴熟而有能力？

操纵工具的前提，是彻底理解工具功能，以及理解用户意图、并为工具使用做规划与推理的能力。在强大基础模型出现之前（Bommasani et al., 2021），做面向工具的 AI 研究极为困难。虽可用浅层统计模型或深度神经网络拟合某些基本工具（Pomerleau, 1988; Mnih et al., 2013; Akkaya et al., 2019），其性能与稳定性仍难满足实际应用，更遑论跨工具泛化。这是因为传统监督学习难以捕捉工具使用所需的复杂操作，而试错式强化学习也不足以掌握与工具使用相关的广阔决策空间。简言之，早期 AI 在工具使用上的根本局限在于模型能力不足。近来，能力显著提升的基础模型使工具学习成为可能。它们在自然语言处理（NLP）（Brown et al., 2020）、计算机视觉（CV）（Ramesh et al., 2022）、生物学（Jumper et al., 2021）等多样任务上展现出巨大的语义理解能力。此外，它们在复杂交互环境中也展现出更强的推理与决策能力（Nakano et al., 2021）。借助预训练中积累的广泛世界知识，它们可以执行有根基的动作并与真实世界交互。值得注意的是，ChatGPT（OpenAI, 2022）的出现凸显了基础模型理解人类意图、自动化复杂流程并生成自然回复的潜力；GPT-4（OpenAI, 2023）则为多模态感知提供了巨大可能，而这对真实世界的 grounding 能力至关重要。

因此，基础模型使 AI 能够驾驭工具，从而为现实任务提供更强、更精简的解决方案。基础模型能够解读复杂数据、模拟类人规划能力，并生成广泛类型的输出。与此同时，专用工具可用于

employed to refine and target specific goals. The amalgamation of tools and models unveils vast potential where sophisticated procedures can be automated with limited human involvement. This paradigm, dubbed as tool learning with foundation models in this paper (Figure 1), aims to combine the strengths of specialized tools and foundation models, thereby culminating in greater accuracy, efficiency, and autonomy in problemsolving. Recent research has shed light on foundation models’ potential to exhibit a level of dexterity and finesse in tool use (Lazaridou et al., 2022; Nakano et al., 2021; Cobbe et al., 2021; Thoppilan et al., 2022; Huang et al., 2022b; Ahn et al., 2022; Yao et al., 2022a,b; Schick et al., 2023; Wu et al., 2023; Bubeck et al., 2023). Despite these breakthroughs, the efforts mainly focus on applying foundation models to specific tasks and domains with delicate algorithm designs. The current understanding of tool learning is still not comprehensive enough to estimate its characteristics and future developments. We believe that it is crucial to examine and summarize the current progress of tool learning with foundation models to explore their potential and challenges and to better pave the way for future technological advancements. In this paper, we conduct a systematic investigation and comprehensive review of tool learning, attempting to build a full grasp of the key challenges, opportunities, and directions in this field. Before delving into the tool learning framework, we introduce essential backgrounds (§ 2), covering both tools and foundation models and their interaction. Specifically, we first recapitulate the cognitive origins of tool use in human history and its potential implications for tool use in AI systems (§ 2.1), followed by a categorization of tools from the perspective of the user interface (§ 2.2). Then we review the AI paradigm shift brought by foundation models and highlight the emergence and significance of tool learning (§ 2.3). After that, we discuss the complementary roles of tools and foundation models, and argue that integrating both can bring various advantages, such as improving interpretability, enhancing robustness, and delivering better user experiences (§ 2.4). We present a comprehensive literature review for existing exploration in tool learning. While previous works often focus on specific aspects in isolation, we strive to formulate a general tool learning framework (§ 3.1), which comprises the controller (typically modeled using a foundation model), tool set, environment, perceiver, and human. Based on the unified framework, we review existing works of tool learning, highlight core research problems, and introduce their existing solutions as well as future explorations. The whole procedure (§ 3.2) of tool learning starts with a user instruction, and models are required to make an executable plan for tool execution. To bridge user instructions with appropriate tools, models should first learn to understand the user intents underlying the instruction (i.e., intent understanding) and understand the functionalities and usage of tools (i.e., tool understanding). Models should also learn to decompose a complex task into several subtasks, dynamically adjust their plan through reasoning, and effectively conquer each sub-task with the appropriate tools. Regarding the training strategy (§ 3.3) to facilitate models for improved tool utilization, we conclude with two mainstream methods: learning from demonstrations and learning from feedback. We discuss how to construct effective training supervision under different settings. To facilitate transferring the learned tool-use skills to new tools and situations, i.e., generalizable tool learning, it is important to design a unified interface that enables the model to interact with different tools in a standardized manner. Considering the lack of a systematic tool learning evaluation in prior works, we conduct experiments (§ 4) on 18 representative tools based on our framework to investigate the efficacy and limitations of foundation models in tool manipulation. We demonstrate that state-of-the-art foundation models (e.g., ChatGPT) can effectively use tools to solve tasks with simple prompting. These results highlight the potential of using the foundation model as a general agent for tool learning. Finally, we discuss the remaining important research topics (§ 5) for applying our general framework to real-world scenarios, including (1) safety and trustworthiness, where we emphasize the potential risks from adversaries, governance, and trustworthiness. We contend that careful considerations are necessary before deploying tool learning models in high-stakes scenarios (§ 5.1); (2) tool learning for large complex systems, where we showcase the unique characters of large complex systems and discuss the challenges in applying tool learning to these systems, such as complicated knowledge and function learning, representative data sampling with privacy concerns, and the strict requirements of efficient tool learning (§ 5.2); (3) tool creation, where we discuss the possibility that AI can also create new tools, challenging the long-held beliefs about what makes humans unique (§ 5.3); (4) personalized tool learning, where models provide tailored assistance to users in tool use. We highlight the challenges of aligning user preference with tool manipulation and introduce the shift from reactive to proactive systems, and the privacy-preserving concerns (§ 5.4); (5) embodied learning, where the intersection of tool learning and embodied agent enables digital embodiment and manipulation of embodied tools (§ 5.5); (6) knowledge conflicts in tool augmentation, where we review how tools can be leveraged to enhance models’ generation and the practical problems of knowledge conflicts, which can lead to inaccurate and unreliable model predictions (§ 5.6); (7) other open problems, such as viewing tool use capability as a measure for machine intelligence and tool learning for scientific discovery (§ 5.7). Overall, we

来精炼并瞄准具体目标。工具与模型的融合展现出巨大潜力：复杂流程可以在有限人工介入下自动化。这一范式在本文中称为基础模型的工具学习（图 1），旨在结合专用工具与基础模型的长处，从而在问题解决中达到更高的准确性、效率与自主性。近期研究已揭示基础模型在工具使用上可表现出相当的灵巧与精细（Lazaridou et al., 2022; Nakano et al., 2021; Cobbe et al., 2021; Thoppilan et al., 2022; Huang et al., 2022b; Ahn et al., 2022; Yao et al., 2022a,b; Schick et al., 2023; Wu et al., 2023; Bubeck et al., 2023）。尽管有这些突破，现有工作大多把基础模型用于特定任务与领域，并依赖精巧的算法设计。对工具学习的理解仍不够全面，难以估计其特性与未来发展。我们认为，有必要审视并总结基础模型工具学习的当前进展，以探索其潜力与挑战，并为后续技术发展铺路。

本文对工具学习做系统考察与全面综述，试图完整把握该领域的关键挑战、机遇与方向。在进入工具学习框架之前，我们介绍必要背景（§ 2），涵盖工具、基础模型及其交互。具体地，我们首先回顾人类历史上工具使用的认知起源及其对 AI 系统工具使用的潜在启示（§ 2.1），随后从用户界面视角对工具分类（§ 2.2）。然后回顾基础模型带来的 AI 范式转变，并强调工具学习的出现与意义（§ 2.3）。之后讨论工具与基础模型的互补角色，并主张二者结合可带来可解释性、鲁棒性与更好用户体验等好处（§ 2.4）。

我们对已有工具学习探索做了全面文献回顾。先前工作往往孤立地关注某些方面，我们则力求提出一般性框架（§ 3.1），包含控制器（通常由基础模型建模）、工具集、环境、感知器与人。基于统一框架，我们回顾已有工作，标出核心研究问题，并介绍现有方案与未来探索。工具学习的整条流程（§ 3.2）从用户指令开始，模型需为工具执行制定可执行计划。为把用户指令与合适工具桥接起来，模型首先应学会理解指令背后的用户意图（即意图理解），并理解工具的功能与用法（即工具理解）。模型还应学会把复杂任务分解为若干子任务，通过推理动态调整计划，并用合适工具有效攻克各子任务。关于促进模型更好利用工具的训练策略（§ 3.3），我们归纳为两条主流路径：从示范中学习与从反馈中学习。我们讨论在不同设置下如何构造有效的训练监督。为便于把已学到的工具使用技能迁移到新工具与新情境——即可泛化的工具学习——重要的是设计统一接口，使模型能以标准化方式与不同工具交互。

鉴于先前工作缺少系统评测，我们基于该框架在 18 种代表性工具上做了实验（§ 4），考察基础模型在工具操纵上的效能与局限。我们表明，最先进的基础模型（如 ChatGPT）只需简单提示即可有效用工具解题。这些结果凸显了把基础模型当作工具学习通用智能体的潜力。

最后，我们讨论把一般框架应用到现实场景时仍重要的研究课题（§ 5），包括：（1）安全与可信，强调来自对手、治理与可信性的潜在风险。我们主张在高风险场景部署工具学习模型前必须仔细考量（§ 5.1）；（2）面向大型复杂系统的工具学习，展示这类系统的独特之处，并讨论应用挑战，如复杂知识与函数学习、兼顾隐私的代表性数据采样，以及对高效工具学习的严格要求（§ 5.2）；（3）工具创造，讨论 AI 也可能创造新工具，从而挑战关于「人之所以为人」的长期信念（§ 5.3）；（4）个性化工具学习，模型在工具使用中为用户提供量身协助。我们强调对齐用户偏好与工具操纵的挑战，并介绍从反应式到主动式系统的转变以及隐私保护问题（§ 5.4）；（5）具身学习，工具学习与具身智能体的交叉使数字具身与对具身工具的操纵成为可能（§ 5.5）；（6）工具增强中的知识冲突，回顾如何用工具增强模型生成，以及知识冲突这一实际问题，它可导致不准确、不可靠的预测（§ 5.6）；（7）其他开放问题，例如把工具使用能力视为机器智能的度量，以及面向科学发现的工具学习（§ 5.7）。总体而言，我们

hope this paper could inspire further research in integrating tools with foundation models and developing more intelligent and capable AI systems. Background In this section, we first discuss the cognitive origins of human tool use (§ 2.1), followed by a tool categorization through the lens of the user interface (§ 2.2). Then we review the recent AI paradigm shift brought by foundation models (§ 2.3) and its significance in tool learning. After that, we examine the respective roles of specialized tools and foundation models in problem-solving, and discuss the benefits and challenges of their integration (§ 2.4). 2.1 Cognitive Origins of Tool Use Tools have played a critical role in the long history of thousands of years of human evolution. Tools are commonly viewed as extensions of human beings, just as ancient fighting equipment and agricultural machinery were. Tool use is defined as a unique characteristic of human beings that is distinguished from other species (Von Eckardt, 1995). Throughout evolution, the ability to use tools has been essential for animals, particularly those with advanced intellectual development (Shumaker et al., 2011). For example, chimpanzees have been observed using stones or other materials to crack nuts (Boesch et al., 2019), while New Caledonian crows can craft and utilize two distinct types of hook tools to aid in capturing prey (Hunt, 1996). However, human tool behavior diverges from these observations in several ways. Humans can create much more complicated tools than other animals, such as converting our actions into fundamentally different mechanical actions in tools like hammers and pencils (Frey, 2007). Additionally, we can harness natural forces such as wind turbines to create tools (Shumaker et al., 2011). This ability may be attributed to our deep comprehension of cause-and-effect relations, which allows us to engage in technical reasoning (Osiurak & Reynaud, 2020). Neural Basis of Tool Use. To better understand human tool use behaviors, researchers analyze the neural basis of tool observation and execution. It is verified that humans have parietal systems involved in grasping objects and using tools, and the anterior supramarginal gyrus activation of observing tool use is typical of human subjects, of which macaques do not exhibit (Orban & Caruana, 2014). This neurocognitive bases of tool observation may be related to the origins of cumulative technological evolution (e.g., the improvement in the efficiency and complexity of human tools and techniques over generations (Osiurak & Reynaud, 2020)) and some other human phenomena (Reynaud et al., 2019). While for the tool execution, researchers hold different views on manipulation-based versus reasoning-based approaches (Osiurak & Badets, 2016). The former claims that tool use has to be supported by the simulation of sensorimotor experiences, and the latter demonstrates the importance of reasoning based on mechanical knowledge in tool use. Nevertheless, the overall trend in cognitive science is understanding cognition as an enactive process that emphasizes interaction with the external world (Engel et al., 2013), and the feedback from observation, communication, and hands-on practice is important for mastering tool use. Three Intelligence Levels of Tool Use. Besides, there are specific frameworks designed to discuss the level of intelligence represented by human tool use. For instance, “intoolligence” (Osiurak & Heinke, 2018) divides the tool use behavior into three modes: assistive tool use is usually passive and unaware (e.g., walking in the rain shelter corridor); arbitrary tool use requires active interaction (e.g., driving, using smart phones); free tool use further needs to comprehend and choose appropriate tools for the scenarios (e.g., cooking new dishes). In this framework, the three modes of tool use present a progressive relationship, and the authors assume that the key cognitive process for achieving free tool use is technical reasoning, which allows someone to learn new actions by observing others using, selecting, or making a tool instead of numerous practices. Transition from Physical Tools to Conceptual Tools. Apart from tools in the physical world, we can also turn to more abstract tools. Take cognitive tools (Heyes, 2018) as an example: it refers to an auxiliary aid that facilitates higher-order thinking (e.g., multi-step critical analysis, the generation of creative problem-solving solutions). Cognitive tools can be classified based on the functionalities they provide (Lajoie & Derry, 2013). These include (1) supporting cognitive processes (e.g., documenting intermediate reasoning outcomes), (2) alleviating lower-level cognitive load to free up resources for advanced-level thinking, (3) enabling learners to engage in activities out of their reach and (4) allowing learners to generate and test hypotheses (e.g., simulated diagnoses for medical students).

希望本文能启发把工具与基础模型相结合、并发展更智能、更有能力的 AI 系统的进一步研究。

背景

本节首先讨论人类工具使用的认知起源（§ 2.1），随后从用户界面视角做工具分类（§ 2.2）。然后回顾基础模型带来的近期 AI 范式转变（§ 2.3）及其对工具学习的意义。之后考察专用工具与基础模型在问题解决中的各自角色，并讨论二者整合的收益与挑战（§ 2.4）。

2.1 工具使用的认知起源

在数千年的人类演化长史中，工具扮演了关键角色。工具通常被视为人的延伸，正如古代兵器与农具。工具使用被定义为人类有别于其他物种的独特特征（Von Eckardt, 1995）。在演化过程中，使用工具的能力对动物、尤其是智力较发达的动物至关重要（Shumaker et al., 2011）。例如，黑猩猩被观察到用石头或其他材料砸开坚果（Boesch et al., 2019），新喀里多尼亚乌鸦能制作并使用两种不同的钩状工具来捕获猎物（Hunt, 1996）。然而，人类的工具行为在若干方面偏离这些观察。人类能创造远比其他动物复杂的工具，例如把自身动作转化为锤子、铅笔等工具中根本不同的机械动作（Frey, 2007）。此外，我们还能驾驭自然力，如用风力涡轮机来制造工具（Shumaker et al., 2011）。这种能力或可归因于我们对因果关系的深刻理解，从而使技术推理成为可能（Osiurak & Reynaud, 2020）。

工具使用的神经基础。为更好理解人类工具使用行为，研究者分析了观察工具与执行工具的神经基础。已证实人类有参与抓取物体与使用工具的顶叶系统，观察工具使用时前缘上回的激活是人类受试的典型表现，猕猴并不表现（Orban & Caruana, 2014）。这种工具观察的神经认知基础可能与累积性技术演化的起源有关（例如人类工具与技术在代际间效率与复杂度的提升（Osiurak & Reynaud, 2020））以及其他一些人类现象（Reynaud et al., 2019）。至于工具执行，研究者对「基于操纵」与「基于推理」持不同观点（Osiurak & Badets, 2016）。前者主张工具使用必须由感觉运动经验的模拟支撑，后者则强调基于机械知识的推理的重要性。尽管如此，认知科学的总体趋势是把认知理解为强调与外部世界交互的生成性（enactive）过程（Engel et al., 2013），而来自观察、交流与动手实践的反馈对掌握工具使用很重要。

工具使用的三个智能层次。此外，还有专门框架讨论人类工具使用所代表的智能水平。例如，「intoolligence」（Osiurak & Heinke, 2018）把工具使用行为分为三种模式：辅助性工具使用通常是被动且无意识的（如走在避雨走廊里）；任意性工具使用需要主动交互（如开车、用智能手机）；自由工具使用则进一步需要理解并为场景选择合适工具（如做新菜）。在该框架中，三种模式呈递进关系，作者假定实现自由工具使用的关键认知过程是技术推理：它使人能通过观察他人使用、选择或制造工具来学习新动作，而无需大量练习。

从物理工具到概念工具的过渡。除物理世界中的工具外，我们也可求助于更抽象的工具。以认知工具（Heyes, 2018）为例：它指促进高阶思维的辅助手段（如多步批判分析、创造性问题解决方案的生成）。认知工具可按其所提供的功能分类（Lajoie & Derry, 2013）。包括（1）支持认知过程（如记录中间推理结果），（2）减轻低层认知负荷以释放资源给高层思维，（3）使学习者能从事原本够不到的活动，以及（4）让学习者生成并检验假设（如医学生的模拟诊断）。

白话解读

本页从认知科学讲「人为什么会用工具」：神经基础、三种智能层次，以及从锤子到「认知工具」的抽象。 作者把人类的技术推理当作后文框架的隐喻：模型也要能观察、推理、选工具。 类比：小孩先会被雨棚「带着走」，再学会开车，最后能自己挑菜刀做新菜。

2.2 Tool Categorization: A User-Interface Perspective Bridging the Gap between Human and Machine Tool Use. First, the abilities to manipulate tools are deeply rooted in our cognitive and perceptual systems and have evolved over millions of years. In contrast, foundation models rely primarily on statistical patterns of pre-training data, and significant gaps still exist between the tool-use capabilities of foundation models and their human counterparts. Humans can perceive the properties of tools, understand their functionalities, and identify the appropriate tools for each task. Gaining insights from this, we investigate and discuss how foundation models can learn such a process in § 3.2.1. Second, humans excel at breaking down complex tasks into smaller, manageable subtasks and deftly manipulating tools to accomplish each sub-task. However, foundation models lack the physical embodiment and sensory experience necessary to fully understand and utilize tools. As a result, these models often struggle with tasks that require higher-order reasoning and adaptivity, and they cannot trustfully integrate multiple sources of knowledge and tools effectively. We will discuss how to better make executable plans leveraging models’ reasoning abilities in § 3.2.2. Furthermore, current algorithms for adapting foundation models to learn specific tools generally require huge amounts of supervised data (Nakano et al., 2021; Reed et al., 2022), hindering their generalization and transferability to broader types of tools or novel situations. Hence we first summarize the training strategies for tool learning (§ 3.3.1 and § 3.3.2) and discuss how to facilitate the generalization and transferability of tool learning (§ 3.3.3). 2.2 Tool Categorization: A User-Interface Perspective The growing number and complexity of tools in our world make it increasingly important to understand and group them in a meaningful way. A system for classifying these tools helps us better grasp their uses, benefits, and potential for growth. In this paper, our focus is particularly on those tools that can be manipulated using instructions in conjunction with foundation models. We introduce a taxonomy that sorts these tools based on their modes of expression and interaction. As depicted in Figure 2, this taxonomy incorporates three levels of interaction, arranged from the most tangible to the least. The physical level involves direct physical interactions with tools. The graphical user interface (GUI) level facilitates user interaction with visual representations of tools. The program level involves users engaging directly with the underlying source code of tools. Physical Interaction-based Tools. We start with the most tangible genre of tools, physical interaction-based tools. As the name suggests, this class of tools involves direct interactions with the physical world, including devices like robots, sensors, and wearables that could physically impact the environment. Physical interaction tools have the capability to sense and respond to the physical environment of users, making them useful in a wide range of applications, from manufacturing to healthcare and education. Physical interaction tools are close to the real world, and they have the potential to substantially improve efficiency and productivity. For example, robots can perform from simple to intricate, even adventurous tasks to reduce human errors and labor costs. Sensors can collect valuable data, such as temperature and pressure, allowing for real-time monitoring and optimization of industrial processes. Wearables, on the other hand, provide users with a personalized experience by tracking physiological or environmental parameters, promoting health and safety. It is worth noting that although the output of such tools interacts with the real world at the physical level, users may also create the input of the tools at the GUI or source code level. GUI-based Tools. Some tools allow users to manipulate them through an interactive interface, i.e., visual representations of tools, with pre-defined operations. These tools, defined as GUI-based tools, do not have a direct impact on the physical world. The GUI interface typically includes buttons, menus, text boxes, and other graphical elements that allow users to interact with the underlying system. These tools are extensively employed in various industries and applications such as software development, data analysis, and design. Particularly, GUI-based tools can improve productivity by streamlining workflows and automating repetitive tasks. GUI-based tools could considerably simplify complex tasks and reduce the learning curve for nontechnical users. From this viewpoint, tool learning with foundation models share the same primary goal, which simplifies intricate tasks to a natural language format. Representative GUI-based tools are usually well-developed software such as browsers, Microsoft Office, Adobe PhotoShop, etc. These applications showcase the versatility that graphical interfaces can provide and enable users to access and manipulate complex features within the software. On the other hand, the main limitation of GUI-based tools is that they may not provide the flexibility and customizability of command-line interfaces or APIs. In specific scenarios that require rapid and mass responses, as well as greater and flexible control, the visual interface may not always be the most effective way to interact with a system. Program-based Tools. The innermost layer of tools that users can access is the source code, offering a high degree of flexibility for the input and output of these program-based tools. Program-based tools are software tools primarily designed for use through programming interfaces rather than visual interfaces. They can take

2.2 工具分类：用户界面视角

弥合人类与机器工具使用的差距。首先，操纵工具的能力深深植根于我们的认知与感知系统，并经历了数百万年演化。相比之下，基础模型主要依赖预训练数据中的统计模式，其工具使用能力与人类之间仍有显著差距。人类能感知工具属性、理解其功能，并为每项任务识别合适工具。受此启发，我们在 § 3.2.1 讨论基础模型如何学习这一过程。其次，人类擅长把复杂任务拆成更小、可管理的子任务，并灵巧地操纵工具完成各子任务。然而，基础模型缺乏充分理解与利用工具所需的物理具身与感官经验。因此，这些模型常在需要高阶推理与适应性的任务上挣扎，也无法可靠地整合多源知识与工具。我们将在 § 3.2.2 讨论如何更好利用模型推理能力制定可执行计划。此外，使基础模型适应特定工具的现有算法通常需要大量监督数据（Nakano et al., 2021; Reed et al., 2022），阻碍其向更广工具类型或新情境的泛化与迁移。因此我们先总结工具学习的训练策略（§ 3.3.1 与 § 3.3.2），再讨论如何促进工具学习的泛化与可迁移性（§ 3.3.3）。

2.2 工具分类：用户界面视角

世界上工具的数量与复杂度不断增长，以有意义的方式理解并分组它们变得愈发重要。一套分类系统有助于更好把握其用途、收益与增长潜力。本文尤其关注那些可与基础模型一道、用指令来操纵的工具。我们引入一种按表达与交互方式排序的分类法。如图 2 所示，该分类法包含三个交互层次，从最具体到最抽象排列。物理层涉及与工具的直接物理交互。图形用户界面（GUI）层促进用户与工具的视觉表征交互。程序层则让用户直接与工具的底层源代码交互。

基于物理交互的工具。我们从最具体的一类工具开始：基于物理交互的工具。顾名思义，这类工具涉及与物理世界的直接交互，包括机器人、传感器与可穿戴设备等可对环境影响产生物理作用的装置。物理交互工具能感知并回应用户的物理环境，因而在制造、医疗与教育等广泛应用中有用。它们贴近真实世界，有潜力显著提高效率与生产力。例如，机器人可执行从简单到复杂甚至冒险的任务，以减少人为错误与劳动力成本。传感器可采集温度、压力等有价值数据，实现对工业过程的实时监控与优化。可穿戴设备则通过追踪生理或环境参数为用户提供个性化体验，促进健康与安全。值得注意的是，尽管这类工具的输出在物理层与真实世界交互，用户仍可能在 GUI 或源代码层创建工具的输入。

基于 GUI 的工具。有些工具允许用户通过交互界面——即工具的视觉表征——用预定义操作来操纵它们。这些被定义为基于 GUI 的工具，并不直接作用于物理世界。GUI 界面通常包括按钮、菜单、文本框及其他图形元素，使用户能与底层系统交互。这类工具广泛用于软件开发、数据分析与设计等行业与应用。尤其是，基于 GUI 的工具可通过理顺工作流、自动化重复任务来提高生产力。它们能显著简化复杂任务，并降低非技术用户的学习曲线。从这个角度看，基础模型的工具学习共享同一首要目标：把复杂任务简化为自然语言形式。代表性的 GUI 工具通常是浏览器、Microsoft Office、Adobe PhotoShop 等成熟软件。这些应用展示了图形界面所能提供的多样性，使用户能访问并操纵软件中的复杂功能。另一方面，基于 GUI 的工具的主要局限是：它们可能无法提供命令行界面或 API 那样的灵活性与可定制性。在需要快速、大批量响应以及更大、更灵活控制的特定场景中，视觉界面未必总是与系统交互的最有效方式。

基于程序的工具。用户能访问的最内层工具是源代码，为这类基于程序的工具的输入与输出提供高度灵活性。基于程序的工具主要是通过编程接口而非视觉界面使用的软件工具。它们可以采取

2.3 Paradigm Shift of Foundation Models (a) Physical Interaction-based Tools Observation Physical Tools Agent Real world Observation User Tools Observation Programming Interface Developer Graphical User Interface (GUI) (b) GUI-based Tools (c) Program-based Tools Database Knowledge Graph Softwares/SDK Physical world to virtual world Visual operation to programming operation Web Photoshop Figure 2: Tool categorization from the perspective of the user interface: (1) physical interaction-based tools, (b) GUI-based tools, and (c) program-based tools. various forms, including declarative languages, programming libraries, software development kits (SDKs), and even neural network-based tools. These tools are typically used by developers or technical users who possess a deeper understanding of the underlying data, system or technology, with which the users could complete complex software applications. The main advantage of program-based tools is that they provide greater flexibility and customizability than GUI-based tools, and users can build more sophisticated solutions for current problems. As a result, such tools also have a steeper learning curve than GUI-based tools, they require a greater degree of technical expertise and programming knowledge, which may not be accessible to non-technical users. For example, program-based tools can be more time-consuming to set up and configure and may require more maintenance and support in the learning process. It is noteworthy that, although these tools pose difficulties for human beings in terms of the learning curve, they may not have the same level of challenges for foundation models. It can be seen that the above three interaction modes have varying levels of connectivity with the tool kernel. They are not strictly mutually exclusive but indicate a tendency to intermingle with each other. Human beings have the ability to deal with complex tasks by flexibly executing tools of different types. In this paper, we contend that regardless of the tool type, it is fundamentally possible to leverage foundation models to execute them by setting up intermediary interfaces. We will introduce ways to unify the interface of different tools for foundation models in § 3.3.3. 2.3 Paradigm Shift of Foundation Models In recent years, the field of natural language processing (NLP) has undergone a paradigm shift, marked by the advent of pre-trained language models (PLMs) (Devlin et al., 2019; Bommasani et al., 2021; Han et al., 2021). Prior to this breakthrough, NLP was a challenging field that necessitated designing separate learning objectives for distinct research domains, such as dependency parsing (Kübler et al., 2009), named entity recognition (Nadeau & Sekine, 2007), and summarization (Nenkova & McKeown, 2012). Despite the successful design of effective models and methods for these specific tasks, the separated nature of this paradigm impeded progress toward a holistic comprehension of language, thereby limiting its potential. The invention of PLMs changes this paradigm. Building on Transformers (Vaswani et al., 2017), PLMs are trained on massive corpora, from which general linguistic ability and world knowledge are learned. This technique has expedited the unification of NLP tasks, giving rise to the pre-train-then-fine-tune paradigm, which has achieved new state-of-the-art performance on several NLP benchmarks, such as GLUE (Wang et al., 2019b) and SuperGLUE (Wang et al., 2019a). At this stage, each task shares the same starting point and only diverges as the task-specific adaptation proceeds. The fusion of task paradigms is still ongoing. T5 (Raffel et al., 2020) transforms all NLP tasks into a text-to-text format with textual descriptions, while GPT-3 (Brown et al., 2020) has discovered that introducing appropriate textual prompts can yield the desired output for specific tasks. Prompts, essentially serving as a natural language interface, are widely believed to stimulate the knowledge learned by PLMs during pre-training. Prompts can enable downstream tasks to be executed without updating model parameters for big models such as GPT-3. Research even suggests that with appropriate prompt guidance, models can perform complex reasoning tasks (Wei et al., 2022c; Wang et al., 2022a). Also, prompts formulated in a natural language format possess remarkable generalization capabilities. Specifically,

图 2：从用户界面视角的工具分类：（1）基于物理交互的工具，（b）基于 GUI 的工具，以及（c）基于程序的工具。

多种形式，包括声明式语言、程序库、软件开发工具包（SDK），甚至基于神经网络的工具。这些工具通常由对底层数据、系统或技术有更深理解的开发者或技术用户使用，用户可据此完成复杂软件应用。基于程序的工具的主要优势是比 GUI 工具提供更大的灵活性与可定制性，用户可为当前问题构建更精细的解决方案。因此，这类工具的学习曲线也比 GUI 工具更陡，需要更高程度的技术专长与编程知识，非技术用户可能无法触及。例如，基于程序的工具在搭建与配置上可能更耗时，学习过程中也可能需要更多维护与支持。值得注意的是，尽管这些工具对人类而言学习曲线陡峭，对基础模型却未必有同等程度的挑战。

可见，上述三种交互模式与工具内核的连通程度不同。它们并非严格互斥，而呈现出相互交织的倾向。人类有能力通过灵活执行不同类型的工具来处理复杂任务。本文主张：无论工具类型如何，原则上都可以通过设置中间接口、借助基础模型来执行它们。我们将在 § 3.3.3 介绍如何为基础模型统一不同工具的接口。

2.3 基础模型的范式转变

近年来，自然语言处理（NLP）领域经历了范式转变，其标志是预训练语言模型（PLM）的出现（Devlin et al., 2019; Bommasani et al., 2021; Han et al., 2021）。在此突破之前，NLP 是一个需要为不同研究领域分别设计学习目标的困难领域，如依存句法分析（Kübler et al., 2009）、命名实体识别（Nadeau & Sekine, 2007）与摘要（Nenkova & McKeown, 2012）。尽管为这些特定任务成功设计了有效模型与方法，这种分离范式阻碍了通向对语言整体理解的进展，从而限制了其潜力。

PLM 的发明改变了这一范式。建立在 Transformer（Vaswani et al., 2017）之上，PLM 在大规模语料上训练，从中学到一般语言能力与世界知识。该技术加速了 NLP 任务的统一，催生了「先预训练再微调」范式，并在 GLUE（Wang et al., 2019b）与 SuperGLUE（Wang et al., 2019a）等若干 NLP 基准上取得新的最优性能。在这一阶段，各任务共享同一起点，仅在任务特定适应推进时才分叉。任务范式的融合仍在继续。T5（Raffel et al., 2020）把所有 NLP 任务变成带文本描述的 text-to-text 格式，而 GPT-3（Brown et al., 2020）发现引入合适的文本提示即可得到特定任务的期望输出。提示本质上充当自然语言接口，被广泛认为能激发 PLM 在预训练中学到的知识。对 GPT-3 等大模型，提示可在不更新模型参数的情况下执行下游任务。研究甚至表明，在合适提示引导下，模型能执行复杂推理任务（Wei et al., 2022c; Wang et al., 2022a）。此外，以自然语言格式表述的提示具有显著的泛化能力。具体而言，

2.4 Complementary Roles of Tools and Foundation Models models that have undergone fine-tuning using diverse instructions are able to effectively generalize to new, unseen data (Wei et al., 2022a; Sanh et al., 2022). Overall, prompts demonstrate a proof-of-concept that uses PLMs as the underlying infrastructure and natural language as the medium to uniformly perform various tasks. A highly successful example is ChatGPT, where all the natural language understanding and generation processes are accomplished through conversational interactions. Nevertheless, there exist numerous tasks that transcend the scope of purely natural language. For instance, generating presentation slides2, constructing 3D models via CAD applications, and scheduling meetings through the analysis of team member calendars are examples of complex tasks that have not been defined in traditional artificial intelligence. Fortunately, the strong generalization ability of PLM enables us to use natural language as a medium to accomplish these tasks by manipulating tools (Zeng et al., 2022). Essentially, the key to tool learning is to decompose complex tasks into sub-actions, tokenize actions in the form of natural language and convert them into executable instructions that can be understood by specific tools. Language models serve as “translators”, making complex tasks more accessible to individuals without specialized technical knowledge. The potential applications of tool learning are vast and exciting, ranging from automated customer service and personal assistants to self-driving cars and even space exploration. By enabling machines to understand and interact with human language in a more natural and nuanced way, we can unlock new possibilities for collaboration and problem-solving that were previously impossible. We anticipate that tool learning will prove instrumental in facilitating the integration of diverse tasks through shared tooling. Thus, while natural language interfaces have enabled unification within the realm of language (Hao et al., 2022), the challenges posed by non-linguistic tasks necessitate a more advanced approach to leveraging both natural language and tool learning. By harnessing the power of natural language, we can create systems that are capable of understanding and adapting to the complex and dynamic world around us, opening up new avenues for innovation and discovery. 2.4 Complementary Roles of Tools and Foundation Models The integration of specialized tools and foundation models represents a promising approach for harnessing the unique strengths of both. By incorporating foundation models’ understanding and reasoning capabilities into specialized tools, we can create intelligent tools capable of performing more complex tasks than either specialized tools or foundation models alone. Specifically, the amalgamation of both confers a multitude of benefits as follows. Benefits of Tools. Tools that are designed to streamline concrete and specific objectives bring several benefits for tool learning: (1) Mitigation for Memorization. Although foundation models have demonstrated an exceptional ability to memorize (Carlini et al., 2021, 2022, 2023), they are not capable of memorizing every piece of training data. Furthermore, foundation models are often prompted with a relatively short context during model generation, thus not all the memorized knowledge can be properly steered (Mialon et al., 2023). Additionally, memorization alone does not support the real-time coverage of up-to-date knowledge, especially in light of the potentially infinite possibilities of novel requests from users. Besides, foundation models are also criticized to hallucinate knowledge (Roller et al., 2021; Shuster et al., 2021) by generating seemingly plausible but non-factual content. This is unacceptable in applications like financial transactions that require the results are 100% correct. Given the above factors, it is necessary to augment foundation models with real-time tool execution to mitigate limitations in memorization. For instance, a significant proportion of the memorization burden can be offloaded to the search engine and database systems (of different modes) if foundation models can learn how to utilize it. (2) Enhanced Expertise. Specialized tools are designed to cater to specific domains with functionalities that are not available in foundation models. As a result, they are better suited to address the needs of domain-specific tasks, such as Wolfram 3 for scientific calculation, through the utilization of tailored algorithms. Instead of solely relying on the foundation model to accomplish the task, models could invoke appropriate tools to generalize to a wider range of tasks that are beyond their capabilities. (3) Better Interpretability. Foundation models are criticized for lacking transparency in their decision-making process (Linardatos et al., 2020), which can be a significant concern in applications such as healthcare or finance, where interpretability is critical for making informed decisions. In contrast, the process of tool execution reflects the whole process of how models solve complex requests, which allows for better interpretability and transparency. Users can easily understand why certain tools are called and how they contribute to the final output, which can improve trust and facilitate human-machine collaboration. (4) Improved Robustness. Foundation models are susceptible to adversarial attacks (Wallace et al., 2019; Jin 2https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365 3https://www.wolframalpha.com/

2.4 工具与基础模型的互补角色

经过多样指令微调的模型能够有效泛化到新的、未见过的数据（Wei et al., 2022a; Sanh et al., 2022）。总体而言，提示证明了一种概念：以 PLM 为底层基础设施、以自然语言为媒介，统一执行各种任务。一个极为成功的例子是 ChatGPT，其中所有自然语言理解与生成过程都通过对话交互完成。

然而，存在大量超越纯自然语言范围的任务。例如，生成演示文稿幻灯片2、通过 CAD 应用构建三维模型、通过分析团队成员日历来安排会议，都是传统人工智能中尚未定义的复杂任务。幸运的是，PLM 的强泛化能力使我们能以自然语言为媒介、通过操纵工具来完成这些任务（Zeng et al., 2022）。本质上，工具学习的关键是把复杂任务分解为子动作，以自然语言形式把动作符号化，并转换成特定工具能理解的可执行指令。语言模型充当「翻译器」，使复杂任务对没有专门技术知识的人更可及。工具学习的潜在应用广阔而令人兴奋，从自动化客服与个人助理，到自动驾驶甚至太空探索。通过使机器以更自然、更细腻的方式理解并与人类语言交互，我们可以解锁先前不可能的协作与问题解决新可能。我们预期工具学习将在通过共享工具促进多样任务整合方面发挥关键作用。因此，尽管自然语言接口已在语言领域实现了统一（Hao et al., 2022），非语言任务带来的挑战需要一种更先进的方法来同时利用自然语言与工具学习。借助自然语言的力量，我们可以创造能够理解并适应周围复杂动态世界的系统，为创新与发现开辟新途径。

2.4 工具与基础模型的互补角色

把专用工具与基础模型整合，是发挥二者独特长处的有前景途径。通过把基础模型的理解与推理能力纳入专用工具，我们可以创造比单独使用专用工具或基础模型更能完成复杂任务的智能工具。具体而言，二者融合带来如下诸多收益。

工具的好处。

旨在理顺具体、特定目标的工具为工具学习带来若干好处：（1）缓解记忆负担。尽管基础模型已展现出非凡的记忆能力（Carlini et al., 2021, 2022, 2023），它们并不能记住每一条训练数据。此外，模型生成时往往只有相对短的上下文提示，因而并非所有已记忆知识都能被恰当引导（Mialon et al., 2023）。再者，单靠记忆无法支持对最新知识的实时覆盖，尤其是面对用户可能无限的新颖请求。此外，基础模型还因幻觉知识而受到批评（Roller et al., 2021; Shuster et al., 2021），即生成看似合理但非事实的内容。这在金融交易等要求结果 100% 正确的应用中不可接受。鉴于上述因素，有必要用实时工具执行来增强基础模型，以缓解记忆上的局限。例如，若基础模型能学会使用搜索引擎与（不同模式的）数据库系统，相当一部分记忆负担便可卸载出去。（2）增强专业能力。专用工具为特定领域设计，具备基础模型所没有的功能。因此，它们更适合应对领域特定任务，如用于科学计算的 Wolfram3，通过定制算法实现。模型不必单独完成任务，而可调用合适工具，泛化到超出自身能力的更广任务。（3）更好的可解释性。基础模型因决策过程缺乏透明度而受批评（Linardatos et al., 2020），这在医疗或金融等可解释性对知情决策至关重要的应用中可能是重大关切。相比之下，工具执行过程反映了模型如何解决复杂请求的全过程，从而带来更好的可解释性与透明度。用户可以容易理解为何调用某些工具、它们如何贡献于最终输出，从而增进信任并促进人机协作。（4）提升鲁棒性。基础模型易受对抗攻击（Wallace et al., 2019; Jin

2https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-365

3https://www.wolframalpha.com/

et al., 2020), where slight modifications to the input can flip the model prediction. This is because these models heavily rely on statistical patterns in the training data. Conversely, tools are designed specifically for their intended use cases, which may be agnostic to the input perturbation. This makes tools more resistant to adversarial attacks. Overall, incorporating tools into the workflow of foundation models can improve the robustness of the system and reduce the risk of malicious attacks. This harmonious interplay between tools and models can enhance the reliability of the system against unpredictable real-world environments. In § 4 and appendix A, we use concrete examples to show how tools can enhance the model’s capabilities in various tasks. Benefits of Foundation Models. Foundation models can provide a solid basis for understanding, planning, reasoning, and generation, which bring several benefits for tool learning as follows: (1) Improved DecisionMaking and Reasoning Abilities. Foundation models are trained on vast amounts of data, enabling them to acquire world knowledge across a wide range of domains. If properly steered, such knowledge can be wielded to perform decision-making and planning over prolonged time horizons (Huang et al., 2022a). Besides, foundation models have demonstrated remarkable reasoning abilities (Wei et al., 2022c; Wang et al., 2022a), thereby enabling them to extrapolate the consequences of actions and make judicious decisions. These reasoning abilities are particularly useful for tasks requiring a deep understanding of cause-and-effect relations (§ 3.2.2). (2) Better User Experience. Benefitting from the powerful intent understanding capability of foundation models, tool learning could revolutionize the way we interact with machines and liberate users from the cognition load, allowing them to engage in higher-order thinking and decision-making processes. This, in turn, fosters a seamless and more natural language-based interaction paradigm that revolutionizes traditional graphical user interfaces (GUIs). The user only needs to provide high-level guidance and direction, and the model will seamlessly comprehend the user’s intent, thereby delivering more personalized and precise responses. In addition, tool learning has the potential to democratize access to complex tools. With the aid of foundation models, even novice users can easily and quickly get started with a new tool, regardless of their prior experience or technical expertise. This not only reduces the barriers to entry for new users but also unlocks a wealth of possibilities for innovation and creativity. However, it should be noted that human-model collaboration in tool use also triggers ethical concerns, which will be discussed in § 5.7. Tool Learning In this section, to unify existing efforts and promote a comprehensive understanding of tool learning, we first present a general framework, which encompasses four fundamental components, namely tool set, environment, controller, and perceiver, as detailed in § 3.1. Subsequently, we provide an elaborate discussion of the general procedure of tool learning in § 3.2. Lastly, we delve into the training methods for tool learning and discuss how to achieve generalizable tool learning in § 3.3. 3.1 Components of Tool Learning How can we enable foundation models to leverage the strengths of specialized tools to accomplish complex tasks? To better answer this question, we frame tool learning with four components as shown in Figure 3. Each component has its own characteristics and functions (§ 3.1.1), but they also interact with each other closely (§ 3.1.2). 3.1.1 Understanding the Components We first introduce each component and explain how they contribute to the tool learning process. Tool Set. Serving as the fundamental ingredient of tool learning, the tool set T = {T1, T2, · · · } contains a collection of different tools that have different functionalities. As we have elaborated in § 2.2, a tool in T can have different interfaces. In the following sections, we mainly take Application Programming Interface (API) as the example to illustrate how to interact with tools. Here we define an API as any function that can take the output of the foundation model as its input. For instance, for a weather API, the input to the API may be a location and time, and the output may contain the temperature or wind speed. Environment. The environment E is the world where the tools operate, which provides the perceiver with the execution results of tools. It provides the infrastructure necessary for tool execution, which can be either virtual or real. The former refers to a simulated environment that allows the model to interact with a digital

et al., 2020），输入的轻微修改即可翻转模型预测。这是因为这些模型严重依赖训练数据中的统计模式。相反，工具是为其预期用例专门设计的，可能对输入扰动不敏感。这使工具更能抵抗对抗攻击。总体而言，把工具纳入基础模型的工作流可提升系统鲁棒性，并降低恶意攻击风险。工具与模型的这种和谐互动，可增强系统面对不可预测现实环境的可靠性。在 § 4 与附录 A 中，我们用具体例子展示工具如何在各种任务上增强模型能力。

基础模型的好处。

基础模型可为理解、规划、推理与生成提供坚实基础，从而为工具学习带来如下好处：（1）改进决策与推理能力。基础模型在海量数据上训练，使其能获得跨广泛领域的世界知识。若引导得当，这类知识可用于在较长时程上做决策与规划（Huang et al., 2022a）。此外，基础模型已展现出显著的推理能力（Wei et al., 2022c; Wang et al., 2022a），从而能外推动作后果并做出明智决策。这些推理能力对需要深刻理解因果关系的任务尤为有用（§ 3.2.2）。（2）更好的用户体验。得益于基础模型强大的意图理解能力，工具学习可能彻底改变我们与机器交互的方式，把用户从认知负荷中解放出来，使其能从事更高阶的思维与决策。这进而促成一种无缝、更自然的基于语言的交互范式，变革传统图形用户界面（GUI）。用户只需提供高层引导与方向，模型将无缝理解用户意图，从而给出更个性化、更精确的回复。此外，工具学习有潜力使复杂工具的使用民主化。在基础模型帮助下，即便新手也能轻松快速上手新工具，无论其先前经验或技术专长如何。这不仅降低新用户的进入门槛，也为创新与创造解锁大量可能。但应注意，人机协作使用工具也会引发伦理关切，将在 § 5.7 讨论。

工具学习

为统一已有努力并促进对工具学习的全面理解，本节首先提出一般框架，包含四个基本组成：工具集、环境、控制器与感知器，详见 § 3.1。随后在 § 3.2 详细讨论工具学习的一般流程。最后在 § 3.3 深入训练方法，并讨论如何实现可泛化的工具学习。

3.1 工具学习的组成

我们如何使基础模型能够利用专用工具的长处来完成复杂任务？为更好回答这一问题，我们如图 3 所示，用四个组成来框定工具学习。每个组成有其自身特性与功能（§ 3.1.1），但也彼此紧密交互（§ 3.1.2）。

3.1.1 理解各组成

我们首先介绍每个组成，并解释它们如何贡献于工具学习过程。

工具集。

作为工具学习的基本原料，工具集 T = {T1, T2, · · · } 包含一组功能各异的工具。如 § 2.2 所述，T 中的工具可以有不同接口。在后续各节，我们主要以应用程序编程接口（API）为例说明如何与工具交互。此处把 API 定义为任何可以把基础模型输出作为其输入的函数。例如，对天气 API，输入可能是地点与时间，输出可能包含温度或风速。

环境。

环境 E 是工具运行的世界，它向感知器提供工具的执行结果。它为工具执行提供必要基础设施，可以是虚拟的或真实的。前者指允许模型与工具的数字

3.1 Components of Tool Learning Controller Foundation Model Environment Perceiver Tool Set Human Environment Feedback Summary Planning Human Instruction Human Feedback Tool Execution Figure 3: Illustration of the tool learning framework, where we display the human user and four core ingredients of the framework: tool set, controller, perceiver, and environment. The user sends an instruction to the controller, which then makes decisions and executes tools in the environment. The perceiver receives feedback from both the environment and the user and summarizes them to the controller. representation of the tool, while a real environment involves actual interaction with the physical tool. Virtual environments have the advantage of being easily accessible and replicable, allowing for more cost-effective training for models. However, virtual environments may not fully replicate the complexities of the real-world environment, leading to overfitting and poor generalization (Hansen et al., 2021). On the other hand, real environments provide a more realistic context but may be more challenging to access and involve greater costs. Controller. The controller C serves as the “brain” for tool learning framework and is typically modeled using a foundation model. The purpose of the controller C is to provide a feasible and precise plan for using tools to fulfill the user’s request. To this end, C should understand user intent as well as the relationship between the intent and available tools, and then develop a plan to select the appropriate tools for tackling tasks, which will be discussed in § 3.2.1. In cases where the query is complex and targets a high-level task, C may need to decompose the task into multiple sub-tasks, which requires foundational models to have powerful planning and reasoning capabilities (§ 3.2.2). Perceiver. The perceiver P is responsible for processing the user’s and the environment’s feedback and generating a summary for the controller. Simple forms of feedback processing include concatenating the user and environment feedback or formatting the feedback using a pre-defined template. The summarized feedback is then passed to the controller to assist its decision-making. By observing this feedback, the controller can determine whether the generated plan is effective and whether there are anomalies during the execution that need to be addressed. Under more complex scenarios, the perceiver should be able to support multiple modalities, such as text, vision, and audio, to capture the diverse nature of feedback from the user and the environment. 3.1.2 Connecting the Components Formally, assume we have a tool set T , which the controller can utilize to accomplish certain tasks. At time step t, environment E provides feedback et on the tool execution. The perceiver P receives the user feedback ft and the environment feedback et, and generates summarized feedback xt. Typically, the perceiver can be achieved by pre-defined rules (e.g., concatenating ft and et) to form xt, or modeled with complex neural models. The controller C generates a plan at, which selects and executes an appropriate tool from T . This process can be formulated as the following probability distribution: pC(at) = pθC(at | xt, Ht, q), (1) where θC denotes the parameters of C, q denotes the user query or instruction, and Ht = {(xs, as)}t−1 s=0 denotes the history feedback and plans. In its simplest form, a generated plan at can simply be a specific action for tool

图 3：工具学习框架示意，展示人类用户与框架的四个核心要素：工具集、控制器、感知器与环境。用户向控制器发送指令，控制器做决策并在环境中执行工具。感知器接收来自环境与用户的反馈，并摘要给控制器。

表征交互的模拟环境，而真实环境则涉及与物理工具的实际交互。虚拟环境的优势是易于访问与复制，从而使模型训练更经济。然而，虚拟环境可能无法完全复现现实环境的复杂性，导致过拟合与泛化差（Hansen et al., 2021）。另一方面，真实环境提供更现实的情境，但可能更难访问、成本更高。

控制器。

控制器 C 充当工具学习框架的「大脑」，通常用基础模型来建模。C 的目的是提供一份可行且精确的计划，通过使用工具来满足用户请求。为此，C 应理解用户意图以及意图与可用工具之间的关系，然后制定计划以选择合适工具来攻克任务，这将在 § 3.2.1 讨论。当查询复杂、瞄准高层任务时，C 可能需要把任务分解为多个子任务，这要求基础模型具备强大的规划与推理能力（§ 3.2.2）。

感知器。

感知器 P 负责处理用户与环境的反馈，并为控制器生成摘要。简单的反馈处理包括拼接用户与环境反馈，或用预定义模板格式化反馈。摘要后的反馈再传给控制器以辅助决策。通过观察该反馈，控制器可以判断所生成的计划是否有效，以及执行中是否有需要处理的异常。在更复杂的场景下，感知器应能支持文本、视觉与音频等多种模态，以捕捉来自用户与环境的多样化反馈。

3.1.2 连接各组成

形式化地，假设我们有工具集 T，控制器可用来完成某些任务。在时间步 t，环境 E 提供关于工具执行的反馈 et。感知器 P 接收用户反馈 ft 与环境反馈 et，并生成摘要反馈 xt。通常，感知器可由预定义规则实现（例如拼接 ft 与 et）以形成 xt，或用复杂神经模型建模。控制器 C 生成计划 at，从 T 中选择并执行合适工具。该过程可表述为如下概率分布：

pC(at) = pθC(at | xt, Ht, q)，

（1）

其中 θC 表示 C 的参数，q 表示用户查询或指令，Ht = {(xs, as)}t−1

s=0 表示历史反馈与计划。在最简单的形式中，生成的计划 at 可以只是针对工具的一个具体动作。

3.2 The General Procedure: From Intent to Plan execution. C can also synergize its reasoning process with the action prediction, where at may additionally contain the reasoning traces that explain which sub-task should be solved next and which tool to choose for solving the sub-task. It is worth noting that if the dependence on xs is removed from Equation (1), the resulting probability distribution becomes equivalent to autoregressive language modeling. From this perspective, the controller additionally grounds the foundation model to the environment and the tool set. Moreover, we can factorize Equation (1) as follows: pθC(at | xt, Ht, q) = X Ti∈T pθC(at | Ti, xt, Ht, q) × pθC(Ti | xt, Ht, q), (2) The decomposition reveals that the construction of the plan at involves two subtasks: selecting the appropriate tool based on the user intent and deciding the actions to execute using the selected tool. For instance, given an instruction such as “I want to book a flight to Beijing next week”, the controller C first infers that the user’s goal is to reserve a flight, with Beijing as the destination and the next week as the travel time. The model then selects the airline reservation system as the tool. Finally, it inputs the time and destination as the preliminary plan. In the process of making a reservation, we may face unexpected situations such as the unavailability of flights to Beijing in the next week. To cope with these anomalies, we can further equip C with the ability to reason about the current context and generate alternative plans, as we will discuss in detail in § 3.2.2. After a plan at is generated, it will be executed in E, and the resulting feedback et+1 from E will be passed on to the perceiver. The above process repeats for multiple rounds until the controller accomplishes the task. The overall objective is to find an action sequence {at} that ultimately fulfills the task specified by the user instruction q. Note after tool execution, the controller may additionally integrate the execution results into a plausible response for the user (see details in § 5.6). 3.2 The General Procedure: From Intent to Plan As formulated in § 3.1.2, the general procedure of tool learning necessitates intricate interplay among different components. In this section, we will further elaborate on the key issues involved in this procedure. 3.2.1 Understanding Intent and Tools To accurately fulfill the task specified by the user query q, the controller needs to understand two aspects: (1) the underlying intent of the user, which involves recognizing and formalizing the natural language q as a high-level task (i.e., intent understanding); (2) the tool set T , which entails comprehending the functionality and objective of each tool within it (i.e., tool understanding). By understanding both aspects, the controller can bridge the gap between the user’s intent and the tool set, which is the pre-requisite for connecting controller C, the user, and tool set T in Figure 3. Intent Understanding. Understanding user intent is a long-standing research topic in NLP (Jansen et al., 2007; Sukthankar et al., 2014), which involves comprehending the underlying purpose of a user query. Intent understanding is essential in scenarios requiring human-computer interaction, such as developing advanced conversational agents capable of conducting intricate and nuanced dialogues with users. It requires learning a mapping from the instruction space to the model’s cognition space. By accurately identifying the user intent, the controller can provide more personalized responses with a better user experience. Recent explorations in instruction tuning (Wei et al., 2022a) demonstrate that foundation models can possess extraordinary proficiency in comprehending user instructions. Prior work has shown that fine-tuning large language models on a collection of datasets templated with human instructions allows models to generalize even to instructions for unseen tasks (Wei et al., 2022a; Mishra et al., 2022; Sanh et al., 2022; Bach et al., 2022; Ouyang et al., 2022). Promisingly, such generalization ability can further be enhanced by scaling up both the model size and the quantity or diversity of training instructions (Iyer et al., 2022). Despite the impressive intent understanding capabilities, challenges still exist in real-world tool learning scenarios: (1) Understanding Vague Instructions. The first challenge is dealing with the inherent vagueness and ambiguity in the user query. Many user queries are inherently imprecise and can even be polysemous, requiring the controller to rely on contextual cues and background knowledge to infer the user’s intended meaning. One possible solution is to actively interact with users to clarify any ambiguity, such as asking for clarifications about a previous user query. (2) Generalization to Diverse Instructions. Another challenge is having the models generalize to more diverse user instructions. As the intent space is theoretically infinite, it is almost impractical for foundation models to be exposed to every real-world intention during training. In addition, the challenge of personalization arises from the fact that each individual has their own unique way of expressing intentions,

pθC(at | xt, Ht, q) = Σ_{Ti∈T} pθC(at | Ti, xt, Ht, q) × pθC(Ti | xt, Ht, q)，

（2）

该分解表明，构造计划 at 涉及两个子任务：根据用户意图选择合适工具，以及决定用所选工具执行哪些动作。例如，给定「我想下周订一张去北京的机票」这样的指令，控制器 C 首先推断用户目标是预订航班，目的地为北京、出行时间为下周。模型随后选择航空预订系统作为工具。最后，它把时间与目的地作为初步计划输入。在预订过程中，我们可能遇到意外情况，如下周没有去北京的航班。为应对这些异常，我们可以进一步赋予 C 对当前情境推理并生成替代计划的能力，详见 § 3.2.2。

计划 at 生成后将在 E 中执行，E 产生的反馈 et+1 再传给感知器。上述过程多轮重复，直到控制器完成任务。总体目标是找到最终能完成用户指令 q 所指定任务的动作序列 {at}。注意，工具执行后，控制器还可把执行结果整合为对用户合理的回复（详见 § 5.6）。

3.2 一般流程：从意图到计划

如 § 3.1.2 所述，工具学习的一般流程需要各组成之间精细交互。本节将进一步阐述该流程中的关键问题。

3.2.1 理解意图与工具

为准确完成用户查询 q 所指定的任务，控制器需要理解两方面：（1）用户的潜在意图，即把自然语言 q 识别并形式化为高层任务（即意图理解）；（2）工具集 T，即理解其中每个工具的功能与目标（即工具理解）。理解这两方面后，控制器才能弥合用户意图与工具集之间的差距，而这是连接图 3 中控制器 C、用户与工具集 T 的前提。

意图理解。

理解用户意图是 NLP 中长期存在的研究课题（Jansen et al., 2007; Sukthankar et al., 2014），涉及理解用户查询的潜在目的。意图理解在需要人机交互的场景中至关重要，例如开发能与用户进行复杂、细腻对话的高级对话智能体。它要求学习从指令空间到模型认知空间的映射。通过准确识别用户意图，控制器可以提供更个性化的回复与更好的用户体验。

指令微调方面的近期探索（Wei et al., 2022a）表明，基础模型可以具备理解用户指令的非凡熟练度。先前工作表明，在一组用人类指令模板化的数据集上微调大语言模型，可使模型甚至泛化到未见任务的指令（Wei et al., 2022a; Mishra et al., 2022; Sanh et al., 2022; Bach et al., 2022; Ouyang et al., 2022）。有希望的是，这种泛化能力还可通过同时扩大模型规模与训练指令的数量或多样性来进一步增强（Iyer et al., 2022）。尽管意图理解能力令人印象深刻，现实工具学习场景中仍存在挑战：（1）理解含糊指令。第一个挑战是处理用户查询中固有的含糊与歧义。许多用户查询本质上不精确，甚至可能多义，要求控制器依赖情境线索与背景知识来推断用户的意图含义。一种可能方案是主动与用户交互以澄清歧义，例如就先前查询请求澄清。（2）泛化到多样指令。另一挑战是使模型泛化到更多样的用户指令。由于意图空间理论上无限，基础模型几乎不可能在训练中接触每一种现实意图。此外，个性化挑战源于每个人都有其独特的意图表达方式，

本页把公式拆成「先选工具、再填参数」，并用订机票说明异常时要改计划。下半页讲意图理解：指令微调有效，但含糊话与无限意图空间仍难。 从意图到计划是工具学习的主流程。 类比：听懂「下周去北京」还不够，还得会换航班、会问清楚是哪一周。

Figure 4: Examples of zero-shot and few-shot prompting for tool understanding. The prompts are constructed by describing the functionalities (zero-shot prompting) or giving usage examples (few-shot prompting) of a weather API. which requires the model to adapt to the diverse expressions of intent of different individuals. One solution is to incorporate more diverse training data that covers a wide range of real-world scenarios, thereby enabling the models to learn the nuances of different instructions. Another solution is to leverage user feedback and actively adapt the model to individual users, i.e., personalized tool learning (§ 5.4). Tool Understanding. As noted by Hernik & Csibra (2009), when learning to utilize a specific tool, human beings perceive it as an object with particular functions, engaging in a cognitive process to understand its purpose and operation. By observing goal-directed demonstrations and following actions performed by other people, they gradually acquire the necessary knowledge and skills to use the tools effectively. Similarly, this understanding process is crucial for successfully solving tasks with tools. Analogously, a comprehensive understanding of the tools’ functionalities is indispensable for enabling the controller to use tools proficiently. The process of tool understanding encompasses grasping what the tool is used for and how to use the tool. Take the case of a calculator: the controller needs to know that a calculator is intended for arithmetic operations, its input should be numbers and mathematical operators, and its output should be a computed value. In real-world scenarios, tools are typically accompanied by a manual (or tutorial), which provides sufficient relevant details about their functionalities and usage. Endowed with strong few-shot learning (Brown et al., 2020) and zero-shot learning (Wei et al., 2022a) capabilities, foundation models can be prompted to unravel tools’ functionalities and comprehend how to use them. To this end, we can construct suitable task-specific prompts either through manual design (Vemprala et al., 2023) or retrievial (Zhou et al., 2023). These prompts should describe the API functionalities or exemplify with demonstrations of their usage. We categorize two prompting approaches as shown in Figure 4: (1) zero-shot prompting, which describes API functionalities, their input/output formats, possible parameters, etc. This approach allows the model to understand the tasks that each API can tackle; (2) few-shot prompting, which provides concrete tool-use demonstrations to the model. By mimicking human behaviors from these demonstrations, the model can learn how to utilize these tools. We provide experimental results of both prompting methods in § 4. Prompting has been widely adopted as a lightweight approach to teach foundation models about tools (Yao et al., 2022b; Driess et al., 2023; OpenAI, 2023) with minimum human effort. Prompts can be easily adjusted to accommodate changes of tools. For instance, when tools are modified or upgraded, we can flexibly rewrite the prompts to adapt the model behaviors. Despite these advantages, prompting methods still face several challenges. First, since the effectiveness of prompting depends a lot on the model, smaller or less capable models cannot understand prompts well. Second, prompting is restricted by input context length. Although foundation models have been shown to learn to use simple tools through prompts, the situation may be more

工具理解。

如 Hernik & Csibra (2009) 所指出，当学习使用特定工具时，人类把它感知为具有特定功能的对象，并进入理解其目的与操作的认知过程。通过观察目标导向的示范并跟随他人执行的动作，他们逐渐获得有效使用工具所需的知识与技能。同样，这一理解过程对用工具成功解题至关重要。类比地，对工具功能的全面理解，是使控制器熟练使用工具不可或缺的。

工具理解过程包括把握工具用来做什么以及如何使用工具。以计算器为例：控制器需要知道计算器用于算术运算，其输入应为数字与数学运算符，输出应为计算值。

在现实场景中，工具通常附有手册（或教程），提供关于其功能与用法的充分相关细节。凭借强大的少样本学习（Brown et al., 2020）与零样本学习（Wei et al., 2022a）能力，基础模型可被提示以揭示工具功能并理解如何使用它们。为此，我们可通过人工设计（Vemprala et al., 2023）或检索（Zhou et al., 2023）构造合适的任务特定提示。这些提示应描述 API 功能，或用其用法示范来举例。

我们如图 4 所示将提示方法分为两类：（1）零样本提示，描述 API 功能、其输入/输出格式、可能参数等。该方法使模型理解每个 API 能处理哪些任务；（2）少样本提示，向模型提供具体的工具使用示范。通过模仿这些示范中的人类行为，模型可以学会如何利用这些工具。两种提示方法的实验结果见 § 4。

提示已被广泛采用为一种轻量方法，以最小人工代价教基础模型认识工具（Yao et al., 2022b; Driess et al., 2023; OpenAI, 2023）。提示可以容易地调整以适应工具变更。例如，当工具被修改或升级时，我们可以灵活改写提示以适配模型行为。尽管有这些优点，提示方法仍面临若干挑战。首先，提示的有效性很大程度上取决于模型，较小或能力较弱的模型不能很好理解提示。其次，提示受输入上下文长度限制。尽管已表明基础模型可通过提示学会使用简单工具，当情况更

3.2 The General Procedure: From Intent to Plan challenging with multiple complex tools with long descriptions. Especially when the tool set greatly expands, providing all possible tools within a prompt becomes infeasible given the limited context length. A potential solution is to add an intermediate stage of tool selection, which first retrieves a small set of tools that are most suitable for the task at hand. Another solution is fine-tuning, which optimizes models with concrete tool-use examples to understand tools. This process involves leveraging the rich knowledge obtained from human tool-use experiences. When deployed in practice, a fine-tuned model alleviates the need for incorporating tool definitions in the input, which shrinks the input length and accelerates model inference. However, a major limitation of fine-tuning is that it often requires extensive human annotations or demonstrations. Additionally, tools are frequently updated, and fine-tuned models will need to be retrained with updated examples, incurring additional costs. More discussion about the tuning-based solution is left in § 3.3. 3.2.2 Planning with Reasoning As discussed in § 3.2.1, understanding the intent and tools lays a solid foundation for planning. Nevertheless, it is still insufficient for tackling intricate tasks. The user query q often implies a complex task that should be divided into multiple sub-tasks with proper sequencing, thereby necessitating a process of reasoning. Recent research has revealed that reasoning capabilities can emerge when foundation models are scaled up to a certain size (Wei et al., 2022b). In particular, foundation models with tens or hundreds of billions of parameters can generate intermediate reasoning traces during complex problem-solving, thereby significantly enhancing their zero-shot and few-shot performances (Nakano et al., 2021; Nye et al., 2021; Wei et al., 2022b). The reasoning ability observed in the foundation models appears to facilitate a transition from System 1 to System 2 (Kahneman, 2011), enabling the accomplishment of more complex tasks. Eliciting Reasoning in Foundation Models. Despite the extensive study of the concept of reasoning in the psychology literature (Wason, 1968; Kelley, 2013), the notion of reasoning as applied to foundation models is not clearly defined. However, in general terms, the reasoning ability in the literature of foundation models can be framed as the capacity to decompose a complex problem into sub-problems and solve the sub-problems step-by-step (Wei et al., 2022c; Press et al., 2022; Khot et al., 2022). Here we keep consistent with these works and discuss reasoning in the sense of problem decomposition and sub-problem solving. The vanilla few-shot prompt learning (Brown et al., 2020), whereby models are provided with a prompt consisting of several examples for the given task, has been shown to fail when it comes to problems that require complex reasoning (Creswell et al., 2022). To address this issue, Wei et al. (2022c) propose Chain-of-Thought (CoT) prompting. Unlike vanilla few-shot prompt learning, CoT additionally inserts the reasoning trace required to derive the final answer for each example in the prompt. In this way, CoT prompts models to generate their “thoughts” on the necessary intermediate steps before arriving at the final answer. CoT has been proven to significantly boost performance on a wide range of tasks, including arithmetic reasoning, commonsense reasoning, and symbolic reasoning (Wei et al., 2022c). In light of the remarkable reasoning abilities of foundation models, recent research has made successful attempts to employ them in the controller in tool learning. It is demonstrated that their reasoning capabilities enable the controller to effectively decompose a complex problem into several sub-problems, and determine which tool to call upon for each sub-problem. We categorize relevant research into two streams: introspective reasoning and extrospective reasoning. The former involves generating a static plan of tool use without interacting with the environment E, while the latter generates plans incrementally by iteratively interacting with E and utilizing feedback obtained from previous executions. As shown in Figure 5, the environment E is invisible to the controller C in introspective reasoning but is visible in extrospective reasoning, creating a closed-loop interaction among the four components. Introspective Reasoning. This kind of reasoning directly generates multi-step plans for tool use without knowing intermediate execution results. For instance, Huang et al. (2022a) investigate the planning ability of foundation models and show that they are capable of decomposing high-level tasks into semantically plausible sub-plans. Another representative work is Program-Aided Language Models (PAL) (Gao et al., 2022), which prompts models to generate Python codes for intermediate reasoning steps. PAL uses the Python program interpreter as the tool, enabling the model to act as a programmer writing detailed comments, and achieving significant improvements in arithmetic, symbolic, and algorithmic reasoning. Notably, the idea of model-as-programmer has also been shown to be successful in embodied agents, as evidenced by ProgPrompt (Singh et al., 2022) and Code-as-Policies (Liang et al., 2022a), which prompt models to generate

3.2 一般流程：从意图到计划

复杂、描述很长的多工具时会更困难。尤其当工具集大幅扩张时，在有限上下文长度内提供所有可能工具变得不可行。

一个潜在方案是增加工具选择的中间阶段，先检索出最适合手头任务的一小套工具。另一方案是微调：用具体的工具使用例子优化模型以理解工具。该过程利用从人类工具使用经验中获得的丰富知识。实际部署时，微调后的模型无需在输入中纳入工具定义，从而缩短输入长度并加速推理。然而，微调的主要局限是往往需要大量人工标注或示范。此外，工具频繁更新，微调模型需要用更新后的例子重新训练，产生额外成本。基于调参方案的更多讨论留在 § 3.3。

3.2.2 带推理的规划

如 § 3.2.1 所述，理解意图与工具为规划奠定了坚实基础。然而，这对攻克复杂任务仍不够。用户查询 q 往往暗示一个应被拆成多个子任务并恰当排序的复杂任务，因而需要推理过程。

近期研究表明，当基础模型放大到一定规模时，推理能力可以涌现（Wei et al., 2022b）。尤其是，拥有数百亿或上千亿参数的基础模型能在复杂解题过程中生成中间推理轨迹，从而显著增强其零样本与少样本表现（Nakano et al., 2021; Nye et al., 2021; Wei et al., 2022b）。在基础模型中观察到的推理能力似乎促进了从系统 1 到系统 2 的过渡（Kahneman, 2011），使完成更复杂任务成为可能。

激发基础模型中的推理。

尽管心理学文献对推理概念有广泛研究（Wason, 1968; Kelley, 2013），应用于基础模型的推理概念并未被清晰定义。但一般而言，基础模型文献中的推理能力可被框定为：把复杂问题分解为子问题并逐步求解子问题的能力（Wei et al., 2022c; Press et al., 2022; Khot et al., 2022）。此处我们与这些工作保持一致，在问题分解与子问题求解的意义上讨论推理。

普通的少样本提示学习（Brown et al., 2020）——即向模型提供由该任务若干例子组成的提示——已被表明在需要复杂推理的问题上失败（Creswell et al., 2022）。为解决这一问题，Wei et al. (2022c) 提出思维链（Chain-of-Thought, CoT）提示。与普通少样本提示学习不同，CoT 额外在提示的每个例子中插入推导最终答案所需的推理轨迹。这样，CoT 提示模型在到达最终答案之前生成关于必要中间步骤的「想法」。CoT 已被证明能显著提升算术推理、常识推理与符号推理等广泛任务上的表现（Wei et al., 2022c）。

鉴于基础模型显著的推理能力，近期研究已成功尝试把它们用作工具学习中的控制器。研究表明，其推理能力使控制器能有效把复杂问题分解为若干子问题，并为每个子问题决定调用哪个工具。我们将相关研究分为两流：内省推理与外观推理。前者在不与环境 E 交互的情况下生成静态的工具使用计划，后者通过与 E 迭代交互并利用先前执行的反馈增量生成计划。如图 5 所示，在内省推理中环境 E 对控制器 C 不可见，而在外观推理中可见，从而在四个组成之间形成闭环交互。

内省推理。

这类推理在不知道中间执行结果的情况下直接生成多步工具使用计划。例如，Huang et al. (2022a) 考察基础模型的规划能力，表明它们能把高层任务分解为语义上合理的子计划。另一代表性工作是程序辅助语言模型（PAL）（Gao et al., 2022），它提示模型为中间推理步骤生成 Python 代码。PAL 把 Python 程序解释器当作工具，使模型充当写详细注释的程序员，并在算术、符号与算法推理上取得显著提升。值得注意的是，「模型即程序员」的想法在具身智能体中也被证明成功，如 ProgPrompt（Singh et al., 2022）与 Code-as-Policies（Liang et al., 2022a）提示模型生成

3.2 The General Procedure: From Intent to Plan Introspective Reasoning Controller Foundation Model Tool Set Env & Human Controller Foundation Model Tool Set Extrospective Reasoning Env & Human Multi-Iteration Figure 5: Illustration of introspective reasoning and extrospective reasoning. Extrospective reasoning requires feedback from the environment and humans to carry out iterative plan generation. We omit the perceiver in the illustration for simplicity. executable programs for embodied agents. These studies reveal that, despite not having direct interaction with the environment, models are capable of generating executable programs for agents and anticipating possible anomalies in the plan execution. Another example is Visual ChatGPT (Wu et al., 2023), which interleaves various vision foundation models with ChatGPT to enable understanding and generating images. In their system, ChatGPT serves as the core controller and makes sequential decisions. At each step, ChatGPT might call a vision model to modify an existing image or respond to the user with plain text. However, since these models are not grounded in the environment, they may generate unrealistic and nonsensical plans. To this end, SayCan (Ahn et al., 2022) emphasizes those actions that the agent is “permitted” to execute instead of those it is “willing” to perform. In practice, they employ a value function to estimate the probability of each action being successfully executed. With this function, the agent becomes more physically grounded in the environment. Overall, despite the absence of environment feedback, foundation models exhibit a remarkable ability to plan effectively in introspective reasoning. They can anticipate potential anomalies in plan execution and adjust their plans accordingly. This ability not only enables the controller to generate executable programs but also enhances its capacity to plan for a wide range of tasks. Extrospective Reasoning. Despite its simplicity, introspective reasoning cannot adapt the plan in response to intermediate execution results. A more rational approach to planning is taking the environment E into account, and generating plans incrementally (e.g., one step at a time) with subsequent plans dependent on previous execution results. This allows the four components described in § 3.1 to be well integrated and to cooperate effectively to achieve complex tasks. We refer to such an incremental reasoning strategy as extrospective reasoning. Compared to introspective reasoning, extrospective reasoning additionally considers feedback from the user and environment (Figure 5), and is thus better suited to complex tasks (Madaan et al., 2023; Wang et al., 2023b), such as multi-step QA and embodied learning, where decision-making at each step is dependent on the preceding context. Recent works such as Self-Ask (Press et al., 2022), ReAct (Yao et al., 2022b), and ToolFormer (Schick et al., 2023) have demonstrated that by providing access to search engine APIs, models are able to achieve improved accuracy on multi-step QA. Through CoT prompting (Self-Ask and ReAct) or fine-tuning (ToolFormer), models can learn to decompose complex questions and utilize the search API to find the answer to the first sub-question. Based on the response and the question, they can then iteratively determine the subsequent question to ask or give the final answer. For embodied learning, while introspective reasoning methods have demonstrated the ability to generate executable programs and address potential execution anomalies, direct interaction with the environment can further enhance models’ planning capabilities. For instance, Inner Monologue (Huang et al., 2022b) leverages multiple sources of feedback from the environment, such as whether a task is completed successfully and the current scene information. In this way, models can generate more feasible plans and improve their ability of high-level instruction completion. LLM-Planner (Song et al., 2022) explicitly considers anomalies that may arise during plan execution and utilizes environmental feedback to regenerate the plan in case of execution failure, enabling models to handle exceptions appropriately. Additionally, ReAct (Yao et al., 2022b) grants models the autonomy to determine when to cease generating action tokens during planning, enabling them to reason about the current situation and develop more refined subsequent plans.
