图 5：内省推理与外观推理示意。外观推理需要来自环境与人的反馈以进行迭代计划生成。为简洁起见，图中省略感知器。

供具身智能体执行的可执行程序。这些研究表明，尽管没有与环境的直接交互，模型仍能为智能体生成可执行程序，并预估计划执行中可能的异常。另一例子是 Visual ChatGPT（Wu et al., 2023），它把各种视觉基础模型与 ChatGPT 交错，以理解并生成图像。在其系统中，ChatGPT 充当核心控制器并做序列决策。每一步，ChatGPT 可能调用视觉模型修改已有图像，或用纯文本回应用户。

然而，由于这些模型未 grounding 到环境中，它们可能生成不现实、无意义的计划。为此，SayCan（Ahn et al., 2022）强调智能体「被允许」执行的动作，而非它「愿意」执行的动作。实践中，他们用价值函数估计每个动作被成功执行的概率。有了该函数，智能体在环境中变得更有物理 grounding。总体而言，尽管缺少环境反馈，基础模型在内省推理中仍展现出有效规划的显著能力。它们能预估计划执行中的潜在异常并相应调整计划。这一能力不仅使控制器能生成可执行程序，也增强其为一类广泛任务做规划的能力。

外观推理。

尽管简单，内省推理无法根据中间执行结果调整计划。更合理的规划方式是把环境 E 考虑进来，并增量生成计划（例如一次一步），后续计划依赖于先前执行结果。这使 § 3.1 所述的四个组成能很好整合并有效合作以完成复杂任务。我们将这种增量推理策略称为外观推理。与内省推理相比，外观推理额外考虑来自用户与环境的反馈（图 5），因而更适合复杂任务（Madaan et al., 2023; Wang et al., 2023b），如多步问答与具身学习，其中每一步决策依赖于先前情境。

近期工作如 Self-Ask（Press et al., 2022）、ReAct（Yao et al., 2022b）与 Toolformer（Schick et al., 2023）表明，通过提供搜索引擎 API 的访问，模型能在多步问答上取得更高准确率。通过 CoT 提示（Self-Ask 与 ReAct）或微调（Toolformer），模型可以学会分解复杂问题并用搜索 API 找到第一个子问题的答案。基于回复与问题，它们再迭代决定下一个要问的问题或给出最终答案。

对具身学习而言，尽管内省推理方法已表明能生成可执行程序并处理潜在执行异常，与环境的直接交互可进一步增强模型的规划能力。例如，Inner Monologue（Huang et al., 2022b）利用来自环境的多种反馈，如任务是否成功完成以及当前场景信息。这样，模型可以生成更可行的计划，并提升完成高层指令的能力。LLM-Planner（Song et al., 2022）显式考虑计划执行中可能出现的异常，并在执行失败时利用环境反馈重新生成计划，使模型能恰当处理例外。此外，ReAct（Yao et al., 2022b）赋予模型在规划期间决定何时停止生成动作 token 的自主权，使其能对当前情境推理并制定更精细的后续计划。

白话解读

本页用图 5 对照两种推理：内省一次出完整计划；外观每步看环境再改。ReAct、Toolformer、Inner Monologue 都走外观这一路。 对 harness 来说，外观推理更像真实运行时：有观察、有失败、有重试。 类比：闭门做菜谱 vs 打开锅盖尝一口再决定下一步。

3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning In summary, extrospective reasoning requires interaction between the controller and the environment, which is a more complex setting. However, the real-time feedback from the user and environment empowers models to have a more comprehensive understanding of the current situation, making it possible to eventually achieve long-term goals that necessitate extensive planning. Challenges in Multi-Step Multi-Tool Scenario. Humans do not stick to only one single tool to complete complex tasks. Instead, we carefully decompose the task into several sub-tasks, select the most suitable tool for each sub-task, and gradually accomplish them step by step. As discussed above, current research has shown satisfactory performance in task decomposition. However, there is a lack of exploration in utilizing different tools for different sub-tasks. Most of the research mentioned in this section is limited to either multi-step single-tool or single-step multi-tool scenarios. However, there has been a recent emergence of research that addresses the multi-step multi-tool scenario. One such example is the ReAct model (Yao et al., 2022b), which integrates multiple APIs of Wikipedia and employs the foundation model to decide when to use which API. Later, MM-ReAct (Yang et al., 2023) generalizes ReAct to the multi-modal domain by including several vision experts. Furthermore, some recent projects such as Auto-GPT4 demonstrate the huge potential of GPT-4 in manipulating multiple tools and making long-term plans, pushing the boundaries of what is possible with tool learning. Given a user query, Auto-GPT will take step-by-step actions to accomplish the objective autonomously. In addition to reasoning about the current state, Auto-GPT can also reflect on past actions to refine decision-making. Although these works constitute a significant step in advancing tool learning in the multi-step multi-tool scenario, there are still several challenges and future directions that need to be investigated.

  • Understanding the Interplay among Different Tools. The multi-step multi-tool scenario typically involves complex tasks that require a higher level of intent understanding and reasoning capability. To effectively utilize multiple tools under this scenario, models need to grasp not only the individual functionalities of tools but also their interactions and dependencies. Models should be able to sequence the tools in a logical order so that the subsequent tools can leverage the information generated by the previous tools and effectively complete the task.
  • From Sequential Execution to Parallel Execution. Tool executions do not have to be performed sequentially. In certain scenarios, parallel execution is possible for sub-tasks that do not depend on each other, which can potentially improve execution efficiency. For instance, given a user instruction “Generate two codes, one for drawing a rectangle, and one for drawing a circle.”, the two tasks can be assigned to two agents, enabling the codes to be generated simultaneously. Determining the dependencies among different sub-tasks and effectively switching between parallel and sequential execution to optimize the overall process is a promising direction that merits further investigation.
  • From Single-agent Problem-Solving to Multi-agent Collaboration. Previous works typically assume that a single agent (controller) is solely responsible for the entire tool learning procedure. However, in practice, complex tasks often demand collaboration among multiple agents, each possessing unique abilities and expertise. Embracing multi-agent collaboration can unlock more effective and efficient problem-solving approaches, necessitating the design of methods for communication, coordination, and negotiation among agents to ensure seamless collaboration and optimal task execution. Notably, recent work like Park et al. (2023) demonstrates that multiple agents modeled with foundation models can simulate human behaviors (e.g., interpersonal communication) in interactive scenarios. This provides promising evidence for the adoption of multiple agents for tool learning. We look forward to more work in the future moving towards more practical multi-step multi-tool scenarios and making efforts to address these challenges. As a prior exploration, we evaluate foundation models’ performance when multiple tools (APIs) are required to solve a task in § 4. 3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning Guidance, either from humans or environments, plays a critical role in training foundation models to use tools. In contrast to the prompting-based methods mentioned in § 3.2.1 and § 3.2.2, which rely on the frozen foundation models’ in-context learning abilities, the training-based method optimizes the model with supervision. As noted by Fagard et al. (2016), there are two primary ways for infants to learn a new tool, that is either from demonstration by an adult modeling the action or relying on their own exploration. Analogously, as shown in Figure 6, we categorize training strategies for tool learning into two streams: (1) learning from 4https://github.com/Torantulino/Auto-GPT

3.3 训练模型以改进工具学习

总之，外观推理需要控制器与环境交互，是更复杂的设定。然而，来自用户与环境的实时反馈使模型能更全面地理解当前情境，从而有可能最终达成需要大量规划的长期目标。

多步多工具场景中的挑战。

人类不会只粘着单一工具去完成复杂任务。相反，我们仔细把任务分解为若干子任务，为每个子任务选择最合适的工具，并逐步完成它们。如上所述，当前研究在任务分解上已有令人满意的表现。然而，对不同子任务使用不同工具的探索仍然不足。本节提到的大多数研究限于多步单工具或单步多工具场景。不过，近期已出现针对多步多工具场景的研究。一个例子是 ReAct 模型（Yao et al., 2022b），它整合维基百科的多个 API，并让基础模型决定何时使用哪个 API。随后，MM-ReAct（Yang et al., 2023）通过纳入若干视觉专家把 ReAct 推广到多模态领域。此外，Auto-GPT4 等近期项目展示了 GPT-4 在操纵多种工具并做长期规划上的巨大潜力，推动了工具学习可能性的边界。给定用户查询，Auto-GPT 将逐步采取动作以自主完成目标。除对当前状态推理外，Auto-GPT 还能反思过去的动作以精炼决策。尽管这些工作在推进多步多工具场景中的工具学习上构成重要一步，仍有若干挑战与未来方向需要研究。

  • 理解不同工具之间的相互作用。多步多工具场景通常涉及需要更高层次意图理解与推理能力的复杂任务。要在该场景下有效利用多种工具，模型不仅需要掌握各工具的个别功能，还需要掌握它们的交互与依赖。模型应以逻辑顺序排列工具，使后续工具能利用先前工具产生的信息并有效完成任务。
  • 从顺序执行到并行执行。工具执行不必按顺序进行。在某些场景中，互不依赖的子任务可以并行执行，从而可能提高执行效率。例如，给定用户指令「生成两段代码，一段画矩形，一段画圆」，两个任务可以分配给两个智能体，使代码同时生成。确定不同子任务之间的依赖，并在并行与顺序执行之间有效切换以优化整体过程，是值得进一步研究的有前景方向。
  • 从单智能体解题到多智能体协作。先前工作通常假设单个智能体（控制器）单独负责整个工具学习流程。然而在实践中，复杂任务往往要求多个各具独特能力与专长的智能体协作。拥抱多智能体协作可以解锁更有效、更高效的解题方法，需要设计智能体间的通信、协调与协商方法，以确保无缝协作与最优任务执行。值得注意的是，Park et al. (2023) 等近期工作表明，用基础模型建模的多个智能体可以在交互场景中模拟人类行为（如人际交流）。这为在工具学习中采用多个智能体提供了有希望的证据。

我们期待未来有更多工作走向更实际的多步多工具场景，并努力应对这些挑战。作为先验探索，我们在 § 4 评估了需要多种工具（API）解题时基础模型的表现。

3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning Tool Set Env & Human Planning Feedback Execution Learning From Demonstrations Controller Foundation Model Tool Set Human/Model Annotation Training Demonstration Learning From Feedback Controller Foundation Model Figure 6: Training strategies for tool learning: (left) learning from human-annotated or model-annotated demonstrations; (right) learning from feedback, where the supervision could come from either the environment or humans. concrete tool-use demonstrations (Nakano et al., 2021; Sasaki & Yamashina, 2021), which often requires human annotation, and (2) learning from feedback, which typically involves reinforcement learning (Reddy et al., 2020; Baker et al., 2022). Finally, considering the existence of potentially massive tools, learning each of them one by one is infeasible in practice. Hence, we emphasize the importance of generalization in tool learning and discuss potential solutions (§ 3.3.3). 3.3.1 Learning from Demonstrations Models can be trained to mimic the behavior of human experts through imitation learning (Hussein et al., 2017; Liu et al., 2018b; Baker et al., 2022). Behavior cloning (Bain & Sammut, 1995) can be viewed as a simplistic form of imitation learning that focuses on learning policies in a supervised fashion, with the general assumption that the expert’s behavior is optimal or near-optimal. The objective of behavioral cloning is to train models to imitate human experts’ actions given certain inputs or conditions, and this approach is commonly adopted when the actions of an expert can be easily recorded and utilized for learning (Torabi et al., 2018). Formally, assume that we have a dataset D of size N consisting of pairs of user query q and the human demonstration annotation a∗, i.e., D = {(qi, a∗ i )}N−1 i=0 . Learning from human demonstrations optimizes the controller’s parameters θC with the following objective: θ∗ C = arg max θC E (qi,a∗ i )∈D Ti Y t=0 pθC(a∗ i,t | xi,t, Hi,t, qi), (3) where a∗ i,t is the human annotation at the t-th iteration for handling qi, and Ti is the total iteration number of ai, other varaibles follow the notations defined in Equation (1). Based on how a∗is obtained, learning from demonstration can be categorized into three streams, with human intervention gradually becoming less: Supervised Learning. Traditionally, behavior cloning has been widely explored in learning end-to-end or modular perceiver-controller models for autonomous vehicles and robotic applications (Ly & Akhloufi, 2020; Codevilla et al., 2019). Recently, there has been a surge of interest in fine-tuning foundation models to perform tool-oriented tasks in a supervised way. For instance, Li et al. (2022) utilize foundation models as policy networks, whose input is the tokenized environment observations, the original goals, and action history. Benefiting from the task-general inductive bias brought by foundation models, behavior cloning using the policy network significantly improves both in-domain performance and out-of-distribution generalization. Another example is WebGPT (Nakano et al., 2021), which interacts with a search engine by iteratively refining its search queries and recording important information. To achieve this, the authors first build a search interface backed up by Bing5 and then fine-tune GPT-3 (Brown et al., 2020) to clone human web search behaviors. As a language model pre-trained on general domains, the original GPT-3 is not intrinsically anchored to valid browser commands. Therefore, it is crucial to first gather demonstrations of human interactions with the browser and then learn state-to-action mappings. After fine-tuning, the model shows exceptional capabilities in manipulating search engines for information retrieval, even surpassing human experts. Similarly, WebShop (Yao et al., 2022a) provides a web-based interactive environment where an agent could browse and 5https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/bing/apis/bing-web-search-api

3.3.1 从示范中学习

模型可通过模仿学习来模仿人类专家的行为（Hussein et al., 2017; Liu et al., 2018b; Baker et al., 2022）。行为克隆（Bain & Sammut, 1995）可被视为模仿学习的一种简化形式，它以监督方式学习策略，一般假设专家行为是最优或近最优的。行为克隆的目标是训练模型在给定某些输入或条件时模仿人类专家的动作，当专家动作易于记录并用于学习时，该方法被普遍采用（Torabi et al., 2018）。

形式化地，假设我们有大小为 N 的数据集 D，由用户查询 q 与人类示范标注 a* 的对组成，即 D = {(qi, a*i)}N−1i=0。从人类示范中学习用如下目标优化控制器参数 θC：

θ*C = arg max_θC E_{(qi,a*i)∈D} ∏_{t=0}^{Ti} pθC(a*i,t | xi,t, Hi,t, qi)，

（3）

其中 a*i,t 是处理 qi 时第 t 轮的人类标注，Ti 是 ai 的总迭代次数，其他变量遵循公式 (1) 中的记号。根据 a* 如何获得，从示范中学习可分为三流，人工介入逐渐减少：

监督学习。

传统上，行为克隆已在为自动驾驶车辆与机器人应用学习端到端或模块化感知器–控制器模型中被广泛探索（Ly & Akhloufi, 2020; Codevilla et al., 2019）。近来，以监督方式微调基础模型以执行面向工具的任务引起了激增的兴趣。例如，Li et al. (2022) 把基础模型用作策略网络，其输入是符号化的环境观察、原始目标与动作历史。得益于基础模型带来的任务通用归纳偏置，使用该策略网络的行为克隆显著提升了域内表现与分布外泛化。另一例子是 WebGPT（Nakano et al., 2021），它通过迭代精炼搜索查询并记录重要信息来与搜索引擎交互。为此，作者首先构建由 Bing5 支撑的搜索界面，然后微调 GPT-3（Brown et al., 2020）以克隆人类网页搜索行为。作为在通用领域预训练的语言模型，原始 GPT-3 并不内在锚定于有效的浏览器命令。因此，首先收集人类与浏览器交互的示范、然后学习状态到动作的映射至关重要。微调后，模型在操纵搜索引擎做信息检索上展现出非凡能力，甚至超越人类专家。类似地，WebShop（Yao et al., 2022a）提供一个基于网页的交互环境，智能体可以浏览并

3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning purchase products. Through behavior cloning, the trained agent exhibits non-trivial performance in purchasing the right product given human instructions. Semi-supervised Learning. As is often the case, human behaviors cannot be easily recorded due to time and cost considerations. However, large-scale unlabeled data is often attainable, from which we could potentially construct weak, noisy supervision. Notably, recent works have shown that we could employ a less-capable model to annotate pseudo-labels on unlabeled data and convert them into weakly-supervised tool-use demonstrations. For instance, with a small amount of seed labeled data, Baker et al. (2022) train a model to predict pseudo-labels of the action taken at each timestep in a Minecraft video game. Learning from these pseudo-labels, a more powerful model can be trained without requiring the rollout of models in a target environment or large-scale gold-standard human behavior annotation. Self-supervised Learning. Despite reducing the heavy requirements on human behavior annotation, semisupervised learning still requires a seed labeled dataset to attain the pseudo labels. Besides, the biases in the seed dataset may also be amplified during training, leading to poor generalization performance. To this end, researchers recently show that with a few demonstrations, foundation models can teach themselves how to utilize a tool in a self-supervised manner (Parisi et al., 2022; Schick et al., 2023). For instance, Toolformer (Schick et al., 2023) leverages the in-context learning ability of foundation models to iteratively bootstrap tool-use examples based on a handful of human-written examples. These auto-generated examples are further filtered to reduce noise. The final tool-use dataset contains sufficient supervision, significantly improving GPT-J’s (Wang & Komatsuzaki, 2021) tool-use performance, highlighting the potential of selfsupervised learning for enhancing tool-use capabilities. 3.3.2 Learning from Feedback Collecting manually annotated tool-use examples, which probably include complete traces of human behaviors and the final answers, is time-consuming and labor-intensive. Moreover, the learned model may not adapt effectively to new environments as it conforms to the recorded human behaviors. Besides, it is impractical to explicitly annotate every possible scenario of environment condition and agent behavior (Codevilla et al., 2019). Alternatively, humans learn from trial and error to correct and rectify their tool-use behaviors (Allen et al., 2019). Similarly, feedback from both the environment and humans can enable the model to understand the consequences of its actions and adapt its behaviors. The supervision from feedback can also enhance the capabilities of an agent trained in a supervised way (Nakano et al., 2021; Baker et al., 2022). Formally, learning from feedback can be described as optimizing the controllers’ parameters θC from open explorations with query set Q = {qi}i: θ∗ C = arg max θC E qi∈Q E {ai,t} Ti t=0∈pθC h R({ai,t}Ti t=0) i , (4) where R is the reward estimated from the sequence of feedback and Ti denotes the number of iterations needed for handling qi. Reinforcement Learning (RL) for Tool Learning. RL is a common solution to enabling artificial agents to learn from their environment in complex decision-making processes (Silver et al., 2018; Berner et al., 2019; Schrittwieser et al., 2020). Tool learning can be considered an RL scenario, where the action space is defined by tools, and the agent learns to select the appropriate tool and perform the correct actions that maximize the reward signal. The policy model can be initialized by a foundation model (Schulman et al., 2017). Such initialization brings the policy model abundant prior knowledge, alleviating the need for the RL agent to learn basic skills. With a reward function that quantifies the performance of the agent in achieving the task goal, RL has been successfully used in various tool learning scenarios, such as robotic grasping (Levine et al., 2018) and multi-agent autocurricula (Baker et al., 2020). By optimizing the loss function, the agent learns to reflect on the current state of the environment, select the appropriate tool, and perform the right actions that lead to the highest expected reward. In the following, we introduce two sources of feedback: environment feedback and human feedback, which can be considered sources of reward signals in the context of tool learning. These two feedbacks are complementary and can be combined with each other. Environment Feedback. The controller interacts with the environment and receives feedback about the consequences of its actions. The model then updates its policy based on this feedback to improve its tool-use behavior. Environment feedback can be categorized into two forms: (1) result feedback, which is ultimate feedback returned from the environment, indicating whether the model’s actions have successfully completed

3.3 训练模型以改进工具学习

购买商品。通过行为克隆，训练后的智能体在根据人类指令购买正确商品上展现出非平凡表现。

半监督学习。

通常由于时间与成本考虑，人类行为不易记录。然而，大规模未标注数据往往可得，我们或可从中构造弱的、有噪声的监督。值得注意的是，近期工作表明，我们可以用能力较弱的模型在未标注数据上标注伪标签，并把它们转成弱监督的工具使用示范。例如，在少量种子标注数据上，Baker et al. (2022) 训练一个模型来预测 Minecraft 视频游戏中每个时间步所采取动作的伪标签。从这些伪标签学习，可以训练更强的模型，而无需在目标环境中展开模型或进行大规模金标准人类行为标注。

自监督学习。

尽管降低了对人类行为标注的沉重需求，半监督学习仍需要种子标注数据集来获得伪标签。此外，种子数据集中的偏置也可能在训练中被放大，导致泛化差。为此，研究者近期表明，只需少量示范，基础模型就能以自监督方式自学如何利用工具（Parisi et al., 2022; Schick et al., 2023）。例如，Toolformer（Schick et al., 2023）利用基础模型的上下文学习能力，基于少量人工撰写的例子迭代自举工具使用例子。这些自动生成的例子再经过过滤以降低噪声。最终的工具使用数据集包含充分监督，显著提升了 GPT-J（Wang & Komatsuzaki, 2021）的工具使用表现，凸显了自监督学习增强工具使用能力的潜力。

3.3.2 从反馈中学习

收集人工标注的工具使用例子——可能包括人类行为的完整轨迹与最终答案——既耗时又费力。而且，学到的模型可能无法有效适应新环境，因为它遵从已记录的人类行为。此外，显式标注环境条件与智能体行为的每一种可能场景并不现实（Codevilla et al., 2019）。另一种方式是，人类从试错中学习以纠正并改正其工具使用行为（Allen et al., 2019）。类似地，来自环境与人的反馈可以使模型理解其动作的后果并适应其行为。来自反馈的监督也可以增强以监督方式训练的智能体的能力（Nakano et al., 2021; Baker et al., 2022）。形式化地，从反馈中学习可描述为用查询集 Q = {qi}i 在开放探索中优化控制器参数 θC：

θ*C = arg max_θC E_{qi∈Q} E_{{ai,t}∼pθC} [ R({ai,t}) ]，

（4）

其中 R 是从反馈序列估计的奖励，Ti 表示处理 qi 所需的迭代次数。

强化学习（RL）用于工具学习。

RL 是使人工智能体在复杂决策过程中从环境学习的常见方案（Silver et al., 2018; Berner et al., 2019; Schrittwieser et al., 2020）。工具学习可被视为一种 RL 场景：动作空间由工具定义，智能体学习选择合适工具并执行能最大化奖励信号的正确动作。策略模型可由基础模型初始化（Schulman et al., 2017）。这种初始化给策略模型带来丰富的先验知识，减轻 RL 智能体学习基本技能的需求。有了量化智能体达成任务目标表现的奖励函数，RL 已在各种工具学习场景中成功使用，如机器人抓取（Levine et al., 2018）与多智能体自动课程（Baker et al., 2020）。通过优化损失函数，智能体学会反思环境的当前状态、选择合适工具，并执行导向最高期望奖励的正确动作。下面我们介绍两种反馈来源：环境反馈与人类反馈，它们可被视为工具学习语境中的奖励信号来源。这两种反馈互补，可以彼此结合。

环境反馈。

控制器与环境交互，并接收关于其动作后果的反馈。模型随后基于该反馈更新其策略以改进工具使用行为。环境反馈可分为两种形式：（1）结果反馈，即环境返回的最终反馈，表明模型的动作是否成功完成

3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning the task or not. This type of feedback performs an overall assessment of the planning generated by the model. For instance, WebShop (Yao et al., 2022a) uses a hand-coded reward to assess the similarity between human-bought and model-bought products, which indicates whether the actions performed by the controller lead to the correct final product. By receiving feedback on the success or failure of its actions, the model can iteratively update its planning strategy, and adjust its decision-making process; (2) intermediate feedback, which refers to the state change of the environment triggered by an action. By observing the state changes, foundation models can learn whether each action is effective and appropriate, making the model better adjust its behaviors accordingly. This kind of feedback provides more detailed and timely information about the effectiveness of each tool execution. Take the case of interacting with a search engine to gather information for question-answering, models could update their policy for more efficient information retrieval by observing the rendered information of a search query. Human Feedback. Humans could give the model rewards and penalties based on its generated plans to regulate its behavior. Human feedback can be explicit, which provides clear and direct insights into the model performance representing human preferences. For example, rating the quality of the model-generated action on a scale of 1 to 5; human feedback can also be implicit, which is not directly specified by the user but can be derived from user behavior and interactions with the model. Examples include users’ comparison (Ouyang et al., 2022), response time, and actions taken after receiving a model’s output (e.g., clicking on a recommended link). Though human feedback is accurate and stable, it is label-intensive and has high latency. To address this issue, reinforcement learning from human feedback (RLHF) (Christiano et al., 2017) is proposed to finetune a model to imitate humans to give rewards, which are then used to optimize the policy with RL algorithms such as PPO (Schulman et al., 2017). RLHF has yielded exceptional performance in various domains such as text summarization (Ziegler et al., 2019; Stiennon et al., 2020). RLHF can also improve a model’s tooluse capabilities even if it has been trained on sufficient supervised human demonstrations. For instance, WebGPT (Nakano et al., 2021) utilizes human feedback to guide a policy model to align with human preferences, which helps better manipulate search engines to answer long-form questions. Despite its remarkable performance, RLHF still faces challenges: (1) task-specific nature: the corresponding evaluation criteria for specific tasks need to be pre-defined, and the preference data annotated for one task is hard to be transferred to other settings, which limits the applicability of RLHF to a wider range of tasks. To this end, it is critical to develop a universal reward model that generalizes to various tasks; (2) biases: RL agents optimize towards the pseudo-human reward model, thus can be up-bounded and biased by human preferences. Besides, societal biases or personal experiences may be amplified during RLHF, and it is essential to carefully evaluate the learned reward model for any biases and take measures to mitigate them. 3.3.3 Generalizable Tool Learning Generalization of tool use is a key characteristic of human intelligence (Seed & Byrne, 2010; Teschke et al., 2013; Osiurak et al., 2018). The ancient human, for instance, recognized that regardless of the specific tool being used, a sharp edge was essential for achieving clean cuts and efficiently carrying out tasks. This recognition allowed them to transfer their knowledge of sharpening a knife to sharpening other tools, such as scrapers or choppers. Generalization is also a critical aspect of tool learning, especially considering the existence of a massive and rapidly expanding array of tools. Although conducting supervised fine-tuning on a vast collection of tool-use data can be a potential solution to facilitating generalization, collecting enough supervised tool-use data and ensuring its quality and diversity is time-consuming and practically infeasible. Generalizable tool learning highlights the importance of abstraction, which is the process of identifying the essential features of a tool. Abstraction involves recognizing commonalities and patterns of tools so that models could synthesize and transfer their knowledge and skills, enabling them to use novel tools with ease. For instance, by abstracting essential features such as layers, filters, and color adjustments, users can transfer their knowledge of using Adobe Photoshop to Adobe Illustrator, even if the interface and specific tool names in these two figure-editing software are different. Abstracting these general features of tools can quickly help users learn a new tool effectively by building on previous experience. Foundation of Generalization: Interface Unification. To facilitate knowledge transfer among tools, it is critical to design a unified interface that enables the model to manipulate various tools in a consistent and standardized manner, which serves as the foundation for generalizable tool learning. Through a unified interface, models can identify and abstract essential features of tools more easily in a unified tool protocol rather than grappling with the difficulty of understanding various tool interfaces. Currently, the manipulation

任务。这类反馈对模型生成的规划做总体评估。例如，WebShop（Yao et al., 2022a）用手工编码的奖励评估人类购买与模型购买商品之间的相似度，以表明控制器执行的动作是否导向正确的最终商品。通过接收关于其动作成败的反馈，模型可以迭代更新规划策略并调整决策过程；（2）中间反馈，指由动作触发的环境状态变化。通过观察状态变化，基础模型可以学习每个动作是否有效、是否恰当，从而更好调整行为。这类反馈提供关于每次工具执行有效性的更详细、更及时的信息。以与搜索引擎交互来为问答收集信息为例，模型可通过观察搜索查询渲染出的信息来更新策略，以实现更高效的信息检索。

人类反馈。

人类可以根据模型生成的计划给出奖励与惩罚，以调节其行为。人类反馈可以是显式的，提供代表人类偏好的、关于模型表现的清晰直接洞见。例如，在 1 到 5 的量表上评定模型生成动作的质量；人类反馈也可以是隐式的，并非由用户直接指定，而可从用户行为及其与模型的交互中导出。例子包括用户比较（Ouyang et al., 2022）、响应时间，以及收到模型输出后采取的动作（如点击推荐链接）。

尽管人类反馈准确且稳定，它标注密集、延迟高。为解决这一问题，从人类反馈中强化学习（RLHF）（Christiano et al., 2017）被提出：微调一个模型来模仿人类给奖励，再用 PPO（Schulman et al., 2017）等 RL 算法优化策略。RLHF 已在文本摘要等多个领域取得非凡表现（Ziegler et al., 2019; Stiennon et al., 2020）。即便模型已在充分的监督人类示范上训练，RLHF 仍可提升其工具使用能力。例如，WebGPT（Nakano et al., 2021）利用人类反馈引导策略模型与人类偏好对齐，从而更好操纵搜索引擎以回答长文问题。

尽管表现显著，RLHF 仍面临挑战：（1）任务特定性：特定任务的相应评价标准需要预先定义，为一个任务标注的偏好数据难以迁移到其他设定，这限制了 RLHF 对更广任务的适用性。为此，开发能泛化到各种任务的通用奖励模型至关重要；（2）偏置：RL 智能体朝伪人类奖励模型优化，因而可能被人类偏好上界与偏置。此外，社会偏置或个人经验可能在 RLHF 中被放大，必须仔细评估学到的奖励模型中的任何偏置并采取措施缓解。

3.3.3 可泛化的工具学习

工具使用的泛化是人类智能的关键特征（Seed & Byrne, 2010; Teschke et al., 2013; Osiurak et al., 2018）。例如，远古人类认识到，无论使用何种具体工具，锋利的刃口对实现干净切割并高效完成任务至关重要。这一认识使他们能把磨刀的知识迁移到打磨刮削器或砍砸器等其他工具。泛化也是工具学习的关键方面，尤其考虑到存在海量且迅速扩张的工具阵列。尽管在大量工具使用数据上做监督微调可以是促进泛化的潜在方案，收集足够的监督工具使用数据并确保其质量与多样性既耗时又在实践中不可行。

可泛化的工具学习强调抽象的重要性，即识别工具本质特征的过程。抽象涉及认识工具的共性与模式，使模型能综合并迁移其知识与技能，从而轻松使用新工具。例如，通过抽象图层、滤镜与颜色调整等本质特征，用户可以把使用 Adobe Photoshop 的知识迁移到 Adobe Illustrator，即使这两款图像编辑软件的界面与具体工具名称不同。抽象这些一般特征可以建立在先前经验之上，快速帮助用户有效学习新工具。

泛化的基础：接口统一。

为促进工具间的知识迁移，关键是设计统一接口，使模型能以一致、标准化的方式操纵各种工具，这构成可泛化工具学习的基础。通过统一接口，模型可以在统一的工具协议中更容易识别并抽象工具的本质特征，而不是费力理解各种工具接口。目前，工具的操纵

3.3 Training Models for Improved Tool Learning of tools is through predicting discrete action tokens, and the action space is not aligned in different scenarios, which prohibits the models from quickly adapting to new scenarios and tools. Inspired by the aspect we categorize tools in § 2.2, we identify three potential ways of interface unification: the semantic interface, GUI interface, and programming interface.

  • Semantic Interface. The semantic interface operates by utilizing a specific text span (action name) as the action trigger, which is the most intuitive and natural way for interface unification. For instance, ReAct (Yao et al., 2022b) employs Action:Search as the trigger for the function that searches for relevant passages. In robotic manipulation (Ahn et al., 2022; Liu et al., 2023), the generated natural language (e.g., pick up the sponge) is mapped to specific actions. Despite its ease of implementation, the semantic interface poses certain challenges that must be addressed. First, the mapping between the generated text and the corresponding tool action should be pre-defined individually, which is a laborious task, particularly when the tool set expands quickly. Moreover, the model may fail to accurately produce the precise form to trigger the intended action, even leading to false triggering of actions.
  • GUI Interface. Humans primarily interact with the digital world through GUI interface (e.g., mouse and keyboard), which has been extensively optimized to follow human action efficiently. Nevertheless, before robots can learn to use a GUI interface flexibly, it is necessary to establish a virtual environment that can facilitate mapping predicted tokens to human-like mouse movements and keyboard inputs. Prior research has explored providing platforms for agents to complete web-based tasks using keyboard and mouse actions (Shi et al., 2017; Liu et al., 2018a). However, these environments restrict models to a limited set of pre-defined mouse options and common keyboard actions such as copy and paste. By leveraging foundation models, it is possible to introduce prior knowledge regarding common combinations of keyword and mouse actions, thereby expanding the potential actions that a model can execute.
  • Programming Interface. This kind of interface allows the model to go beyond pure natural language and specify its action using a program. Such unification requires the model to be acquainted with the syntax of the function calls. The recent code-generating language models (CLM) such as Incoder (Fried et al., 2022) and CodeX (Chen et al., 2021) provide the possibility of such unification. The programming interface has been applied widely. For example, Code-as-Policies (Liang et al., 2022a) finds that with CLM as the backbone for robotic control, the robots can leverage the code grammar to execute complex actions, generalize to novel instructions, and give precise control with accurate parameter values to the functions. The programming interface provides promising opportunities for tool learning because (1) complex tool learning logic can be modeled using the control flow of programming language; (2) explicit calls of external APIs can be naturally implemented by executing programs. It should be noted that the interface selection should align with the capabilities and limitations of the foundation model. For instance, language foundation models are trained to generate text and may be better suited for the semantic interface. Similarly, a multimodal foundation model that combines visual and textual information may be more appropriate for the GUI interface, as it can understand and generate human-like mouse movements and keyboard inputs. On the other hand, code foundation models may be more suitable for the programming interface, as it is trained to understand code syntax and function calls. Under certain cases, we may face challenges where the tool’s output is not aligned with model’s input format. A common practice is to compose the functionality of the model and tool in the same modality. For example, Zeng et al. (2022) chain together foundation models of various modalities by converting their outputs into natural languages. This simple method leverages prompting to compose new multimodal capabilities without fine-tuning. In contrast, another solution is to building multimodal foundation models that can perceive general modalities, based on the belief that multimodal foundation models can all be unified through a general-purpose interface (Alayrac et al., 2022; Hao et al., 2022). Gato (Reed et al., 2022) is a representative generalist multi-embodiment agent trained on tremendous datasets of agent experience. Gato can sense and act with different embodiments, such as playing Atari, captioning images, chatting, etc. Similarly, PaLM-E (Driess et al., 2023) incorporates continuous inputs from different modalities into a PLM. By joint training on multiple embodied tasks, PaLM-E could make grounded decisions in the real world. Strategies of Generalizable Tool Learning. In general, a unified interface enables models to learn and transfer knowledge more easily and efficiently, but it does not guarantee optimal learning outcomes in all scenarios. Generalizable tool learning requires models to further adapt, refine, and specialize their learned knowledge to specific tasks or domains. Here, we discuss two potential approaches to achieving this goal and facilitating generalization.

3.3 训练模型以改进工具学习

是通过预测离散动作 token，且不同场景的动作空间并不对齐，这阻碍模型快速适应新场景与新工具。受 § 2.2 中工具分类的启发，我们识别出接口统一的三种潜在方式：语义接口、GUI 接口与编程接口。

  • 语义接口。语义接口通过利用特定文本跨度（动作名）作为动作触发器来运作，这是接口统一最直观、最自然的方式。例如，ReAct（Yao et al., 2022b）用 Action:Search 作为搜索相关段落的函数触发器。在机器人操纵中（Ahn et al., 2022; Liu et al., 2023），生成的自然语言（如 pick up the sponge）被映射到具体动作。尽管易于实现，语义接口也带来必须应对的挑战。首先，生成文本与对应工具动作之间的映射需要逐个预定义，当工具集迅速扩张时这是费力的任务。此外，模型可能无法准确产生触发预期动作的精确形式，甚至导致错误触发动作。
  • GUI 接口。人类主要通过 GUI 接口（如鼠标与键盘）与数字世界交互，该接口已被广泛优化以高效跟随人类动作。然而，在机器人能灵活使用 GUI 接口之前，有必要建立虚拟环境，以促进把预测 token 映射到类人的鼠标移动与键盘输入。先前研究已探索为智能体提供用键盘与鼠标动作完成基于网页任务的平台（Shi et al., 2017; Liu et al., 2018a）。然而，这些环境把模型限制在有限的预定义鼠标选项与复制粘贴等常见键盘动作上。通过利用基础模型，有可能引入关于关键词与鼠标动作常见组合的先验知识，从而扩展模型可执行的潜在动作。
  • 编程接口。这类接口允许模型超越纯自然语言，用程序指定其动作。这种统一要求模型熟悉函数调用的语法。近期的代码生成语言模型（CLM）如 Incoder（Fried et al., 2022）与 Codex（Chen et al., 2021）为这种统一提供了可能。编程接口已被广泛应用。例如，Code-as-Policies（Liang et al., 2022a）发现，以 CLM 作为机器人控制的主干，机器人可以利用代码语法执行复杂动作、泛化到新颖指令，并用精确参数值对函数给出精确控制。编程接口为工具学习提供有前景的机会，因为（1）复杂的工具学习逻辑可以用编程语言的控制流来建模；（2）对外部 API 的显式调用可以通过执行程序自然实现。

应注意，接口选择应与基础模型的能力与局限对齐。例如，语言基础模型被训练来生成文本，可能更适合语义接口。类似地，结合视觉与文本信息的多模态基础模型可能更适合 GUI 接口，因为它可以理解并生成类人的鼠标移动与键盘输入。另一方面，代码基础模型可能更适合编程接口，因为它被训练来理解代码语法与函数调用。

在某些情况下，我们可能面临工具输出与模型输入格式不对齐的挑战。常见做法是在同一模态中组合模型与工具的功能。例如，Zeng et al. (2022) 通过把输出转成自然语言来把不同模态的基础模型串起来。这一简单方法利用提示来组合新的多模态能力而无需微调。相反，另一方案是构建能感知一般模态的多模态基础模型，其信念是多模态基础模型都可以通过通用接口统一（Alayrac et al., 2022; Hao et al., 2022）。Gato（Reed et al., 2022）是在海量智能体经验数据集上训练的代表性通用多具身智能体。Gato 可以用不同具身去感知与行动，如玩 Atari、给图像加标题、聊天等。类似地，PaLM-E（Driess et al., 2023）把来自不同模态的连续输入纳入 PLM。通过在多个具身任务上联合训练，PaLM-E 可以在真实世界中做出有根基的决策。

可泛化工具学习的策略。

一般而言，统一接口使模型能更轻松、高效地学习并迁移知识，但它并不能保证在所有场景中都有最优学习结果。可泛化的工具学习要求模型进一步适应、精炼并将其学到的知识专门化到特定任务或领域。此处我们讨论实现这一目标并促进泛化的两种潜在方法。

白话解读

本页给出三种统一接口：语义触发词、GUI 键鼠、以及写代码调函数。作者倾向编程接口能表达控制流与 API。Gato / PaLM-E 则走「一种模型吃多种模态」。 对 harness 工程，这页几乎是在问：工具协议到底用自然语言、点按钮，还是函数签名。 类比：对工人下口令、看他点屏幕，或直接让他写一段可执行脚本。

第 21 / 75 页
  • Meta Tool Learning. Metacognition (Clarebout et al., 2013) is a crucial aspect of human intelligence that allows individuals to reflect on their own thinking and adapt their behaviors when faced with unfamiliar situations. In the context of tool learning, metacognition refers to the ability of a model to reflect on its own learning process and adapt new tool-use strategies when necessary. With metacognition, models can identify common underlying principles or patterns in tool-use strategies and transfer them to new tasks or domains. Take the case of the web search tool, when the model trained on a source search engine (e.g., Bing Search) is transferred to a target one (e.g., Google Search), the model can leverage its metacognitive awareness to adapt its tool-use strategies based on its previous experiences. This may include identifying common underlying patterns in tool-use strategies, such as effective search queries, relevant results, and user feedback, and using this metacognitive awareness to better align with the algorithms and user interface of the new search engine.
  • Curriculum Tool Learning. Another approach to improving model generalization is through curriculum learning (Bengio et al., 2009), which starts with simple tools and gradually introduces the model to more complex tools so that it can build upon its prior knowledge and develop a deeper understanding of the tool. For instance, we could start with a curriculum of basic algorithms and operations to effectively teach a model to use Mathematica6, e.g., addition and subtraction, and then gradually move on to more complex mathematical concepts like calculus and linear algebra. This training strategy ensures that the model is introduced to the simple, essential features of the tool before moving on to more complex concepts in a way that is manageable and effective. Moreover, curriculum tool learning allows the model to learn how complex tools are built upon simple tools. It provides an understanding of how a complex tool can be seen as an updated high-level version of a simple tool, and how its function is a combination of several basic tools. This understanding of the relationship between simple and complex tools facilitates the transfer of previously learned knowledge to new tools, enabling the model to more effectively identify similarities and differences between situations and adjust its approach accordingly. Application and Experiment In this section, we aim to explore the applications of tool learning and investigate the efficacy and limitations of state-of-the-art foundation models in utilizing tools. We select 18 representative tools for evaluation and place the main results in this section. For more case studies of ChatGPT, please refer to Appendix A. 4.1 Evaluated Tools We first briefly introduce the tools selected in experiments as follows: Machine Translator. General-purpose language models may exhibit suboptimal proficiency when processing text from multiple linguistic domains. Machine translators can effectively alleviate this issue by enabling non-translation-dedicated language models to better comprehend multi-lingual texts. Following Toolformer, we use NLLB (Costa-jussà et al., 2022) as our translator and choose MLQA (Lewis et al., 2020a), a multilingual question answering benchmark, as the testbed. Given a context in English and a question in Arabian, the task requires answering the question using English. We randomly sample 200 test instances from the original test data. For the evaluation metric, we choose F1-score. Calculator. Following the setting of Toolformer, we conduct experiments in which language models use a simple calculator to solve math word problems. We choose a simple implementation for the calculator, which supports basic arithmetic operations (i.e., +, −, ×, ÷). We evaluate two math word problem datasets: ASDiv (Miao et al., 2020) and MathQA (Amini et al., 2019) and choose accuracy as the metric. Map. We choose Bing Map API7 for location information retrieval, assisting in user queries related to the route, driving distance, latitude coordinates, nearby locations of interest, etc. We manually curate user queries through crowdsourcing. Weather. We choose Weather API8 and investigate whether models could use the tool to answer weatherrelated questions, such as questions about current weather in any city, forecasting the weather within two weeks in any city, and giving suggestions based on the weather information. Two APIs are supported, the first one is GetWeatherToday<city>, which provides the current weather condition of a city; another 6https://www.wolfram.com/mathematica 7https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/bingmaps 8https://www.weatherapi.com
  • 元工具学习。元认知（Clarebout et al., 2013）是人类智能的关键方面，使人能在面对陌生情境时反思自身思维并适应行为。在工具学习语境中，元认知指模型反思自身学习过程、并在必要时适应新的工具使用策略的能力。有了元认知，模型可以识别工具使用策略中的共同底层原则或模式，并把它们迁移到新任务或领域。以网页搜索工具为例，当在源搜索引擎（如 Bing Search）上训练的模型被迁移到目标搜索引擎（如 Google Search）时，模型可利用其元认知意识，基于先前经验适应工具使用策略。这可能包括识别有效搜索查询、相关结果与用户反馈等共同底层模式，并利用这种元认知意识更好对齐新搜索引擎的算法与用户界面。
  • 课程式工具学习。改进模型泛化的另一途径是课程学习（Bengio et al., 2009）：从简单工具开始，逐渐向模型引入更复杂工具，使其能建立在先验知识之上并对工具形成更深理解。例如，我们可以从基本算法与运算的课程开始，以有效教模型使用 Mathematica6，如加法与减法，然后逐渐进入微积分与线性代数等更复杂的数学概念。这一训练策略确保模型在以可管理、有效的方式进入更复杂概念之前，先被引入工具的简单、本质特征。此外，课程式工具学习使模型能理解复杂工具如何建立在简单工具之上。它提供这样一种理解：复杂工具可被视为简单工具的更新高层版本，其功能是若干基本工具的组合。对简单与复杂工具关系的这种理解，促进先前学到的知识向新工具迁移，使模型能更有效识别情境之间的相似与差异并相应调整方法。

应用与实验

本节旨在探索工具学习的应用，并考察最先进基础模型在利用工具上的效能与局限。我们选择 18 种代表性工具进行评测，主要结果放在本节。关于 ChatGPT 的更多案例研究，请参阅附录 A。

4.1 评测所用工具

我们首先简要介绍实验中所选工具如下：

机器翻译器。通用语言模型在处理来自多个语言域的文本时可能表现不佳。机器翻译器可通过使非翻译专用语言模型更好理解多语言文本来有效缓解该问题。遵循 Toolformer，我们用 NLLB（Costa-jussà et al., 2022）作为翻译器，并选择多语言问答基准 MLQA（Lewis et al., 2020a）作为测试床。给定英语上下文与阿拉伯语问题，任务要求用英语回答问题。我们从原始测试数据中随机采样 200 个测试实例。评测指标选择 F1 分数。

计算器。遵循 Toolformer 的设定，我们进行语言模型使用简单计算器解数学应用题的实验。我们为计算器选择简单实现，支持基本算术运算（即 +、−、×、÷）。我们评测两个数学应用题数据集：ASDiv（Miao et al., 2020）与 MathQA（Amini et al., 2019），并以准确率为指标。

地图。我们选择 Bing Map API7 做位置信息检索，协助与路线、驾驶距离、纬度坐标、附近兴趣点等相关的用户查询。我们通过众包人工策划用户查询。

天气。我们选择 Weather API8，考察模型能否用该工具回答与天气相关的问题，如任意城市的当前天气、任意城市两周内的天气预报，以及基于天气信息给出建议。支持两个 API，第一个是 GetWeatherToday<city>，提供某城市的当前天气状况；另一个

4.1 Evaluated Tools one is ForecastWeather<city, N>, which forecasts the weather of a city after N days. The detailed information returned includes the temperature, wind speed, UV index, sunrise, sunset time, etc. We manually curated 100 weather-related user queries. Stock. We choose Alpha Vantage Stock API9 for stock market querying. We aim to obtain specific information about the opening, closing, highest, or lowest price for one particular stock on one specific day or month. We manually curate 1200 question-answer pairs and choose accuracy as the evaluation metric. Slides. Slides-making is traditionally performed by humans using a human-computer interface (e.g., mouse and keyboard). However, current models cannot directly move a mouse or press computer keys. To address this limitation, we provide six APIs with high-level semantics for the model. Four APIs are built based on the open-source library python-pptx10 to control the slides, one API allows the model to retrieve images from the internet based on a topic, and one API is used to submit and display the final slides to the user. To collect the data, we brainstorm 50 different careers that require slides-making in their work, for each career, we brainstorm 2 cases where practitioners have to make slides. The final dataset consists of 100 slides-making tasks. We evaluate the model’s performance by counting the fraction of instances in which the model-generated API calls are correctly executed without errors. Table Processing. We craft a suite of table processing APIs using pandas.DataFrame in Python. By leveraging these tools, models can provide a more natural and streamlined experience for users, allowing them to perform data analysis and visualization tasks directly. We manually construct a table processing dataset containing 13 tables and 117 corresponding queries. Knowledge Graphs. Knowledge graphs contain factual knowledge about the real world, which is stored in the form of RDF triplets. The triplets can be retrieved by SPARQL (Standard Protocol and RDF Query Language). We provide 7 APIs that mimic the process of human querying the knowledge graph, including showing the candidate entity/relation given a name surface form, showing a head entity’s home page, showing a tail entity’s home page, sending SPARQL queries, showing the result of SPARQL queries, and finding a keyword in the output of a SPARQL query. We curate 64 questions that could be answered by querying knowledge graphs. Search Engine. We choose Bing Search API11 and test the model on real-time question answering. Two APIs are supported: the first one is Search<query>, which returns the top-related search results back to the model; another one is LoadPage<N>, which loads the detailed information of page N indexed in the search results, and returns the detailed contents. We experiment with RealTimeQA (Kasai et al., 2022), which is a dynamic question-answering platform that inquires about novel events or information. Specifically, we choose the most recent release (20230217 version) of multiple-choice data for evaluation. Given the question and choices, the model is expected to interact with the search engine to extract the necessary information, before settling on the final answer. Wikipedia. We largely build our Wikipedia Search tool upon ReAct (Yao et al., 2022b) with slight modifications on the API designs. The tool consists of 3 APIs: search<entity>, which searches for an exact entity name on Wikipedia and returns the first 5 sentences of the corresponding page if the entity exists; otherwise, it displays related entity names; lookup<keyword>, which looks up the keyword on the current page and returns the next sentence containing the keyword, similar to humans’ using the CTRL+F function on a web page; disambiguate<entity>, which inputs an entity name and displays all entities that share the same name. We focus on HotpotQA (Yang et al., 2018b) for question answering. We conduct our experiments in an open-domain setting, where only the question is shown to the model. We randomly sample 200 instances from the dataset. Online Shopping. Amazon online shopping is a relatively complex web environment, in which models need to buy a commodity that satisfies various requirements mentioned in a user instruction. Based on WebShop (Yao et al., 2022a), we build our online shopping tool, which covers mainstream online shopping actions including searching for an item, loading detailed information about an item, choosing a feature for an item, going to the previous/next page, deciding to purchase, etc. We use the dataset provided by WebShop and randomly sample 100 test instances, which cover instructions about various customers’ needs with specific requirements of commodities’ attributes. Embodied Scene. ALFWorld (Shridhar et al., 2021) is an aligned text and embodied environment game, where agents need to interact with objects (e.g., fridge, microwave, drawer, etc.) in a house to complete a task 9https://www.alphavantage.co/documentation 10https://pypi.org/project/python-pptx 11https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/bing/apis/bing-web-search-api

是 ForecastWeather<city, N>，预报某城市 N 天后的天气。返回的详细信息包括温度、风速、紫外线指数、日出日落时间等。我们人工策划了 100 条与天气相关的用户查询。

股票。我们选择 Alpha Vantage Stock API9 做股票市场查询。目标是获取某一特定股票在某一特定日或月的开盘、收盘、最高或最低价等具体信息。我们人工策划了 1200 个问答对，并以准确率为评测指标。

幻灯片。制作幻灯片传统上由人用鼠标键盘等人机界面完成。然而，当前模型不能直接移动鼠标或按键盘。为解决这一局限，我们为模型提供六个带高层语义的 API。四个 API 基于开源库 python-pptx10 构建以控制幻灯片，一个 API 允许模型按主题从互联网检索图像，一个 API 用于向用户提交并展示最终幻灯片。为收集数据，我们头脑风暴了 50 种工作中需要做幻灯片的职业，对每种职业头脑风暴 2 个必须做幻灯片的情形。最终数据集包含 100 个幻灯片制作任务。我们通过统计模型生成的 API 调用被正确执行且无错误的实例比例来评测表现。

表格处理。我们用 Python 中的 pandas.DataFrame 制作一套表格处理 API。借助这些工具，模型可为用户提供更自然、更顺畅的体验，使其能直接执行数据分析与可视化任务。我们人工构造了一个包含 13 张表与 117 条对应查询的表格处理数据集。

知识图谱。知识图谱以 RDF 三元组形式存储关于真实世界的事实知识。三元组可通过 SPARQL（标准协议与 RDF 查询语言）检索。我们提供 7 个模仿人类查询知识图谱过程的 API，包括根据名称表层形式展示候选实体/关系、展示头实体主页、展示尾实体主页、发送 SPARQL 查询、展示 SPARQL 查询结果，以及在 SPARQL 查询输出中查找关键词。我们策划了 64 个可通过查询知识图谱回答的问题。

搜索引擎。我们选择 Bing Search API11，并在实时问答上测试模型。支持两个 API：第一个是 Search<query>，把最相关的搜索结果返回给模型；另一个是 LoadPage<N>，加载搜索结果中索引为 N 的页面的详细信息并返回详细内容。我们用 RealTimeQA（Kasai et al., 2022）做实验，这是一个询问新事件或信息的动态问答平台。具体地，我们选择最新发布（20230217 版）的选择题数据做评测。给定问题与选项，模型应与搜索引擎交互以提取必要信息，再确定最终答案。

维基百科。我们大体建立在 ReAct（Yao et al., 2022b）的维基百科搜索工具之上，对 API 设计略作修改。该工具包含 3 个 API：search<entity>，在维基百科上搜索精确实体名，若实体存在则返回对应页面的前 5 句，否则显示相关实体名；lookup<keyword>，在当前页查找关键词并返回下一句包含该关键词的句子，类似人类在网页上使用 CTRL+F；disambiguate<entity>，输入实体名并显示所有同名实体。我们聚焦 HotpotQA（Yang et al., 2018b）做问答。实验在开放域设定下进行，只向模型展示问题。我们从数据集中随机采样 200 个实例。

在线购物。亚马逊在线购物是相对复杂的网页环境，模型需要购买满足用户指令中提到的各种要求的商品。基于 WebShop（Yao et al., 2022a），我们构建在线购物工具，覆盖主流在线购物动作，包括搜索商品、加载商品详细信息、为商品选择特征、前往上一/下一页、决定购买等。我们使用 WebShop 提供的数据集并随机采样 100 个测试实例，覆盖关于顾客各种需求与商品属性具体要求的指令。

具身场景。ALFWorld（Shridhar et al., 2021）是对齐的文本与具身环境游戏，智能体需要与房屋中的物体（如冰箱、微波炉、抽屉等）交互以完成任务

第 24 / 75 页

4.2 Experiments can perform: (1) finding the target recipe, and (2) answering questions based on observed details. We manually curate 50 queries for evaluation. Movie Search. We choose Douban Film API13 to search for movie-related information. Three APIs are devised with the aim of discovering movies that are currently playing or upcoming, as well as extracting detailed information about each movie. We curate 60 questions about the movies, such as recommending some movies which are on display or upcoming and providing a brief introduction to a movie. AI Painting. AI image generation model has been widely used by human artists. To endow models with the capacity to create images using the AI image generation model, we provide the following APIs: one API generates an image given a prompt using stable diffusion (Rombach et al., 2022), others are the image segmentation 14 and image inpainting APIs 15, which replace a target object in an image with a new object described by a prompt. We curate 25 queries as the initial prompt, together with subsequent queries for modifying that image. 3D Model Construction. We investigate three-dimensional (3D) modeling by manually devising a collection of APIs that leverage the capabilities of the sophisticated 3D rendering engine Taichi16. Due to the complexity of executing this API (3D rendering), we only demonstrate the performance on 10 curated questions. Chemical Properties. To evaluate the capability of tool learning in professional domains, we utilize the Chemical Property query, and more specifically, the PubChem17 API for resolving scientific inquiries. 4 APIs are supported, which facilitate the retrieval of a chemical’s identification number based on the name or SMILES notation (Weininger, 1988), as well as obtaining the chemical’s properties based on its identification number. We manually curated 100 questions for evaluation. Database. We explore the potential of tool learning in accessing database data via natural language. The fundamental APIs include (i) obtaining the structural information of the target data (schema); (ii) translating the input natural language text into an equivalent SQL query; (iii) rewriting the query into an executionefficient one; and (iv) querying the result data by connecting to the database. We automatically curated 50 relatively complex queries under the TPC-H schema, which are of relatively complex structures (involving 2-6 tables and composite predicates) and take over 111.5 minutes to execute in total. The evaluation metric is the ratio of queries for which tool learning can accurately output the results. To facilitate future research attempts, we implement and integrate all the above tools into BMTools18, which is an open-source repository that extends foundation models using tools and also serves as a platform for the community to build and share tools. With BMTools, users can easily build a new plugin by writing Python functions and also integrating external tools from other sources (e.g., ChatGPT Plugins). Building a tool library for foundation models is critical to connecting foundation models with tools and we are glad to see there are emerging works in this direction. LangChain 19 is the first open-sourced project that attempts to chain foundation models with tools. Under a unified interface, users could either build their own task pipelines or let the foundation models call APIs. Most recently, TaskMatrix.AI (Liang et al., 2023) and HuggingGPT (Shen et al., 2023) extend APIs and tasks to broader scenarios, including multimodal models for visual tasks, local software, and cloud service APIs. OpenAI also proposed its official tool library, ChatGPT Plugins 20, to empower ChatGPT with other applications. By simply providing APIs with descriptions, ChatGPT is enabled to call applications and complete more complex tasks. Different from third-party libraries, ChatGPT plugins are cautious about safety risks and establish strict standards for plugins. The library prioritizes the most essential tools such as the web browser, code interpreter, and retrieval plugin. 4.2 Experiments Settings. We conduct experiments on all the above tools and choose both text-davinci-003 and ChatGPT to evaluate their performance with zero-shot prompting and few-shot prompting as mentioned in § 3.2.1: 13https://movie.douban.com 14https://huggingface.co/CIDAS/clipseg-rd64-refined 15https://huggingface.co/runwayml/stable-diffusion-inpainting 16https://github.com/taichi-dev/voxel-challenge 17https://pubchem.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov 18https://github.com/OpenBMB/BMTools 19https://docs.langchain.com 20https://openai.com/blog/chatgpt-plugins

4.2 实验

可以执行：（1）找到目标食谱，（2）基于观察到的细节回答问题。我们人工策划 50 条查询做评测。

电影搜索。我们选择豆瓣电影 API13 搜索与电影相关的信息。设计了三个 API，旨在发现正在上映或即将上映的电影，并提取每部电影的详细信息。我们策划了 60 个关于电影的问题，如推荐一些正在上映或即将上映的电影，以及提供某部电影的简要介绍。

AI 绘画。AI 图像生成模型已被人类艺术家广泛使用。为赋予模型用 AI 图像生成模型创作图像的能力，我们提供如下 API：一个 API 用 stable diffusion（Rombach et al., 2022）根据提示生成图像，其他是图像分割14 与图像修复 API15，用提示描述的新对象替换图像中的目标对象。我们策划 25 条查询作为初始提示，以及后续修改该图像的查询。

三维模型构建。我们通过人工设计一套利用精密三维渲染引擎 Taichi16 能力的 API 来考察三维（3D）建模。由于执行该 API（三维渲染）的复杂性，我们仅在 10 个策划问题上展示表现。

化学性质。为评测工具学习在专业领域的能力，我们利用化学性质查询，更具体地，用 PubChem17 API 解决科学询问。支持 4 个 API，便于根据名称或 SMILES 记号（Weininger, 1988）检索化学品标识号，以及根据标识号获取化学品性质。我们人工策划 100 个问题做评测。

数据库。我们探索通过自然语言访问数据库数据的工具学习潜力。基本 API 包括（i）获取目标数据的结构信息（schema）；（ii）把输入自然语言文本翻译成等价 SQL 查询；（iii）把查询改写成执行高效的查询；以及（iv）通过连接数据库查询结果数据。我们在 TPC-H schema 下自动策划 50 条相对复杂的查询，结构较复杂（涉及 2–6 张表与复合谓词），总共执行超过 111.5 分钟。评测指标是工具学习能准确输出结果的查询比例。

为促进未来研究尝试，我们把上述所有工具实现并整合进 BMTools18，这是一个用工具扩展基础模型的开源仓库，也作为社区构建与共享工具的平台。有了 BMTools，用户可通过编写 Python 函数轻松构建新插件，也可整合来自其他来源（如 ChatGPT Plugins）的外部工具。

为基础模型构建工具库对连接基础模型与工具至关重要，我们很高兴看到该方向已有新兴工作。LangChain19 是第一个尝试把基础模型与工具链接起来的开源项目。在统一接口下，用户既可以构建自己的任务流水线，也可以让基础模型调用 API。最近，TaskMatrix.AI（Liang et al., 2023）与 HuggingGPT（Shen et al., 2023）把 API 与任务扩展到更广场景，包括视觉任务的多模态模型、本地软件与云服务 API。OpenAI 也提出了其官方工具库 ChatGPT Plugins20，用其他应用增强 ChatGPT。只需提供带描述的 API，ChatGPT 就能调用应用并完成更复杂任务。与第三方库不同，ChatGPT plugins 对安全风险谨慎，并为插件建立了严格标准。该库优先考虑最必要的工具，如网页浏览器、代码解释器与检索插件。

本页补完烹饪、电影、绘画、三维、化学、数据库，并介绍 BMTools / LangChain / ChatGPT Plugins 这类工具库。实验设定是 davinci-003 与 ChatGPT，零样本 vs 少样本。 工具库本身就是 harness：把函数注册进去，模型按说明书调用。 类比：先把十八般兵器挂上墙，再比谁会按说明书取用。

第 25 / 75 页
  • Zero-shot prompting provides the instruction to model about the task description, and information about the APIs in the tool. Some basic guidelines can also be added to the instruction.
  • Few-shot prompting additionally adds concrete tool-use examples as a hint of how to use the APIs given a user query. Providing examples is expected to improve the performance. Whenever feasible, we also compare the results with a baseline that does not involve the utilization of tools,i.e., No Tool. In such cases, we solely depend on the model’s internal knowledge to accomplish the given task (e.g., machine translation). Nonetheless, many tasks (e.g., slides-making) cannot be completed without the aid of tools. Consequently, we omit the “no tool” configuration in such cases. In the experiment of machine translator, calculator, search engine, Wikipedia, online shopping, and ALFWorld, we employ existing datasets for evaluation. However, for other tools, a suitable dataset for experiments does not exist. To address this issue, we adopt a methodology similar to that of Wang et al. (2022b), wherein we curate a set of user queries. Specifically, we manually write a few user queries as seed examples and use ChatGPT’s in-context learning ability to generate more instances. Then we manually filter those instances with low quality. We find empirically that the generated examples are diverse enough. Unless otherwise specified, for these manually curated test sets, we employ the trace of API calls as the metric for evaluating the models’ performance. Specifically, if humans judge that all the API calls are accurate for the given task, and they yield a reasonable result, the task is deemed to be correctly completed. The codes and our curated dataset will be made available to the academic community21. Results. We present the results in Table 1, from which we can conclude that: (1) In most cases, models can learn how to effectively use tools with simple prompting, and improve their task performances. (2) For the tasks that models can leverage their internal knowledge to solve (such as the cases of the calculator and search engine), utilizing tools with zero-shot prompting could sometimes lead to worse performance, which implies that sub-optimal utilization of tools may negatively impact performance. Nevertheless, incorporating tools with few-shot prompting still consistently yields superior performance than not incorporating tools. This underscores the concrete benefits that tools can bring to problem-solving, provided that they are employed effectively. (3) Additionally, comparing the performance of ChatGPT and text-davinci-003, we observe that although ChatGPT has been fine-tuned with RLHF, it does not yield better results than text-davinci-003. We attribute this to two reasons: firstly, the alignment tax issue mentioned in Ouyang et al. (2022), that is, the specific task skills and in-context learning ability are undermined during RLHF training; secondly, the model size of ChatGPT, though not officially stated, might be much smaller than text-davinci-003, thus making ChatGPT harder to handle complex scenarios. Regarding the performance of different tools, it is important to acknowledge that the evaluation setups of these tools are inherently different, making direct comparison difficult. However, limiting our comparison to solely those tools that employ manually curated test sets and examining the successful rate of API calls, we have observed that under the few-shot prompting setting, certain tools such as Map, Weather, Slides, Tables, Cooking Assistant, and AI Painting exhibit a satisfying completion rate. These tools are deemed to be less challenging than other tools. In fact, we find empirically that both ChatGPT and text-davinci-003 can utilize these tools proficiently despite not directly being fine-tuned on them. However, for several tools such as KGs, Wikipedia, online shopping, and 3D model construction, the model performance is still far from satisfactory even with few-shot prompting. The reason is perhaps that the usage of these tools cannot be easily learned with a few examples. For example, tools requiring the generation of executable code as the parameter to the API, such as the search_by_query API in the KGs tool (see Appendix A.9 for more details), are found to be significantly more arduous. This implies the necessity of training foundation models to use tools as mentioned in § 3.3. We provide the prompts and model responses of ChatGPT in Appendix A as case studies for all the tools. Discussion 5.1 Safe and Trustworthy Tool Learning Armed with external tools, AI systems can be unprecedentedly capable and human-like. With the ability to perceive, act, and make decisions, these models can potentially intervene and significantly influence human society. Although we are eager to witness how tool learning with foundation models will change our life, it is 21https://github.com/OpenBMB/BMTools
  • 零样本提示向模型提供关于任务描述以及工具中 API 信息的指令。指令中也可加入一些基本指南。
  • 少样本提示额外加入具体的工具使用例子，作为给定用户查询时如何使用 API 的提示。提供例子预期能改进表现。

在可行时，我们还与不涉及工具利用的基线比较，即「无工具」。在这种情况下，我们仅依赖模型的内部知识完成给定任务（如机器翻译）。不过，许多任务（如制作幻灯片）没有工具就无法完成。因此，在这种情况下我们省略「无工具」配置。

在机器翻译器、计算器、搜索引擎、维基百科、在线购物与 ALFWorld 的实验中，我们采用已有数据集做评测。然而，对其他工具，并不存在合适的实验数据集。为解决这一问题，我们采用与 Wang et al. (2022b) 类似的方法，策划一组用户查询。具体地，我们人工撰写若干用户查询作为种子例子，并用 ChatGPT 的上下文学习能力生成更多实例。然后我们人工过滤低质量实例。我们经验上发现生成的例子足够多样。除非另有说明，对这些人工策划的测试集，我们采用 API 调用轨迹作为评测模型表现的指标。具体地，若人类判断给定任务的所有 API 调用都准确，且产生合理结果，则该任务被视为正确完成。代码与我们策划的数据集将向学术界公开21。

结果。

我们在表 1 中呈现结果，从中可得出：（1）在大多数情况下，模型可以通过简单提示学会如何有效使用工具，并改进其任务表现。（2）对模型可利用内部知识求解的任务（如计算器与搜索引擎的情形），用零样本提示利用工具有时会导致更差表现，这意味着次优的工具利用可能负面影响表现。尽管如此，用少样本提示纳入工具仍持续产生优于不纳入工具的表现。这凸显了只要有效使用，工具能为问题解决带来的具体好处。（3）此外，比较 ChatGPT 与 text-davinci-003 的表现，我们观察到尽管 ChatGPT 经过 RLHF 微调，它并未产生优于 text-davinci-003 的结果。我们将其归因于两点：首先，Ouyang et al. (2022) 提到的对齐税问题，即特定任务技能与上下文学习能力在 RLHF 训练中被削弱；其次，ChatGPT 的模型规模虽未官方说明，可能远小于 text-davinci-003，从而使 ChatGPT 更难处理复杂场景。

关于不同工具的表现，必须承认这些工具的评测设定本质上不同，使直接比较困难。然而，若仅限于那些采用人工策划测试集的工具，并考察 API 调用的成功率，我们观察到在少样本提示设定下，地图、天气、幻灯片、表格、烹饪助手与 AI 绘画等某些工具展现出令人满意的完成率。这些工具被认为比其他工具挑战更小。事实上，我们经验上发现 ChatGPT 与 text-davinci-003 都能熟练利用这些工具，尽管并未直接在其上微调。

然而，对知识图谱、维基百科、在线购物与三维模型构建等若干工具，即便有少样本提示，模型表现仍远不能令人满意。原因或许是这些工具的用法不能轻易用几个例子学会。例如，需要生成可执行代码作为 API 参数的工具，如知识图谱工具中的 search_by_query API（更多细节见附录 A.9），被发现显著更费力。这暗示了如 § 3.3 所述训练基础模型使用工具的必要性。我们在附录 A 中提供 ChatGPT 对所有工具的提示与模型回复作为案例研究。

讨论

5.1 安全可信的工具学习

配备外部工具后，AI 系统可以前所未有地有能力、类人。有了感知、行动与决策的能力，这些模型可能干预并显著影响人类社会。尽管我们渴望见证基础模型的工具学习将如何改变我们的生活，但

第 26 / 75 页

5.1 Safe and Trustworthy Tool Learning paramount to take a step back and contemplate the underlying risks. For responsible AI research, here we discuss the safety and trustworthiness problems of tool learning. Adversaries. Same as all the other AI systems, we could foresee that there will be external adversaries once the tool learning models are deployed in reality, and thus how to defend against these threats is of great significance (Szegedy et al., 2014; Wallace et al., 2019; Jin et al., 2020; Hendrycks et al., 2021). Recent works suggest that large foundation models like ChatGPT are more robust on hard and adversarial examples (Taori et al., 2020; Wang et al., 2023a), which improves their utility in the complicated real world. But the attempt of crafting misleading or even harmful queries will undoubtfully persist as well (Perez & Ribeiro, 2022). Moreover, due to training on massive web data, foundation models are faced with long-lasting training-time security issues in deep learning, such as backdoor attacks (Kurita et al., 2020; Cui et al., 2022) and data poisoning attacks (Wallace et al., 2021). In addition to foundation models, the incorporated tools could be new attack targets for adversaries. For example, the attackers could maliciously modify the manual documentation or even the tools themselves (e.g. attacking a news API to give biased reports) to mislead the model into erroneous outcomes. The key challenge lies in the interplay between foundation models and tools, since a safe and robust system requires the models to not only learn to use tools, but also possess the ability to scrutinize, rectify, and secure them. Currently, most research endeavors aimed at defending against external adversaries focus solely on ensuring the model safety. Nonetheless, in light of the everchanging paradigm shift, safety research must also attend to tools to protect the entire system. Governance. There is long-standing worry about the misuse of AI, especially the powerful foundation models (Bommasani et al., 2021). Under the paradigm of tool learning, governance over foundation models is more urgently needed. The pertinent question at hand is which tools should be involved? In § 4, we list a bunch of tools that may empower foundation models to solve complicated tasks. However, given the countless tools human beings have manufactured, we must consider if it is appropriate to allow models to master all of them. Certain tools, such as calculators and translators, may be deemed safe as they do not pose any harm to individuals. However, granting models access to the internet or permitting them to make decisions in the real world could be perilous, as they could cause negative or even dangerous influences such as disseminating falsehoods (Zellers et al., 2019) and harming human lives. In this regard, research communities and companies need to deliberate carefully before permitting machines to master a certain tool. Apart from potentially engaged harmful tools, governance over tool usage is also a pertinent issue. As highlighted by Amodei et al. (2016), the end-to-end training paradigm in deep learning does not regulate how models achieve their objectives. Fortunately, such goal-oriented approaches did not result in catastrophic consequences due to the capability limitation of task-specific models, but it warrants serious consideration moving forward. Foundation models are not only expected to finish tasks with the help of tools but also should follow the regulations and constraints of tool usage. Trustworthiness. The goal of tool learning lies in creating advanced intelligent agents. However, determining whether these agents are trustworthy or not is a complex challenge. Even though tool learning delivers enhanced interpretability and robustness, the core foundation models are still considered “black boxes”. Recent research (Chen et al., 2022b) shows that although large models achieve better performance, they are unable to predict when they will be wrong, rendering the calibration problem unresolved yet. Accompanied with tools, under what circumstances will the model call on the tools is unpredictable as well. Therefore, before we apply these models to high-stake scenarios such as autonomous driving (Milakis et al., 2017) and clinical trials (Matheny et al., 2019), it is essential to thoroughly discuss to what extent should we allow AI to engage in human lives. Moreover, the morality of foundation models has emerged as a contentious issue in recent times. Despite OpenAI’s commendable efforts to imbue InstructGPT (Ouyang et al., 2022) and GPT-4 (OpenAI, 2023) with human values and preferences, given the discomforting “jailbreak” responses by ChatGPT (Borji, 2023) and New Bing (Roose, 2023), whether these big models will be mild and compliant remains doubtful. Ironically, the very discourse that once centered around the potential recklessness of autonomous robots is now mirrored in the development of large language models, thereby fueling a self-fulfilling prophecy that further exacerbates the already frayed trustworthiness of these systems. When models could learn actively from the world via tools, the challenge of controlling their actions will become more daunting than ever before.

5.1 安全可信的工具学习

至关重要的是退后一步，思考底层风险。为负责任的 AI 研究，此处我们讨论工具学习的安全与可信问题。

对手。

与所有其他 AI 系统一样，我们可以预见一旦工具学习模型在现实中部署，就会有外部对手，因而如何防御这些威胁意义重大（Szegedy et al., 2014; Wallace et al., 2019; Jin et al., 2020; Hendrycks et al., 2021）。近期工作表明，ChatGPT 等大基础模型在困难与对抗例子上更鲁棒（Taori et al., 2020; Wang et al., 2023a），这提升了它们在复杂现实世界中的效用。但精心制作误导甚至有害查询的企图无疑也将持续存在（Perez & Ribeiro, 2022）。此外，由于在海量网页数据上训练，基础模型面临深度学习中长期存在的训练时安全问题，如后门攻击（Kurita et al., 2020; Cui et al., 2022）与数据投毒攻击（Wallace et al., 2021）。

除基础模型外，被纳入的工具也可能成为对手的新攻击目标。例如，攻击者可能恶意修改手册文档甚至工具本身（如攻击新闻 API 以给出有偏报道），从而误导模型走向错误结果。关键挑战在于基础模型与工具之间的相互作用，因为一个安全鲁棒的系统要求模型不仅学会使用工具，还要具备审视、纠正并保护它们的能力。目前，旨在防御外部对手的大多数研究努力只聚焦于确保模型安全。然而，鉴于不断变化的范式转变，安全研究也必须关注工具以保护整个系统。

治理。

对 AI 滥用、尤其是强大基础模型的担忧由来已久（Bommasani et al., 2021）。在工具学习范式下，对基础模型的治理更为迫切。手头的相关问题是：应纳入哪些工具？在 § 4 中，我们列出一串可能增强基础模型以解决复杂任务的工具。然而，鉴于人类制造的工具不计其数，我们必须考虑是否适合允许模型掌握所有这些工具。某些工具，如计算器与翻译器，可能被视为安全，因为它们不会对个人造成伤害。然而，授予模型互联网访问或允许它们在真实世界中做决策可能是危险的，因为它们可能造成负面甚至危险的影响，如传播虚假信息（Zellers et al., 2019）与伤害人类生命。就此而言，研究社区与公司在允许机器掌握某种工具之前需要仔细斟酌。

除可能参与的有害工具外，对工具使用的治理也是相关问题。如 Amodei et al. (2016) 所强调，深度学习中的端到端训练范式并不规范模型如何达成其目标。幸运的是，由于任务特定模型的能力限制，这种目标导向方法并未导致灾难性后果，但向前推进时值得认真考虑。基础模型不仅被期望在工具帮助下完成任务，还应遵循工具使用的规章与约束。

可信性。

工具学习的目标在于创造先进的智能体。然而，判定这些智能体是否可信是一个复杂挑战。尽管工具学习带来增强的可解释性与鲁棒性，核心基础模型仍被视为「黑箱」。近期研究（Chen et al., 2022b）表明，尽管大模型取得更好表现，它们无法预测自己何时会错，校准问题尚未解决。伴随工具，模型在何种情形下会调用工具同样不可预测。因此，在把这些模型应用于自动驾驶（Milakis et al., 2017）与临床试验（Matheny et al., 2019）等高风险场景之前，必须充分讨论我们应在多大程度上允许 AI 介入人类生活。

此外，基础模型的道德近来已成为有争议的问题。尽管 OpenAI 为使 InstructGPT（Ouyang et al., 2022）与 GPT-4（OpenAI, 2023）充满人类价值与偏好做出了值得称赞的努力，鉴于 ChatGPT（Borji, 2023）与 New Bing（Roose, 2023）令人不适的「越狱」回复，这些大模型是否会温和、顺从仍存疑。具有讽刺意味的是，曾经围绕自主机器人潜在鲁莽的话语，如今在大语言模型的发展中得到镜像，从而助长一种自我实现的预言，进一步加剧这些系统本已受损的可信性。当模型能通过工具从世界主动学习时，控制其行动的挑战将比以往任何时候都更艰巨。

白话解读

本页讨论安全：对手会改模型也会改工具手册；治理要问「哪些工具不该给」；可信性则说黑箱加上不可预测的调用时机。 对 harness 这是权限与沙箱问题，不是附录里的一句警告。 类比：把钥匙串交给助手之前，先想清楚哪些门不能开，以及说明书会不会被换成假的。

5.2 Tool Learning for Large Complex Systems 5.2 Tool Learning for Large Complex Systems Different from tools with limited functionality, large complex systems (e.g., relational databases (Zhou et al., 2020), manufacturing execution systems Kletti (2007), supply chain management systems Misra et al. (2010)) are composed of numerous components (e.g., over 500 knobs that control different functions in relational databases (Li et al., 2019)). This complexity results in much more flexible and complicated interaction and management approaches. Consequently, there are three main challenges in applying tool learning in large complex systems. System Learning. Acquiring and comprehending the knowledge and functions of a large complex system is a labor-intensive task for human beings. Similarly, existing foundational models face challenges in memorizing and mastering the relevant skills associated with such systems. For instance, relational databases involve numerous built-in functions (e.g., selecting, inserting, updating, and managing user data), complicated query syntax (e.g., ORM code, SQL queries), and various maintenance problems during execution (e.g., connection anomalies, workload contention, resource problems). To address this issue, we first need to fine-tune foundation models with textual materials or even source code that describe the components, functions, execution mechanisms, and even anomaly cases of the system. This process deepens the models’ comprehension of the intricacies inherent in the system. Next, we must carefully design prompts to assist foundation models to become proficient in using specific functions (e.g., determining the calling order) and effectively manipulating this system. Finally, existing foundation models may occasionally generate erroneous or unintended actions. We can augment these models with a checking layer (e.g., SMT solver), which ensures accurate generation of function calls by the foundation model and derives both legal and reasonable interactions with the tools. Efficiency Requirements. In many scenarios, the efficiency of tool learning with foundation models is a critical metric when implementing inside real systems. For example, in real-time scenarios (e.g., fraud detection (Wang, 2010)), users expect fast and accurate responses within milliseconds. However, existing foundation models take a relatively long time to reason and plan how to call the functions, which is intolerable in practice. Thus, it is crucial to find efficient ways to speed up the response of tool learning while maintaining high performance. First, we can filter out irrelevant, redundant, or low-quality input information before sending it to the foundation model. Second, we can add hints in zero-prompting to enforce the foundation model to jump over redundant function calls and reasoning (e.g., ending the process right away after obtaining a good enough solution). Third, we can cache the relevant knowledge in high-speed storage (e.g., in-memory vector database) and the foundation models only retrieve the relevant knowledge to augment the reasoning procedure when necessary (e.g., the disk status information for system diagnosis). Privacy Concerns. When training the foundation model for tool learning, it requires much user behavior data to simulate the thinking, planning, and decision-making processes. However, in modern systems, a significant portion of user data is of high sensitivity, which necessitates strict privacy and security regulations to ensure responsible data utilization. To address the privacy concerns, two possible solutions are federated learning and model distillation. First, federated learning enables the training of foundation models while preserving data privacy by allowing users to update model parameters using relevant data on their local machines (see § 5.4), which can be particularly useful in industry-specific scenarios such as scheduling high-concurrency transactions in the banking sector. Furthermore, model distillation involves the process of compressing the original foundational model into a smaller model that exhibits excellent performance on comparable tasks. By employing this approach, vendors can effectively retain both the model itself and user data within their systems, thereby ensuring efficient knowledge acquisition and data privacy. 5.3 From Tool User to Tool Maker: AI’s Evolutionary Role Throughout the annals of human civilization, the evolution of tools has occupied a pivotal position (Mithen, 1996; Ko, 2016). The Stone Age, in particular, witnessed the emergence of stone-based weaponry and hunting tools, which afforded humans a competitive edge over their animal counterparts. Subsequent epochs of human history were equally marked by significant societal transformations made possible by the introduction of novel tools. Notably, the invention of the steam engine heralded the onset of the first industrial revolution, while the widespread utilization of electricity catalyzed the second industrial revolution. The progression of human civilization is inextricably intertwined with the evolution of tools, and the relentless pursuit of innovative tool creation constitutes a vital aspect of human ingenuity.

5.2 面向大型复杂系统的工具学习

5.2 面向大型复杂系统的工具学习

与功能有限的工具不同，大型复杂系统（如关系数据库（Zhou et al., 2020）、制造执行系统 Kletti (2007)、供应链管理系统 Misra et al. (2010)）由众多组件组成（例如关系数据库中控制不同功能的 500 多个旋钮（Li et al., 2019））。这种复杂性导致交互与管理方式灵活得多、复杂得多。因此，在大型复杂系统中应用工具学习有三个主要挑战。

系统学习。

对人类而言，获取并理解大型复杂系统的知识与功能是费力的任务。类似地，现有基础模型在记忆并掌握与此类系统相关的技能上面临挑战。例如，关系数据库涉及众多内置函数（如选择、插入、更新与管理用户数据）、复杂查询语法（如 ORM 代码、SQL 查询），以及执行期间的各种维护问题（如连接异常、工作负载争用、资源问题）。为解决这一问题，我们首先需要用描述系统组件、功能、执行机制甚至异常案例的文本材料乃至源代码来微调基础模型。这一过程加深模型对系统内在复杂性的理解。其次，我们必须仔细设计提示，以帮助基础模型精通使用特定功能（如确定调用顺序）并有效操纵该系统。最后，现有基础模型偶尔可能生成错误或非预期动作。我们可以用检查层（如 SMT 求解器）增强这些模型，以确保基础模型准确生成函数调用，并导出与工具既合法又合理的交互。

效率要求。

在许多场景中，当在真实系统内部实现时，基础模型工具学习的效率是关键指标。例如，在实时场景（如欺诈检测（Wang, 2010））中，用户期望在毫秒级内得到快速准确的响应。然而，现有基础模型在推理并规划如何调用函数上花费相对较长时间，这在实践中不可忍受。因此，找到在保持高性能的同时加速工具学习响应的有效方法至关重要。首先，我们可以在把输入发送给基础模型之前过滤掉无关、冗余或低质量的输入信息。其次，我们可以在零样本提示中加入提示，强制基础模型跳过冗余的函数调用与推理（例如在获得足够好的解后立即结束过程）。第三，我们可以把相关知识缓存在高速存储中（如内存向量数据库），基础模型仅在必要时检索相关知识以增强推理过程（如系统诊断的磁盘状态信息）。

隐私关切。

为工具学习训练基础模型时，需要大量用户行为数据来模拟思考、规划与决策过程。然而，在现代系统中，相当一部分用户数据高度敏感，需要严格的隐私与安全规章以确保负责任的数据利用。为应对隐私关切，两种可能方案是联邦学习与模型蒸馏。首先，联邦学习通过允许用户在本地机器上用相关数据更新模型参数，在保护数据隐私的同时训练基础模型（见 § 5.4），这在银行业调度高并发事务等行业特定场景中可能特别有用。此外，模型蒸馏涉及把原始基础模型压缩为在可比任务上表现优异的较小模型的过程。通过采用该方法，供应商可以有效地把模型本身与用户数据保留在其系统内，从而确保高效的知识获取与数据隐私。

5.3 从工具使用者到工具制造者：AI 的演化角色

纵观人类文明史，工具的演化占据了关键位置（Mithen, 1996; Ko, 2016）。尤其是石器时代见证了基于石头的武器与狩猎工具的出现，使人类相对其动物对手获得竞争优势。人类历史的后续时代同样以新颖工具引入所使可能的重大社会转型为标志。值得注意的是，蒸汽机的发明预示了第一次工业革命的到来，而电力的广泛利用催化了第二次工业革命。人类文明的进程与工具的演化密不可分，对创新工具创造的不懈追求构成人类才智的重要方面。

白话解读

本页把工具学习放到数据库、MES 这类大系统里：要先读懂系统、还要毫秒级响应、还不能泄露用户数据。下半页开始「从用工具到造工具」。 大系统不是再多一个 API，而是几百个旋钮和严格时延。 类比：会用计算器是一回事，去开一家工厂的中控台是另一回事。
