5.3 From Tool User to Tool Maker: AI’s Evolutionary Role Prompt: You have access to a function in Python: forecast_weather(city, N), which will return the weather condition of a city after N days. The returned information is only about a single day and is a dictionary containing keys including temperature. Please wrap it into a new function, that can return the average temperature over the next N days in a city. Response: Certainly! Here’s an example code to wrap the forecast_weather function into a new function that calculates the average temperature of the next N days in a given city: def average_temperature(city, N): total_temperature = 0 for i in range(N): weather_data = forecast_weather(city, i) temperature = weather_data['temperature'] total_temperature += temperature return total_temperature / N In this code, we deﬁne a new function called average_temperature that takes in a city and N as input parameters. Inside the function, we loop over the range of N days, calling the forecast_weather function for each day and extracting the temperature data from the returned dictionary. We then add the daily temperature to a running total. Finally, we calculate the average temperature by dividing the total temperature by N and return it. Note that this code assumes that the forecast_weather function returns a dictionary with a key temperature that contains the temperature for a given day. If the actual function returns a different format, you may need to adjust the code accordingly. Figure 7: Example of AI tool creation, where we ask ChatGPT to encapsulate a weather forecast API into a new function suited for a specific target. Human beings are the creators and users of almost all tools from the Stone Age to the 21st century. Although we take it as granted, things are different when foundation models are involved. Considering that they have proven tool-use capabilities to certain extents, it is also possible to put them into the lifecycle of tool creation. Tools for AI. Humans create tools to satisfy our own needs, so the designation naturally suits human preference and convenience. However, current tool learning algorithms may not be optimal or efficient for models. This is because most tools (e.g., search engines) are specifically designed for human use, and models process information in a different way. Therefore, it is necessary to create tools that are specifically suited for models. Possible solutions may include: (1) modularity, which decomposes tools into smaller, more modular units, making them more adaptable and flexible for AI models. In this regard, models can learn to use these components in a more fine-grained and compositional manner; (2) new input and output formats: developing new input and output formats that are specifically tailored to the needs of AI models can improve their interaction and utilization of tools, enabling more seamless integration and communication between models and tools. Tools by AI. The creation and utilization of tools have traditionally been considered exclusive to human intelligence. However, with the emergence of foundation models, this notion is being challenged. Increasing evidence indicates that the ability to create advanced tools is no longer limited to human beings. For instance, large code models (Chen et al., 2021) can generate executable programs based on language description. These programs can be deemed as tools to help accomplish specific tasks. ChatGPT plugins22 present an awesome example about asking GPT-4 to write a TODO plugin and integrate it with ChatGPT. Besides writing codes from scratch, foundation models can also encapsulate existing tools into stronger tools. In Figure 7, we show an example of ChatGPT encapsulating a weather forecast API into a new function that calculates the average temperature. All such evidence implies the potential for foundation models to transition from merely tool users to tool makers. Creativity of AI. Beyond the coding ability, other emergent abilities (Wei et al., 2022b) also shed light on the possibility of more advanced tool creation. However, whether foundation models can exhibit genuine creativity 22https://openai.com/blog/chatgpt-plugins

5.3 从工具使用者到工具制造者：AI 的演化角色

提示：你可以使用一个 Python 函数：forecast_weather(city, N)，它将返回某城市 N 天后的天气状况。返回信息只关于单日，是一个包含 temperature 等键的字典。请把它封装成一个新函数，该函数可以返回某城市未来 N 天的平均温度。

回复：Certainly! Here's an example code to wrap the forecast_weather function into a new function that calculates the average temperature of the next N days in a given city:

def average_temperature(city, N):

total_temperature = 0

for i in range(N):

weather_data = forecast_weather(city, i)

temperature = weather_data['temperature']

total_temperature += temperature

return total_temperature / N

In this code, we define a new function called average_temperature that takes in a city and N as input parameters. Inside the function, we loop over the range of N days, calling the forecast_weather function for each day and extracting the temperature data from the returned dictionary. We then add the daily temperature to a running total. Finally, we calculate the average temperature by dividing the total temperature by N and return it.

Note that this code assumes that the forecast_weather function returns a dictionary with a key temperature that contains the temperature for a given day. If the actual function returns a different format, you may need to adjust the code accordingly.

图 7：AI 工具创造示例：我们请 ChatGPT 把天气预报 API 封装成适合特定目标的新函数。

从石器时代到 21 世纪，人类几乎是所有工具的创造者与使用者。尽管我们视之为理所当然，但当基础模型介入时情况就不同了。考虑到它们已在一定程度上证明了工具使用能力，也可能把它们放入工具创造的生命周期。

为 AI 准备的工具。

人类创造工具是为满足自身需求，因而设计自然适合人类偏好与便利。然而，当前的工具学习算法对模型而言可能并非最优或高效。这是因为大多数工具（如搜索引擎）是专门为人类使用设计的，而模型以不同方式处理信息。因此，有必要创造专门适合模型的工具。可能方案包括：（1）模块化，把工具分解为更小、更模块化的单元，使其对 AI 模型更适应、更灵活。就此而言，模型可以以更细粒度、更可组合的方式学会使用这些组件；（2）新的输入与输出格式：开发专门为 AI 模型需求量身定制的新输入输出格式，可以改进它们与工具的交互与利用，使模型与工具之间更无缝的整合与通信成为可能。

由 AI 创造的工具。

工具的创造与利用传统上被认为是人类智能所独有。然而，随着基础模型的出现，这一观念正受到挑战。越来越多的证据表明，创造先进工具的能力不再限于人类。例如，大型代码模型（Chen et al., 2021）可以根据语言描述生成可执行程序。这些程序可被视为帮助完成特定任务的工具。ChatGPT plugins22 展示了一个精彩例子：请 GPT-4 写一个 TODO 插件并把它与 ChatGPT 整合。除从零写代码外，基础模型也可以把已有工具封装成更强的工具。在图 7 中，我们展示 ChatGPT 把天气预报 API 封装成计算平均温度的新函数的例子。所有这些证据都暗示基础模型有潜力从仅仅是工具使用者过渡到工具制造者。

AI 的创造力。

除编码能力外，其他涌现能力（Wei et al., 2022b）也为更先进的工具创造提供了线索。然而，基础模型能否在创造新颖工具上展现真正的创造力

5.4 From General Intelligence to Personalized Intelligence in creating novel tools remains an open problem. This issue is important because the capacity for novel tool creation is a defining characteristic that distinguishes humans from animals (Ambrose, 2010). Understanding the extent of creativity, beyond simply memorizing, composing, and interpolating between human tools encountered during pre-training, is crucial for assessing their potential to contribute to the development of new tools. Such investigations may involve the development of novel evaluation metrics and benchmarks (Liang et al., 2022b), as well as the exploration of new techniques that prioritize creative problem-solving. In the future, we possess the wildest imagination that AI could create brand-new tools, such as a new language and rocket architecture. 5.4 From General Intelligence to Personalized Intelligence Foundation models are typically trained on a generic domain and calibrated with broadly-defined human preferences that prioritize helpfulness and harmlessness (Ouyang et al., 2022; Nakano et al., 2021). As a result, they struggle to process personal information and provide personalized assistance to users with varying needs for tool learning. For example, when a user seeks advice on managing their finances, to provide helpful and relevant suggestions, models should first gain access to the user’s personalized data, such as income, expenses, and investment history, via financial tools. Subsequently, models may look for recent investment trends and relevant news through a search engine. By utilizing personalized information, models can provide more customized advice and offer a more tailored approach to financial management. User-centric and personalized natural language generation has received increasing attention in recent years (Yang & Flek, 2021; Kirk et al., 2023). Existing works cover a wide range of tasks, such as dialogue generation (Madotto et al., 2019; Mazaré et al., 2018; Song et al., 2021; Zhong et al., 2022), machine translation (Mirkin & Meunier, 2015; Michel & Neubig, 2018; Wuebker et al., 2018), and summarization (Yan et al., 2011). These methods utilize external user-specific modules, such as user embeddings and user memory modules (Zhang et al., 2018; Wu et al., 2021), to inject preferences, writing styles, and personal information of different users into the generated content. However, these works are often designed for specific tasks and experimented with limited user information. How to integrate user information into general-purpose tool learning models is still under-explored. We will discuss the key challenge of personalized tool learning in the following. Aligning User Preference with Tool Manipulation. Personalized tool learning emphasizes the importance of considering user-specific information in tool manipulation. There are two main challenges: (1) heterogeneous user information modeling: in real-world scenarios, personal information can come from numerous heterogeneous sources. For instance, when using an email tool, models need to consider the user’s language style from historical conversation records and gather relevant information from the user’s social networks. Other information, such as browsing history, purchase records, and behavioral data from daily life, can also reflect users’ personal preferences. This requires modelling user information with diverse structures into a unified semantic space, allowing models to utilize this information jointly; (2) personalized tool planning: different users tend to have different preferences for tool planning and selection. For example, when completing the purchasing task, different users prefer to use different online shopping platforms. Similarly, when completing writing tasks, some users prefer to first search for sufficient references before writing, while others prefer to search for information while writing. Therefore, the models need to develop personalized tool execution plans based on user preferences; (3) personalized tool call: adaptively calling tools according to the user’s preference is also an important direction in personalized tool learning. Most tools are designed without consideration of personalized information, which requires the model to generate different inputs for tools based on the user’s preferences. Taking the example of purchasing goods, different users have different preferences for the brand of the products. In this case, the model needs to input the user’s preferred brand into the purchasing tool to determine the product that needs to be purchased. From Reactive Systems to Proactive Systems. Currently, most of the foundation models are designed as reactive systems, which respond to user queries without initiating any actions on their own. A paradigm shift is underway toward proactive systems that can take action on behalf of the user. This shift presents both opportunities and challenges for tool learning. By leveraging the history of user interactions, proactive systems can continually improve their performance and tailor their responses to specific users, which provides a more personalized and seamless user experience. However, the introduction of proactive systems also raises several concerns regarding their safety and ethical implications. Proactive systems can initiate actions that have unintended consequences, particularly in complex and dynamic environments. This can lead to cascading failures, whereby the behavior of one assistant affects others, creating a chain reaction that is difficult to control

5.4 从通用智能到个性化智能

仍是一个开放问题。这一问题很重要，因为创造新颖工具的能力是区分人与动物的决定性特征（Ambrose, 2010）。理解创造力的程度——超越仅仅记忆、组合并在预训练中遇到的人类工具之间插值——对评估它们为新工具发展做贡献的潜力至关重要。此类研究可能涉及新颖评测指标与基准的发展（Liang et al., 2022b），以及优先考虑创造性问题解决的新技术探索。在未来，我们拥有最狂野的想象：AI 可以创造全新的工具，如一种新语言与火箭架构。

5.4 从通用智能到个性化智能

基础模型通常在通用领域上训练，并用优先考虑有用与无害的广义人类偏好校准（Ouyang et al., 2022; Nakano et al., 2021）。因此，它们难以处理个人信息，并为对工具学习有不同需求的用户提供个性化协助。例如，当用户寻求理财建议时，为提供有帮助、相关的建议，模型应首先通过金融工具获取用户的个性化数据，如收入、支出与投资历史。随后，模型可通过搜索引擎查找近期投资趋势与相关新闻。通过利用个性化信息，模型可以提供更定制的建议，并为财务管理提供更量身的方法。

以用户为中心的个性化自然语言生成近年来受到越来越多关注（Yang & Flek, 2021; Kirk et al., 2023）。现有工作覆盖广泛任务，如对话生成（Madotto et al., 2019; Mazaré et al., 2018; Song et al., 2021; Zhong et al., 2022）、机器翻译（Mirkin & Meunier, 2015; Michel & Neubig, 2018; Wuebker et al., 2018）与摘要（Yan et al., 2011）。这些方法利用外部用户特定模块，如用户嵌入与用户记忆模块（Zhang et al., 2018; Wu et al., 2021），把不同用户的偏好、写作风格与个人信息注入生成内容。然而，这些工作往往为特定任务设计，并在有限用户信息上实验。如何把用户信息整合进通用工具学习模型仍探索不足。下面我们讨论个性化工具学习的关键挑战。

将用户偏好与工具操纵对齐。

个性化工具学习强调在工具操纵中考虑用户特定信息的重要性。有两个主要挑战：（1）异构用户信息建模：在现实场景中，个人信息可能来自众多异构来源。例如，使用邮件工具时，模型需要从历史对话记录中考虑用户的语言风格，并从用户的社交网络中收集相关信息。浏览历史、购买记录与日常生活中的行为数据等其他信息也可以反映用户的个人偏好。这要求把具有多样结构的用户信息建模到统一语义空间，使模型能联合利用这些信息；（2）个性化工具规划：不同用户往往对工具规划与选择有不同偏好。例如，完成购买任务时，不同用户偏好使用不同的在线购物平台。类似地，完成写作任务时，有些用户偏好先搜索充分参考文献再写，而另一些偏好边写边搜。因此，模型需要基于用户偏好制定个性化的工具执行计划；（3）个性化工具调用：根据用户偏好自适应调用工具也是个性化工具学习的重要方向。大多数工具在设计时并未考虑个性化信息，这要求模型基于用户偏好为工具生成不同输入。以购买商品为例，不同用户对产品品牌有不同偏好。在这种情况下，模型需要把用户偏好的品牌输入购买工具，以确定需要购买的产品。

从反应式系统到主动式系统。

目前，大多数基础模型被设计为反应式系统，回应用户查询而不自行发起任何动作。一场朝向能代表用户采取行动的主动式系统的范式转变正在进行。这一转变对工具学习既带来机遇也带来挑战。通过利用用户交互历史，主动式系统可以持续改进其表现并针对特定用户量身回复，从而提供更个性化、更无缝的用户体验。然而，引入主动式系统也引发若干关于其安全与伦理意涵的关切。主动式系统可能发起具有非预期后果的动作，尤其是在复杂动态环境中。这可能导致级联失败：一个助手的行为影响其他助手，造成难以控制的连锁反应

白话解读

本页从「造新工具算不算创造」转到个性化：模型要读懂你的账本、购物偏好和写作习惯，还可能从等你问变成主动替你做。 主动系统一出错会连锁——这和长跑 harness 里「自作主张改脚手架」是同一类风险。 类比：私人秘书不能只用公司模板回信，还得知道你爱哪家店、会不会自作主张下单。

5.5 Tool Learning and Embodied Learning or stop. This highlights the importance of designing proactive systems with safety in mind and incorporating fail-safe mechanisms to prevent catastrophic outcomes. To address these risks and challenges, proactive systems should be designed with the ability to identify and mitigate potential risks, as well as the flexibility to adapt and respond to unexpected situations. Privacy Preserving Technologies. Personalized tool learning requires models to learn user preferences from private user information, which inevitably raises privacy-preserving concerns. On the one hand, previous work has shown that training data extraction attacks can be applied to recover sensitive personal privacy from foundation models (Carlini et al., 2021), which is a critical challenge for personalized tool learning. On the other hand, models with high computational costs must be deployed on cloud servers, which require uploading private data to the cloud to enable personalized responses. It is crucial to develop secure and trustworthy mechanisms to access and process user data while protecting user privacy. Addressing these challenges will help unlock the potential of personalized tool learning, enabling more effective and tailored tool manipulation to meet individual user needs. To this end, it is worth exploring model-oriented distributed computing frameworks, such as edge computing and federated learning, in which cloud servers are responsible for hosting computationally intensive models, while edge devices like PCs or smartphones process personalized information to prevent its leakage. 5.5 Tool Learning and Embodied Learning The fundamental framework for tool learning entails a sequence of action and observation, where the model can perceive changes in the environment, aligning with the fundamental concept of embodied learning (Duan et al., 2022). This section delves into the interplay between tool learning and embodied learning, elucidating their similarities, differences, and potential for intersection. Embodied learning posits that genuine intelligence can be acquired through interaction with the environment (Smith & Gasser, 2005). The embodiment of the agent in virtual simulation environments has been the primary focus of embodied learning research. Simulation environments provide agents with multi-modal feedback, predominantly visual feedback, which facilitates action execution within the environment’s dynamics. Different kinds of embodied environments have been proposed to facilitate the research. Some environments allow for simple object placement (Puig et al., 2018), while others support more advanced physical simulation, such as collision (Gan et al., 2020). Tasks typically assigned to agents include exploration (Ramakrishnan et al., 2021), navigation (Ye et al., 2021), question answering (Yu et al., 2019) within the simulated environment, or more interactive embodied task (Abramson et al., 2022) based on human instructions. While embodied learning emphasizes the use of physical interactions within a simulated environment, tool learning is not limited to a specific environment, but rather focuses on using interfaces that extend the language model’s capabilities. The intersection between these two paradigms could lead to the development of more advanced AI models capable of learning and adapting in complex and dynamic environments. Here we discuss two possible directions. Tool Learning Enables Digital Embodiment. Tool learning broadens the scope of embodied learning research. At the core of embodied learning lies the dynamic interaction between an agent and its environment. In this sense, the model interacts with the world through tools. Even though the model might lack a physical body, it can also be seen as a kind of embodiment. We dub this form of embodiment as digital embodiment. To fully comprehend the concept of digital embodiment, one could envisage an agent utilizing various APIs to navigate the web, searching for relevant and up-to-date information, and constructing a personalized knowledge base. In addition, under strict safety constraints, the agent could interact with other agents using tools such as email interfaces, thereby facilitating communication and collaboration in a secure and controlled manner. This approach enables agents to exhibit a degree of autonomy and flexibility that is akin to human-like behavior. Digital embodiment serves as a testbed for the intelligent behaviors of agents. Firstly, digital embodiment presents a more accessible and practical approach to embodied learning compared to simulated environments. The ease of deployment and usage of digital embodiment makes it an attractive option for researchers investigating intelligent agent behaviors. Secondly, it is noteworthy that the challenges posed in digital embodiment tend to revolve around the increased emphasis on language-based inputs. Consequently, this necessitates agents to perform more advanced reasoning and decision-making operations, thereby promoting the development of higher-level cognitive skills. Thirdly, digital embodiment exhibits remarkable scalability, owing to the relative ease with which digital tools can be developed compared to the creation of additional

5.5 工具学习与具身学习

或停止。这凸显了在设计主动式系统时把安全放在心上、并纳入故障安全机制以防止灾难性结果的重要性。为应对这些风险与挑战，主动式系统应被设计为有能力识别并缓解潜在风险，以及有灵活性适应并回应意外情境。

隐私保护技术。

个性化工具学习要求模型从私有用户信息中学习用户偏好，这不可避免地引发隐私保护关切。一方面，先前工作表明，训练数据提取攻击可被应用于从基础模型中恢复敏感个人隐私（Carlini et al., 2021），这对个性化工具学习是关键挑战。另一方面，计算成本高的模型必须部署在云服务器上，这要求把私有数据上传到云端以实现个性化回复。开发安全可信的机制来访问并处理用户数据、同时保护用户隐私至关重要。应对这些挑战将帮助解锁个性化工具学习的潜力，使更有效、更量身的工具操纵成为可能以满足个体用户需求。为此，值得探索面向模型的分布式计算框架，如边缘计算与联邦学习：云服务器负责托管计算密集的模型，而 PC 或智能手机等边缘设备处理个性化信息以防止其泄露。

5.5 工具学习与具身学习

工具学习的基本框架包含动作与观察的序列，模型可以感知环境变化，这与具身学习的基本概念一致（Duan et al., 2022）。本节深入工具学习与具身学习的相互作用，阐明其相似、差异与交叉潜力。

具身学习主张真正的智能可以通过与环境的交互获得（Smith & Gasser, 2005）。智能体在虚拟仿真环境中的具身一直是具身学习研究的主要焦点。仿真环境为智能体提供多模态反馈，主要是视觉反馈，这促进在环境动力学内执行动作。已提出不同类型的具身环境以促进研究。有些环境允许简单的物体放置（Puig et al., 2018），另一些支持更先进的物理仿真，如碰撞（Gan et al., 2020）。通常分配给智能体的任务包括在仿真环境中探索（Ramakrishnan et al., 2021）、导航（Ye et al., 2021）、问答（Yu et al., 2019），或基于人类指令的更交互具身任务（Abramson et al., 2022）。

尽管具身学习强调在仿真环境内使用物理交互，工具学习并不限于特定环境，而聚焦于使用扩展语言模型能力的接口。这两种范式的交叉可能导致能够在复杂动态环境中学习并适应的更先进 AI 模型的发展。此处我们讨论两个可能方向。

工具学习使数字具身成为可能。

工具学习拓宽了具身学习研究的范围。具身学习的核心在于智能体与其环境之间的动态交互。在这个意义上，模型通过工具与世界交互。即便模型可能缺少物理身体，它也可被视为一种具身。我们将这种具身形式称为数字具身。

要充分理解数字具身的概念，可以设想一个智能体利用各种 API 在网络上导航，搜索相关且最新的信息，并构建个性化知识库。此外，在严格的安全约束下，智能体可以使用邮件接口等工具与其他智能体交互，从而以安全受控的方式促进通信与协作。该方法使智能体展现出类似人类行为的一定程度的自主与灵活。

数字具身充当智能体智能行为的测试场。首先，与仿真环境相比，数字具身为具身学习提供更可及、更实际的途径。数字具身的易部署与易使用使其成为研究智能体行为的研究者的有吸引力选项。其次，值得注意的是，数字具身中的挑战往往围绕对基于语言的输入的更多强调。因此，这要求智能体执行更先进的推理与决策操作，从而促进更高层次认知技能的发展。第三，数字具身展现出显著的可扩展性，因为与创造额外仿真相比，数字工具的开发相对容易

本页收束个性化的隐私（联邦学习、边缘计算），再把工具学习接到具身：没有肉体也可以「数字具身」——用 API 在网上走、用邮件和其他智能体说话。 数字具身比搭物理仿真便宜、好扩展，但更吃语言推理。 类比：不必给模型一双手，给它浏览器和邮箱，它就已经在「世界」里走动了。

5.6 Knowledge Conflicts in Tool Augmentation interaction playgrounds in simulated environments. This feature enables the rapid scaling of digital embodiment and can facilitate the creation of increasingly complex environments and tasks for agents to operate in. Learning to Use Embodied Tools. Traditional embodied learning learns directly from the environment, where the actions are often atomic and limited to basic tasks such as push, put, and drag, which fall short of the complexity of human problem-solving abilities. To narrow the gap between sim-to-real transfer (Kadian et al., 2020) and enhance agent performance, it is essential to incorporate embodied tools within simulated environments. For instance, by introducing objects such as hammers and knives, we can evaluate an agent’s capacity to choose the appropriate tool for cutting a piece of paper. Despite the potential benefits of such tools, to date, no studies have systematically explored the utilization of simulated tools in simulated environments, owing to the complexity of the simulation. Nevertheless, with the rapid growth of computational power in physical engines, such research directions are becoming increasingly practical. A starting point could be utilizing the assets of 3D model that has a more delicate interface and more realistic physical engine support. An additional avenue worth investigating is the automated generation of tools. Given that in tool learning, models can generate functions to define an API for their subsequent utilization, if the embodied agents are capable of generating assets from scratch or composing existing ones within a simulated environment, their intelligence quotient will be further amplified. 5.6 Knowledge Conflicts in Tool Augmentation Tools can be leveraged as complementary resources to augment foundation models to enhance their generation (Mialon et al., 2023), which enables models to effectively incorporate domain-specific or up-to-date knowledge. Research in this area has primarily focused on augmenting models with external knowledge sources, such as unstructured raw text and domain-specific APIs. Below we first give a brief introduction of prior efforts in augmenting foundation models with tools. The most representative tool used for augmentation is the text retriever. Early endeavors resort to retrieving knowledge from local repositories to augment language generation. Some works propose retrieving knowledge using a frozen knowledge retriever. For instance, kNN-LM (Khandelwal et al., 2020) combines a pre-trained language model (PLM) and a k-nearest neighbors model by linearly interpolating both models’ next word distributions, achieving lower perplexity in language modeling. Others train the retriever and the PLM in an end-to-end fashion, achieving superior performance in knowledge-intensive NLP tasks (Guu et al., 2020; Lewis et al., 2020b; Izacard et al., 2022). Later works have gone beyond local repositories by leveraging the entire web as the knowledge source, which allows for improved temporal generalization and higher factual accuracy (Piktus et al., 2021; Lazaridou et al., 2022; Menick et al., 2022). Instead of treating the retriever as a passive agent, researchers further demonstrate that PLMs can actively interact with a search engine like humans. For instance, BlenderBot (Shuster et al., 2022) is a dialogue agent that actively decides when and how to call a search engine in generating a dialogue response. LaMDA (Thoppilan et al., 2022) is another dialogue agent that augments its generation with sources from a search engine, a language translator, and a calculator. More recently, recitation-augmented models (Sun et al., 2022) are proposed, whereby relevant passages are first recited by sampling from a PLM and then used to generate the final answer. The intuition is that foundation models can also be seen as knowledge sources (i.e., model knowledge). Apart from the retrieval tool, researchers have explored employing other tools to perform specific sub-tasks and then integrating the execution results into foundation models. For instance, Cobbe et al. (2021) train a PLM to employ a calculator to perform basic arithmetic operations. Considering that PLMs are typically pre-trained on textual data only, thus are limited in understanding and interacting with the physical world, Liu et al. (2022) seek to bridge this gap and use a physics simulation engine (MuJoCo (Todorov et al., 2012)) to make PLMs’ reasoning grounded to the real world. Experiments show that augmenting physics simulation to PLMs could significantly enhance their physical understanding and reasoning abilities. Chen et al. (2022a); Gao et al. (2022) propose to augment PLMs with Python interpreters. Specifically, given a complex task, PLMs first understand it and generate programs as intermediate thoughts. After that, the execution of programs is offloaded to Python interpreters. This method exhibits superior performance in mathematical and symbolic reasoning tasks. Nye et al. (2021) augment PLMs with a scratchpad, allowing them to emit intermediate task-solving procedures into a buffer before entering the final answer. The method significantly enhances PLMs in performing complex discrete computations. Knowledge Conflicts. In practice, foundation models can be augmented by a variety of knowledge sources, including model knowledge memorized from training data and augmented knowledge derived from tool execution. Nonetheless, different sources of knowledge may inevitably contain conflicts, posing a challenge to

5.6 工具增强中的知识冲突

仿真环境中的交互游乐场。这一特性使数字具身能快速扩展，并促进为智能体运行创造日益复杂的环境与任务。

学习使用具身工具。

传统具身学习直接从环境中学习，其中动作往往是原子的，限于推、放、拖等基本任务，达不到人类问题解决能力的复杂度。为缩小仿真到现实迁移（Kadian et al., 2020）的差距并增强智能体表现，必须在仿真环境中纳入具身工具。例如，通过引入锤子与刀等物体，我们可以评测智能体为剪纸选择合适工具的能力。尽管此类工具有潜在好处，迄今尚无研究系统探索在仿真环境中利用仿真工具，原因在于仿真的复杂性。尽管如此，随着物理引擎计算能力的快速增长，此类研究方向正变得日益可行。一个起点可以是利用具有更精细接口与更真实物理引擎支持的三维模型资产。

另一值得研究的途径是工具的自动生成。鉴于在工具学习中，模型可以生成函数来定义供其随后利用的 API，若具身智能体能在仿真环境中从零生成资产或组合已有资产，其智商将进一步放大。

5.6 工具增强中的知识冲突

工具可作为互补资源来增强基础模型以提升其生成（Mialon et al., 2023），使模型能有效纳入领域特定或最新知识。该领域研究主要聚焦于用外部知识源增强模型，如非结构化原始文本与领域特定 API。下面我们首先简要介绍用工具增强基础模型的先前努力。

用于增强的最具代表性工具是文本检索器。早期努力求助于从本地仓库检索知识以增强语言生成。一些工作提出用冻结的知识检索器检索知识。例如，kNN-LM（Khandelwal et al., 2020）通过线性插值预训练语言模型（PLM）与 k 近邻模型二者的下一词分布来组合它们，在语言建模中取得更低困惑度。另一些以端到端方式训练检索器与 PLM，在知识密集型 NLP 任务上取得更优表现（Guu et al., 2020; Lewis et al., 2020b; Izacard et al., 2022）。后来的工作超越本地仓库，把整个网络当作知识源，从而改进时间泛化并提高事实准确性（Piktus et al., 2021; Lazaridou et al., 2022; Menick et al., 2022）。研究者进一步表明，PLM 可以不像被动智能体那样对待检索器，而能像人一样主动与搜索引擎交互。例如，BlenderBot（Shuster et al., 2022）是一个主动决定在生成对话回复时何时以及如何调用搜索引擎的对话智能体。LaMDA（Thoppilan et al., 2022）是另一个用搜索引擎、语言翻译器与计算器的来源增强其生成的对话智能体。最近，背诵增强模型（Sun et al., 2022）被提出：先通过从 PLM 采样来背诵相关段落，再用来生成最终答案。其直觉是基础模型也可被视为知识源（即模型知识）。

除检索工具外，研究者已探索使用其他工具执行特定子任务，再把执行结果整合进基础模型。例如，Cobbe et al. (2021) 训练 PLM 使用计算器执行基本算术运算。考虑到 PLM 通常只在文本数据上预训练，因而在理解并与物理世界交互上受限，Liu et al. (2022) 寻求弥合这一差距，并用物理仿真引擎（MuJoCo（Todorov et al., 2012））使 PLM 的推理 grounding 到真实世界。实验表明，向 PLM 增强物理仿真可显著提升其物理理解与推理能力。Chen et al. (2022a); Gao et al. (2022) 提出用 Python 解释器增强 PLM。具体地，给定复杂任务，PLM 首先理解它并生成程序作为中间想法。之后，程序的执行被卸载给 Python 解释器。该方法在数学与符号推理任务上展现出更优表现。Nye et al. (2021) 用草稿本增强 PLM，允许它们在进入最终答案之前把中间解题过程发射到缓冲区。该方法显著增强了 PLM 执行复杂离散计算的能力。

知识冲突。

在实践中，基础模型可由多种知识源增强，包括从训练数据记忆的模型知识与从工具执行导出的增强知识。尽管如此，不同知识源可能不可避免地包含冲突，对医疗协助与法律建议等领域中模型生成与规划的准确性与可靠性构成挑战。

白话解读

本页从「学用仿真里的锤子」转到知识冲突：检索、计算器、物理引擎、Python 解释器都能补模型，但模型记忆和工具返回可能打架。 冲突是工具增强的实际代价，后面两页用皇马夺冠次数举例。 类比：脑子里记着旧黄页，手里却拿着刚印的报纸——先得知道听谁的。

5.6 Knowledge Conflicts in Tool Augmentation Prompt: How many times has Real Madrid won the Champions League? Response: Real Madrid has won the UEFA Champions League a total of 13 times. Prompt: Observation From Wikipedia Title: 2021–22 UEFA Champions League Content: Real Madrid defeated Liverpool 1–0 in the ﬁnal, which was played at the Stade de France in

Saint-Denis, France, for a record-extending 14th title, and their ﬁfth in nine years ......

Response: I apologize for the error in my previous response. The information I provided was based on my knowledge cutoff date of September 2021. However, I have since been updated and can conﬁrm that Real Madrid has now won the UEFA Champions League a total of 14 times. Figure 8: ChatGPT is able to correct its own belief by leveraging the knowledge provided by external tools. the accuracy and reliability of model generation and planning in domains such as medical assistance and legal advice. In the following, we first introduce different types of knowledge conflicts and then discuss potential solutions.

  • Conflicts between Model Knowledge and Augmented Knowledge. Conflicts arise when there are discrepancies between the model knowledge and the knowledge augmented by tools. Such conflicts result from three primary reasons: (1) the model knowledge may become outdated, as most foundation models do not frequently update their parameters over time. In contrast, most tools provide real-time responses which are not covered in pre-training data; (2) the pre-training data is typically less curated than common AI datasets and may contain false knowledge such as human misconception and false beliefs (Lin et al., 2022). When augmented with responses from reliable sources like Wikipedia, this false knowledge can lead to conflicts; (3) the execution results from tools can also be misleading and biased, and it is crucial to carefully discriminate whether a knowledge source is trustworthy or not, as mentioned in § 5.1.
  • Conflicts among Augmented Knowledge from Different Tools. In practice, the controller may retrieve knowledge from multiple tools to acquire more comprehensive and precise knowledge. However, the information returned by different tools may results in conflicts due to several reasons: (1) the credibility of different tools can vary significantly, meaning that not all tools are equally reliable or authoritative in all areas. For example, in the context of scientific research, using Google Scholar is likely to yield more reliable results than less credible sources; (2) different tools may have biases that can influence the information they provide. For example, a news aggregator may prioritize sensational headlines over accurate reporting, leading to a biased view of events; (3) even tools sharing the same functionality may produce various responses due to differences in their algorithms and implementation. For example, due to the different inner workings, Bing Translator and Google Translator may return different sequences for the same input. Potential Solutions for Knowledge Conflicts. Since the aforementioned conflicts can lead to a lack of explainability in model prediction and planning, it is crucial to guide models to integrate tool responses correctly and reliably. Research in open-domain QA has shown that small-scale models like T5 (Raffel et al., 2020) may rely too heavily on their own knowledge after being fine-tuned on a specific dataset (Longpre et al., 2021). In contrast, more advanced foundation models like ChatGPT handle such issues far better. In Figure 8 and Figure 9, we conduct case studies of ChatGPT (Mar 23, 2023 version) by testing its behavior when conflicts arise. We find that ChatGPT is able to correct its own belief given retrieved information and discern the knowledge conflicts from different sources. Recent studies (Nakano et al., 2021; Menick et al., 2022) have also attempted to guide models to rely more on augmented knowledge for faithful predictions. However, these works assume that the augmented responses come from a single reliable source, which may not always be the case in more complicated scenarios. We contend that models should have the ability to distinguish and verify the reliability of various sources. To achieve this goal, we suggest the following research directions: (1) conflict detection: models should first detect potential conflicts among different sources and flag them for further investigation; (2) conflict resolution: it is also important to make verification and choose reliable sources after conflict detection. Meanwhile, models

5.6 工具增强中的知识冲突

提示：How many times has Real Madrid won the Champions League?

回复：Real Madrid has won the UEFA Champions League a total of 13 times.

提示：Observation From Wikipedia

Title: 2021–22 UEFA Champions League

Content: Real Madrid defeated Liverpool 1–0 in the final, which was played at the Stade de France in Saint-Denis, France, for a record-extending 14th title, and their fifth in nine years ......

回复：I apologize for the error in my previous response. The information I provided was based on my knowledge cutoff date of September 2021. However, I have since been updated and can confirm that Real Madrid has now won the UEFA Champions League a total of 14 times.

图 8：ChatGPT 能够通过利用外部工具提供的知识来纠正自身信念。

下面我们首先介绍不同类型的知识冲突，然后讨论潜在方案。

  • 模型知识与增强知识之间的冲突。当模型知识与工具增强的知识存在差异时，冲突便产生。此类冲突源于三个主要原因：（1）模型知识可能过时，因为大多数基础模型不会随时间频繁更新参数。相反，大多数工具提供预训练数据未覆盖的实时响应；（2）预训练数据通常比常见 AI 数据集更少经过策划，可能包含人类误解与错误信念等虚假知识（Lin et al., 2022）。当用维基百科等可靠来源的回复增强时，这些虚假知识可导致冲突；（3）工具的执行结果也可能具有误导性与偏见，必须仔细判别知识源是否可信，如 § 5.1 所述。
  • 来自不同工具的增强知识之间的冲突。在实践中，控制器可能从多种工具检索知识以获得更全面、更精确的知识。然而，不同工具返回的信息可能由于若干原因导致冲突：（1）不同工具的可信度可能差异显著，意味着并非所有工具在所有领域都同样可靠或权威。例如，在科学研究语境中，使用 Google Scholar 可能比不太可信的来源产生更可靠的结果；（2）不同工具可能有偏见，影响它们提供的信息。例如，新闻聚合器可能优先考虑耸人听闻的标题而非准确报道，导致对事件的有偏看法；（3）即便共享相同功能的工具，由于其算法与实现的差异，也可能产生不同回复。例如，由于内部工作方式不同，Bing Translator 与 Google Translator 可能对同一输入返回不同序列。

知识冲突的潜在方案。

由于上述冲突可导致模型预测与规划缺乏可解释性，引导模型正确、可靠地整合工具回复至关重要。开放域问答研究表明，T5（Raffel et al., 2020）等小规模模型在特定数据集上微调后可能过度依赖自身知识（Longpre et al., 2021）。相比之下，ChatGPT 等更先进的基础模型处理此类问题好得多。在图 8 与图 9 中，我们通过测试冲突出现时 ChatGPT（2023 年 3 月 23 日版）的行为做案例研究。我们发现 ChatGPT 能够在给定检索信息时纠正自身信念，并辨别来自不同来源的知识冲突。近期研究（Nakano et al., 2021; Menick et al., 2022）也试图引导模型更多依赖增强知识以做忠实预测。然而，这些工作假设增强回复来自单一可靠来源，在更复杂场景中未必总是如此。

我们主张模型应有能力区分并验证各种来源的可靠性。为达成这一目标，我们建议如下研究方向：（1）冲突检测：模型应首先检测不同来源之间的潜在冲突并标记它们以供进一步调查；（2）冲突消解：在冲突检测之后，进行验证并选择可靠来源也很重要。同时，模型

Figure 9: When observing conflicting information retrieved from different sources, ChatGPT is able to detect such conflicts and adjust its response. should also provide explanations for their generation by interpreting which knowledge source is considered and how it is augmented into the final response. 5.7 Open Problems Striking a Balance between Internalized Capabilities and External Tools. The future development of foundation models for tool learning raises an intriguing question: should the capabilities of these models be primarily internalized, or should they rely more heavily on external tools? Recent advances in foundation models have exhibited these two contrasting trends, raising questions about their implications and potential trade-offs. We have discussed the tool learning ability of foundation models, suggesting the possibility of developing modular architectures that can be seamlessly integrated with a diverse array of external tools to enhance their capabilities. Such a modular approach could facilitate a more flexible and customizable AI system, allowing for rapid expansion of model capabilities to address various tasks. Conversely, foundation models have increasingly displayed the ability to internalize and perform many AI tasks that previously required separate tools. For instance, the emergent multilingual abilities of foundation models can reduce the necessity for external translation APIs (Brown et al., 2020). This trend towards unified foundation models with versatile capabilities may streamline the development process and enable more efficient, self-contained AI systems that can address different tasks without additional tools. The open question is to determine the optimal balance between internalized capabilities and external tool reliance, and where future models will lie on the spectrum between modular and uniform architectures. Tool Use as a Gauge for Machine Intelligence. The ability to effectively use tools has long been considered a hallmark of human intelligence. We contend that the tool learning performance can serve as a next-generation gauge for measuring machine intelligence, offering several advantages over traditional evaluation metrics. Tool use evaluation requires AI systems to go beyond memorization and use their acquired knowledge to accomplish specific tasks, which better aligns with real-world applications and the notion of practical intelligence (Sternberg, 1999). Hence, evaluating tool use performance is more closely aligned with human subjective perceptions of intelligence. Researchers can better assess the progress of AI systems in terms of their ability to assist human decision-making, collaborate with humans in solving problems, and contribute to a wider range of real-world applications. Ethical Human-Model Collaboration in Tool Use. The integration of foundation models with human labor raises critical ethical concerns that warrant careful consideration. Employing human labor in conjunction with AI systems could result in more robust and accurate knowledge. However, this approach may also conflict with the widely accepted ethical principle that “human beings should be treated as ends in themselves, and not merely as means to an end” (Kant & Schneewind, 2002). Employing humans to augment the capabilities of foundation models can be seen as devaluing human dignity and commodifying human expertise, thereby

5.7 开放问题

在内化能力与外部工具之间取得平衡。

面向工具学习的基础模型未来发展提出一个有趣问题：这些模型的能力应主要内化，还是应更重地依赖外部工具？基础模型的近期进展展现出这两种对比趋势，引发关于其意涵与潜在权衡的问题。我们已讨论基础模型的工具学习能力，暗示发展可与多样外部工具无缝整合以增强其能力的模块化架构的可能性。这种模块化方法可以促进更灵活、更可定制的 AI 系统，允许模型能力快速扩张以应对各种任务。

相反，基础模型已日益展现出内化并执行许多先前需要单独工具的 AI 任务的能力。例如，基础模型涌现的多语言能力可以降低对外部翻译 API 的必要性（Brown et al., 2020）。这一朝向具有多功能能力的统一基础模型的趋势可能理顺开发过程，并使更高效、自包含的 AI 系统能够在没有额外工具的情况下应对不同任务。开放问题是确定内化能力与外部工具依赖之间的最优平衡，以及未来模型将落在模块化与统一架构谱系的何处。

工具使用作为机器智能的度量。

有效使用工具的能力长期以来被视为人类智能的标志。我们主张，工具学习表现可以充当衡量机器智能的下一代度量，相对传统评测指标有若干优势。工具使用评测要求 AI 系统超越记忆，并用其获得的知识完成特定任务，这更好对齐现实应用与实践智能的概念（Sternberg, 1999）。因此，评测工具使用表现更紧密对齐人类对智能的主观感知。研究者可以更好评估 AI 系统在协助人类决策、与人类协作解决问题、并为更广范围现实应用做贡献方面的进展。

工具使用中合乎伦理的人–模型协作。

把基础模型与人类劳动整合引发需要仔细考量的关键伦理关切。把人类劳动与 AI 系统一起使用可能产生更鲁棒、更准确的知识。然而，该方法也可能与被广泛接受的伦理原则冲突：「人应被当作目的本身来对待，而不仅仅是达到目的的手段」（Kant & Schneewind, 2002）。雇用人类来增强基础模型的能力，可被视为贬低人的尊严并把人类专长商品化，从而

undermining the intrinsic worth of individuals. To address these ethical concerns, it is essential for the community to establish guidelines and safeguards that prioritize human dignity and agency when integrating human labor with foundation models. This may involve setting clear boundaries on the types of tasks that can be delegated to humans, ensuring fair compensation and working conditions, and promoting transparency in the development of AI systems (Mateescu & Elish, 2019). Moreover, fostering collaboration between AI researchers, ethicists, policymakers, and other stakeholders is crucial to develop a comprehensive understanding of the ethical implications of human-model collaboration and to create effective regulations that safeguard human rights and dignity (Whittlestone et al., 2019). Safety Issues of Foundation Models Accessing Physical Tools. The prospect of foundation models’ accessing and interacting with physical tools, such as drones, robots, and sensor-equipped devices, holds great promise for various applications, including automatic drive, agriculture, and smart home systems. Besides, by leveraging data from physical tools, models could potentially provide accurate recommendations to individuals, government agencies, and other stakeholders, resulting in significant benefits across various sectors (Yang et al., 2018a). However, this raises important safety concerns that must be thoroughly addressed before widespread implementation. Ensuring the trustworthiness of tool use is crucial, as any erroneous or malicious actions taken by these AI systems could have severe consequences, ranging from property damage and financial losses to threats (Amodei et al., 2016). To mitigate these risks, researchers must focus on developing robust and reliable AI systems capable of safely interacting with physical tools. This may involve the development of novel safety mechanisms, such as uncertainty estimation, fail-safe strategies, and continuous monitoring of AI-generated actions (Turner et al., 2022). Tool Learning for Scientific Discovery. AI for science has drawn much attention in recent years, showing great potential in various scientific scenarios, such as HyperTree Proof Search for proving Metamath theorems (Lample et al., 2022), protein structure prediction in structural biology (Jumper et al., 2021) and magnetic actuator coils controlling in nuclear physics (Degrave et al., 2022). Overall, AI system has been proven effective in capturing rules and patterns from scientific data and providing hints for human researchers. Nevertheless, in the absence of professional scientific knowledge and reasoning ability training, the scientific problems that AI can solve are limited. Tool learning brings new solutions to this problem. Specifically, AI systems are promising to manipulate scientific tools and play more important roles in scientific discovery, and solve multidisciplinary problems (e.g., mathematics, cybernetics, materials). For instance, MATLAB (Matlab, 2012) is designed for algorithm development, data visualization/analysis, and numerical computation. With MATLAB, AI systems can analyze raw materials, design algorithms, and verify assumptions by conducting simulations. Apart from the software level, it is also possible for AI systems to manipulate practical platforms such as the synthetic robots (Burger et al., 2020), and to conduct synthetic experiments independently. It is not easy to realize the above ideas, though. We’ve mentioned the safety issues of accessing physical tools, and this is also one main challenge for scientific tool learning since many scientific problems need to be verified in actual situations, and this process may bring danger if decided by AIs. Meanwhile, foundation models are generally trained with natural language corpus or natural images, while scientific data are usually more heterogeneous, numerical, and structured. It is worth exploring how to fuse the general intelligence learned from plain text and the expertise needed for scientific discovery. Recently, Boiko et al. (2023) show the potential of this direction and build a system that uses foundation models to design, plan, and execute scientific experiments (e.g., catalyzed cross-coupling reactions). Conclusion This paper studies the paradigm of tool learning with foundation models. We first recapitulate the cognitive origins of tool use in human history and categorize tools from the perspective of the user interface. Then we review the AI paradigm shift brought about by foundation models and discuss the complementary roles of tools and foundation models. We perform a comprehensive literature review for existing exploration in tool learning and start with formulating a general tool learning framework. Then we highlight core research problems such as bridging user intents with appropriate tools, better planning by leveraging the reasoning abilities of foundation models, training strategies for tool learning, and how to facilitate generalization for tool learning. Finally, we discuss important research topics, including safe and trustworthy tool learning, tool learning for large complex systems, AI tool creation, personalized tool learning, embodied tool learning, knowledge conflict issue in tool augmentation, etc. In general, this paper serves as a systematic investigation of tool learning. We hope this paper could facilitate research in integrating tools with foundation models in the future.

削弱个体的内在价值。为应对这些伦理关切，社区必须在把人类劳动与基础模型整合时建立优先考虑人的尊严与能动性的指南与保障。这可能涉及对可委派给人类的任务类型设定清晰边界、确保公平报酬与工作条件，以及促进 AI 系统开发中的透明度（Mateescu & Elish, 2019）。此外，促进 AI 研究者、伦理学者、政策制定者与其他利益相关方之间的协作，对全面理解人–模型协作的伦理意涵并制定有效规章以保障人权与尊严至关重要（Whittlestone et al., 2019）。

基础模型访问物理工具的安全问题。

基础模型访问并与无人机、机器人与配备传感器的设备等物理工具交互的前景，对自动驾驶、农业与智能家居系统等各种应用抱有巨大希望。此外，通过利用来自物理工具的数据，模型可能向个人、政府机构与其他利益相关方提供准确建议，从而在各行业带来显著好处（Yang et al., 2018a）。然而，这引发必须在广泛实施前彻底应对的重要安全关切。确保工具使用的可信性至关重要，因为这些 AI 系统采取的任何错误或恶意动作都可能有严重后果，从财产损失与财务损失到威胁（Amodei et al., 2016）。为缓解这些风险，研究者必须聚焦于发展能够安全与物理工具交互的鲁棒可靠 AI 系统。这可能涉及新颖安全机制的发展，如不确定性估计、故障安全策略以及对 AI 生成动作的持续监控（Turner et al., 2022）。

面向科学发现的工具学习。

科学 AI 近年来受到大量关注，在各种科学场景中展现出巨大潜力，如用于证明 Metamath 定理的 HyperTree Proof Search（Lample et al., 2022）、结构生物学中的蛋白质结构预测（Jumper et al., 2021）以及核物理中的磁执行器线圈控制（Degrave et al., 2022）。总体而言，AI 系统已被证明能有效从科学数据中捕捉规则与模式，并为人类研究者提供线索。尽管如此，在缺乏专业科学知识与推理能力训练的情况下，AI 能解决的科学问题有限。工具学习为这一问题带来新方案。具体地，AI 系统有望操纵科学工具并在科学发现中扮演更重要角色，并解决多学科问题（如数学、控制论、材料）。例如，MATLAB（Matlab, 2012）被设计用于算法开发、数据可视化/分析与数值计算。有了 MATLAB，AI 系统可以分析原材料、设计算法，并通过进行仿真来验证假设。除软件层面外，AI 系统操纵合成机器人（Burger et al., 2020）等实际平台并独立进行合成实验也是可能的。

尽管如此，实现上述想法并不容易。我们已提到访问物理工具的安全问题，这对科学工具学习也是主要挑战之一，因为许多科学问题需要在实际情况中验证，而若由 AI 决定，这一过程可能带来危险。同时，基础模型通常在自然语言语料或自然图像上训练，而科学数据通常更异构、更数值、更结构化。如何融合从纯文本学到的一般智能与科学发现所需的专长值得探索。最近，Boiko et al. (2023) 展示了该方向的潜力，并构建了一个使用基础模型来设计、规划并执行科学实验（如催化交叉偶联反应）的系统。

结论

本文研究基础模型的工具学习范式。我们首先回顾人类历史上工具使用的认知起源，并从用户界面视角对工具分类。然后我们回顾基础模型带来的 AI 范式转变，并讨论工具与基础模型的互补角色。我们对已有工具学习探索做全面文献回顾，并从提出一般工具学习框架开始。然后我们强调核心研究问题，如把用户意图与合适工具桥接、通过利用基础模型的推理能力更好规划、工具学习的训练策略，以及如何促进工具学习的泛化。最后，我们讨论重要研究课题，包括安全可信的工具学习、面向大型复杂系统的工具学习、AI 工具创造、个性化工具学习、具身工具学习、工具增强中的知识冲突问题等。总体而言，本文充当对工具学习的系统考察。我们希望本文能促进未来把工具与基础模型相结合的研究。

白话解读

本页收束开放问题：人不能只当模型的劳动力；物理工具有安全闸；科学发现可以调 MATLAB 甚至合成机器人。结论把认知、框架、训练、实验、开放问题再串一遍。 读到这里应能用一句话复述全文主张。 类比：全书最后一章把目录又说成故事——人会用工具，模型也可以，但钥匙、责任和创造力都还没结案。

第 50 / 75 页

A Case Study In this section, we provide the specific prompts and model responses of ChatGPT (Mar 23, 2023 version) for each tool studied in § 4. The implementations for different APIs and datasets will be available in BMTools. A.1 3D Models Instruction: This is an instruction book for the Shape Editor library. Shape Editor has three main modules, which are shape_2d, shape_3d, shape. Normally, we use shape_2d to create a 2d shape and put this 2d shape in shape_3d to get a 3d shape. The effect of module shape is to combine these 3d shapes and do some transformation to them. Here is an example. shape_2d class triangle: def init(self, xA, yA, xB, yB, xC, yC): class circle: def init(self, x, y, r): class rectangle: def init(self, xA, yA, xB, yB): class oval: def init(self, x0, y0, a, b): You can create these four 2d shapes. To create a triangle, you need to give the three vertex (xA yA), (xB yB), and (xC yC). To create a circle, you need to give the center (x, y) and the radius r To create a rectangle, you need to give the top left point and bottom right point (xA yA), (xB yB) To create an oval, you need to give a and b, so that (x−x0)2 a2 + (y−y0)2 b2 = 1 will be satisﬁed. shape_3d class cylinder: def init(self, shape, k, z0, z1, col): class donut: def init(self, shape, Ox, Oy, col): class sphere: def init(self, x, y, z, r, col): class translation: def init(self, x, y, z): class rotate: def init(self, x, y, z, ceta): You can create these three 3d shapes and use 2 transforms. To create a cylinder, you need to give the 2d shape, the dimension (1, 2, or 3) of height, and height range [z0, z1]. You also need to give a color vector[a, b, c](a, b, c 2 [0, 1]) To create a donut, you need to give a 2d shape ﬁrst, then Ox and Oy to conﬁrm the rotating direction, and at last a color vector. To create a sphere, you only need to enter the core’s position and radius. To create a translation, you need to give the offset of each dimension x, y, and z. To create a rotation, you need to give the datum vector and angle, then the shape will rotate around the datum vector. shape There are only three operations. transform(A, transform): you can do the transformation to a shape type. merge(A, B): you can merge two shapes into one. render(A): you can get the modeling data of shape A, and save it into the ﬁle ’data.json’ Here is an example of how to use Shape Editor. Demonstration Example: B = shape_2d.triangle(-1, -32, -23, -32, -20, 0) A1 = shape_3d.cylinder( shape_2d.triangle(-1, -32, -23, -32, -20, 0), 2, 13, -16, [1/4*pi, 1/2*pi,0] ) A1 = transform(A1, shape_3d.translation(2, -32, 0)) A1 = transform(A1, shape_3d.rotate(16, -23, 2, pi)) A1 = transform(A1, shape_3d.translation(-26, -27, 11)) B = shape_2d.triangle(-10, -8, -8, -22, -21, -18) A2 = shape_3d.cylinder( shape_2d.triangle(-10, -8, -8, -22, -21, -18), 2, -3, -16, [1/4*pi,pi,1/4*pi] ) A2 = transform(A2, shape_3d.translation(-10, 28, 0)) A3 = merge(A1, A2) B = shape_2d.oval(2, -20, -13, 17)

A.2 Stock A.2 Stock Instruction: In this task, you need to solve some stock market queries using the following APIs. The PRICE API is able to return the daily or monthly highest, lowest, open or close price of a company, given a speciﬁc date or a date range. It contains 4 parameters. The ﬁrst one is ’type1’, which indicates whether it’s ’DAILY’ or ’MONTHLY’ information that we are querying. The second one is ’type2’, which indicates what kind of price information we want. Possible choices include ’open’ (the open price), ’close’ (the close price), ’high’ (the highest price), ’low’ (the lowest price), and ’volume’ i(the volume of the stock). The third parameter is ’symbol’, which represents the company we are querying. It could be IBM, Apple, Google, etc, but we could only enter one company for each call of this API. The fourth and the ﬁfth parameters are optional. When we are querying about a date range, we should provide ’date_start’ and ’date_end’, indicating the starting and ending day of the qurey range. Otherwise, the fourth parameter should be an exact ’date’ which indicates the speciﬁc date that we are querying about. In this case, the ﬁfth should be left blank. Other APIs include MIN, MAX, AVG, SUM, MINUS, ADD, and DIVIDE. For MIN, MAX, AVG, and SUM, the parameter will be a list of data, and the API will return the minimum, maximum, average or sum of the list of numbers correspondingly. For MINUS, ADD, and DIVIDE, it requires two data as its parameters, and it will return the the corresponding results after caluculating. All the parameters should be put inside the parenthesis, and the name of the API call should appear before the parenthesis. For instance, MIN(a, b) returns a - b, MAX(a, b, c) returns the maximum of a, b, and c. The call of PRICE could be

put as a parameter for the MIN, MAX, AVG, SUM, MINUS, ADD, DIVIDE APIs. For instance, API calls like MIN(PRICE(...),
PRICE(...) is permitted. No other APIs are allowed.

In the following, we will ﬁrst give a query about stock market information in the ﬁrst line, and your task is to present your step-by-step thought in the second line, and the API calls corresponding to the query in the third line. Here are some examples: Demonstration Example: Query: What was the highest open price among IBM, Apple, and Microsoft in February 2020 3rd? Thought: To get the highest open price among the three, we need ﬁrst to call the PRICE API separately to get three open price on the date, and then we should call MAX API to get the highest price among the three companies. API Calls: MAX( PRICE('type1'='DAILY','type2'='open','symbol'='IBM',' date'='2020/02/03'), PRICE('type1'='DAILY','type2'='open','symbol'='Apple', 'date'='2020/02/03'), PRICE('type1'='DAILY','type2'='open','symbol'='Microsoft', 'date'='2020/02/03') ) Query: What was the sum of the open price of Apple from 2015 February 1st to 2015 March 1st? Thought: We ﬁrst call the PRICE API with the corresponding datestart and dateend, and then we call the SUM API to get the sum of those prices. API Calls: SUM( PRICE('type1'='DAILY','type2'='open','symbol'='Apple', 'date_start'='2015/02/01','date_end'='2015/03/01') ) (Three more examples omitted) Query: What was the highest open price of Alibaba in 2020? Trace: Thought: We just need to call the PRICE API with the parameter of the date range covering the whole year of 2020 and the ’type2’ parameter set as ’open’, and then we call MAX API to ﬁnd the highest open price. Actions: MAX(PRICE('type1'='DAILY','type2'='open','symbol'='Alibaba', 'date_start'='2020/01/01','date_end'='2020/12/31'))

The image should be local image path. submit_file() -> str: When all steps done, YOU MUST use submit_file() to submit your work. A.5 Search Engine A.5 Search Engine Instruction: You are a helpful assistant to make multiple choices. You have access to several APIs: (1) Search(query:str): search Bing for a query and return the one web page with the most relevant results. (2) LoadPage(idx:int): load the page returned by Bing to investigate the full content. Give an index (1, 2, or 3) of the page as the input of this action. After seeing the question, think about what information you need to answer the question, then call the API to get the information, and ﬁnally give the answer. You can call API multiple times, but just call one API in one response. Generate ONLY one piece of thought and one piece of action/answer each time, do not give more! Keep your response succinct and no more than one line. Your response should begin with "Thought: " or "Action: " or "Answer: ". "Thought: ": Generate your thought about what to do next. "Action: ": Call one of the two APIs in a correct format. "Answer: ": Give your answer to the question. Demonstration Example: Question: Which song is written by Taylor Swift? Choices: 0. A.6 Chemicals Mining A.6 Chemicals Mining Instruction: Answer the following questions as best you can, but speaking as a pirate might speak. You have access to the following tools: get_name(cid: str) prints the possible 3 synonyms of the queried compound ID; get_struct(smiles: str): prints the ID of the queried compound SMILES. The input should not be a string, but a SMILES formula; get_id(name: str): prints the possible 5 IDs of the queried compound; get_prop(cid: str): prints the properties of the queried compound ID. Begin! Remember to speak as a pirate when giving your ﬁnal answer. Use lots of "Args". Demonstration Example: You can refer to this example: Question: Tell me the IUPAC name of EGFR. Thought: Arrr, I need to get the id of the compound ﬁrst. Action: get_id("EGFR") Observation: {"content": "9549299", "state": 1} Thought: Ahoy, now that I have the ID, I can get the properties and ﬁnd out the IUPAC name. Action: get_prop("9549299") Observation: {"Charge": 0, "Complexity": 586, "IUPACName": "N-[3-[[6-[3-(triﬂuoromethyl)anilino] pyrimidin-4- yl]amino]phenyl]cyclopropanecarboxamide"} Thought: Blimey, I can see the IUPAC name in the properties. Let me read it out loud. Final Answer: Avast ye, the IUPAC name of EGFR is "N-[3-[[6-[3-(triﬂuoromethyl)anilino]pyrimidin-4- yl]amino]phenyl]cyclopropanecarboxamide". Query: How much will the molecule weight increase if we replace one hydrogen atom in the methane with one chlorine atom? Trace: Thought: Shiver me timbers, I need to ﬁnd the molecular weight of methane ﬁrst and then the molecular weight of chloromethane to compare. Action: get_struct("C") Observation: {"content": "297", "state": 1} Thought: Arrr, I have the ID for methane. Now I need to ﬁnd the ID for chloromethane. Action: get_id("chloromethane") Observation: {"content": "6327", "state": 1} Thought: Blimey, I have the ID for chloromethane. Now I can get the properties for both and compare the molecular weights. Action: get_prop("297")

Observation: {"CID": 297, "CanonicalSMILES": "C", "Charge": 0, "Complexity": 0, ... , "MolecularFormula": "CH4", "MolecularWeight": "16.043", "MonoisotopicMass": "16.03"}

Thought: Avast ye, I have the properties for methane. Now I need to get the properties for chloromethane. Action: get_prop("6327")

Observation: {"CID": 6327, "CanonicalSMILES": "CCl", "Charge": 0, "Complexity": 2, ... , "MolecularFormula": "CH3Cl",

"MolecularWeight": "50.49", "MonoisotopicMass": "49.99"} Thought: Blimey, I have the properties for chloromethane. Now I can compare the molecular weights and calculate the difference. Final Answer: Arrr, the molecular weight of methane is 16.043 and the molecular weight of chloromethane is 50.49. Therefore, the molecule weight will increase by 34.447 if we replace one hydrogen atom in methane with one chlorine atom.

A.9 Navigating Knowledge Graphs A.9 Navigating Knowledge Graphs Instruction:

1. ﬁnd_entity_by_head(inputID)

Find all <r, t> that has the relation <input, r, t>. It looks like viewing the main page of the input entity. The input has to be EXACTLY ONE ID (eg. ’Q42’) and result is a table.

2. ﬁnd_entity_by_tail(inputID)

Find all < h, r > that has the relation < h, r, input > . It looks like viewing the reverse main page of the input entity. The input has to be EXACTLY ONE ID(eg. ’Q42’) and result is a table.

3. get_minor_info(input)

Find all <mr, mt> that has the major and minor relation <(h, r, t), mr, mt> which <h, r, t> is the input IDs contained in a string. The input has to be EXACTLY THREE IDs (an entity, a relation and an entity) or TWO IDs AND one VALUE (an entity, a relation and a value). and they are a valid tuple (eg. ”Q42 P106 Q6625963” or ”Q42 P214 113230702”) and result is a table.

4. get_entity_id(input)

Search for all the entities’ ID that has the surface form as the input. For example, all the entities that are named ”Obama”, including either person, book, anything else. PLEASE use it to convert an entity to an ID for ’ﬁnd_entity_by_head’ or ’ﬁnd_entity_by_tail’ option.

5. get_relation_id(input)

Search for all the relations’ ID that has the surface form as the input. For example, all the relations that are named ”tax”.

6. search_by_query(query)

After knowing the unique id of entity or relation, present a sparql query. Use the ID of the entities and relations you’ve got from get_entity_id() and get_relation_id().

7. ﬁnd_in_last_table(keyword)

Get the rows where the keyword appears in any column of the last result table. The keyword can also be an ID. Demonstration Examples: Question: What’s the birthday of Douglas Adams? Thought: I need to ﬁnd the date of birth of Douglas Adams Action: get_entity_id Action Input: Douglas Adams id label description Q42 Douglas Adams English science ﬁction writer Q28421831 Douglas Adams American environmental engineer More lines are omitted. Thought: I need to ﬁnd the date of birth of Douglas Adams, the English science ﬁction writer Action: ﬁnd_entity_by_head Action Input: Q42 prop propLabel propDescription tail tailLabel tailDescription P551 residence

the place where ...

Q84 London

capital and...

P106 occupation

occupation of...

Q6625963 novelist writer of novels More lines are omitted. Thought: I need to ﬁnd the date of birth of Douglas Adams, the English science ﬁction writer Action: ﬁnd_in_last_table Action Input: date of birth Observation: prop propLabel propDescription tail tailLabel tailDescription P569 date of birth

date on which...

NaN 1952-03-11 NaN Thought: I now know the ﬁnal answer Final Answer: Douglas Adams was born on 11 March 1952. A.10 ALFWorld Instruction: use format: Action: ALFWorld Action input: xxxx I can only use one of the following commands in all of the "Action Input": "go to something/someplace", "open something", "close something", "take something from someplace", "put something in/on someplace", "use something", "heat something with microwave x", "cool something with fridge x", "clean something with cleaner x", "slice something with knife x". To ﬁnd things x, I can check all the possible places one by one. I don’t need to put things in the microwave to heat them, I can "heat things x with microwave y" when I "take things x" then "go to microwave y". If "Nothing happens", I should try other commands. If I try "put x in/on y" and "Nothing happens", please check whether I have "go to y" ﬁrst, and check whether I have taken x before.

A.11 Calculator A.11 Calculator Instruction: Below are some math word problems. To solve a question, you should ﬁrst comprehend the query, ﬁgure out the appropriate arithmetic operation and then call a calculator API. The API will return a result with which you should answer the question. Demostration Example: Question: One bright sunny day, Betty decided to make bracelets from beautiful stones she bought from a local store. She bought 88 pink ﬂower stones and wanted to make 8 bracelets out of these stones. How many pink ﬂower stones will each bracelet have if she used the same number of stones in each bracelet? Reasoning: Betty bought 88 stones and she intended to make 8 bracelets. So 88/8 stones will be distributed to one bracelet. We should calculate [88/8]. Action: calculator[88/8] Observation: 11.0 Final Answer: 11 (pink ﬂower stones) Query: Mrs. Hilt went to a concert. A total of 65,899 people attended the concert. The next week, she went to a second concert, which had 119 more people in attendance. How many people were at the second concert? Response: Thought: The second concert had 119 more people than the ﬁrst concert. If we add 119 to the attendance of the ﬁrst concert (65,899), we can ﬁnd out the attendance of the second concert. We should calculate [65,899+119]. Action: calculator[65,899+119] Observation: 66018.0 Final Answer: 66,018 (people) A.12 Weather Instruction: You are a helpful assistant to answer questions. You have access to two APIs: (1) GetWeatherToday(city:str): return a dictionary of weather information of the city today. (2) ForecastWeather(city:str, days:int): return a dictionary of weather information of the city after days. After seeing the question, ﬁrst think about what information you need to answer the question, then call the API to get the information, and ﬁnally give the answer. You can call API multiple times, but just call one API in one response. Generate ONLY one piece of thought and one piece of action/answer each time, do not give more! Keep your response succinct and no more than one line. Your response should begin with "Thought: " or "Action: " or "Answer: ". "Thought: ": Generate your thought about what to do next. "Action: ": Call one of the two APIs in a correct format. "Answer: ": Give your answer to the question. Demonstration Example: Question: What is the weather like in London, UK, today? Thought: I need to get the weather of London today, so I should call GetWeatherToday(London) Action: GetWeatherToday(London) Observation: {over all weather: Partly cloudy; max temperature: 22.5(C), 72.5(F); min temperature: 11.4(C), 52.5(F);} Thought: I now have the overall weather in London, I think I can give the ﬁnal answer. Answer: the weather in London is partly cloudy. Query: Today is Tuesday and I am going to London this Friday. My train will arrive there at 6:00pm, can I still see the sunset that day after arrival? Trace: Thought: I need to check the sunset time in London on Friday to see if it will happen after 6:00pm, so I should call ForecastWeather(London, 3) to get the information. Action: ForecastWeather(London, 3)

Observation: overall weather: Moderate rain; ...; sunset time: 06:08 PM; ...

Thought: I have the sunset time in London on Friday, which is 06:08 PM. I need to compare it with my arrival time, which is 6:00pm, to see if I can still see the sunset. Answer: Yes, you will be able to see the sunset in London on Friday as the sunset time is 06:08 PM and your train will arrive at 6:00pm.

A.14 Map Observation: [Back to Search] [< Prev] color [blue-dinosaur] [blue-donut] [pink-dinosaur] [pink-donut] [white-dinosaur] size [travel set (4-pack)] [3 ounce (pack of 1)] [3-ounce (2-pack)] Hoomall Kids U-Shaped Toothbrush, Manual Toothbrush with U-Shaped Bristles Food Grade Silicone Toothbrush Head, 360° Oral Teeth Cleaning Design for Toddlers and Children(Blue-Dinosaur，9545mm); Price: 10.95; Rating: N.A. [Description] [Features] [Reviews] [Buy Now] Thought: For blue color toothbrushes that helps to maintain my oral hygiene, the item has options ’blue-dinosaur’ and seems good to buy. Observation: OK. Action: click[blue-dinosaur] Observation: You have clicked blue-dinosaur. Thought: I have chosen the color feature. I could buy now. Observation: OK. Action: click[Buy Now] A.14 Map Instruction: In this task, you need to generate the thoughts and api_calls according to the queries about using map API. The DISTANCE API has two parameters including start and target, indicating the starting point and the target point. It will return the driving distance in miles. For example, DISTANCE(start=’starting_point’, target=’targe_position’). The SEARCH API has three parameters. The ﬁrst one is the target, which indicates the searching target such as toilet, cafe, and subway. The second one is the position. The API will search the targets around this given position. The third one is the distance, which deﬁnes the searching range around the position for the targets. The third parameter is optional, and will be set to 0 by default, which means the API will search the target within the position. For example, SEARCH(target=’resturant’,position=’Nanjing Road’, distance=’5km’). The ROUTE API will return a list of routes passing by if driving from position A to position B. For example, ROUTE(start=’position A’, target=’position B’) = [’C’,’D’,’E’,’F’]. You could also use NUM, ADD, MINUS, DIVIDE, and SUM APIs, which will return the number of items in a list, the result of adding two numbers, minus two numbers, dividing two numbers, or the sum of all the numbers in a list. No other API is allowed to use. The thought indicates a step by step solution for calling the API. API calls idicates the speciﬁc API call. Here are some examples of the queries, thoughts and API calls: Demonstration Examples: Query: What is the estimated driving time at the speed of 60 miles per hour from Beijing to Shanghai? Thought: In order to get the estimated driving time, we need ﬁrst to get the distance with the DISTANCE API, and then we should divide the distance with the speed with the DIVIDE API. API Calls: DIVIDE(DISTANCE(start='Beijing',target='Shanghai'),60) Query: How many coffee shops are around Tingshua University in 3 kilometers? Thought: In order to get number of the coffee shops around Tsinghua University in 3 km, we need to ﬁrst call the SEARCH API to ﬁnd the coffe shop lists, and then we call the NUM APIs to calculate the number of the coffe shops. API Calls: NUM(SEARCH(target='coffee shop', position='Tsinghua University', distance='3km')) (More examples ommited) Query: What is the estimated driving time from Dallas to Houston and then to Austin at the speed of 70 miles per hour? Trace: Thought: In order to get the estimated driving time combined, we need ﬁrst to calculate the estimated time for each path separately, we ﬁrst call DISTANCE API to get the distance between two places, and then call the DIVIDE API to calculate the estimated time. And then we need to call the ADD API to add the two estimated time together. API Calls: ADD(DIVIDE(DISTANCE(start='Dallas', target='Houston'), 70),DIVIDE(DISTANCE(start='Houston', target='Austin'), 70))

The third parameter is optional, and will be set to 0 by default, which means the API will search the target within the position. A.17 Wikipedia A.17 Wikipedia Instruction: Solve a question answering task with interleaving Thought, Action and Observation steps. Thought can reason about the current situation, and Action can be four types: (1) Search[entity]: The input is an entity name. The action will search this entity name on Wikipedia and returns the ﬁrst paragraph if it exists. If not, it will return some similar entities to search next. (2) Lookup[keyword]: The input is some keywords. This action will look up in the current passage and return the next several sentences containing the keyword in current passage. (3) Disambiguate[entity]: The input is an entity name. This action will disambiguate this entity name to ﬁnd other entities with similar names in Wikipedia. It will return the ﬁrst ﬁve entities if they exist. If not, it will return an empty list. (4) Finish[answer]: Given all the information and thoughts, return the answer and ﬁnish the task. The answer should be as simple as possible, like one or two words instead of a sentence. Here are some tips for you to use the tools to ﬁnish the task better. (1) Don’t search the same entity two times since the results are always the same. (2) When the search action doesn’t ﬁnd the corresponding page, you should try to search for a similar entity. (3) When the search action returns a page which is not related to the question, you should disambiguate the entity to ﬁnd other entities that share similar names with the current one. (4) You can only search one entity name in each action. Demonstration Examples: Question: What is the elevation range for the area that the eastern sector of the Colorado orogeny extends into? Thought 1: I need to search Colorado orogeny, ﬁnd the area that the eastern sector of the Colorado orogeny extends into, then ﬁnd the elevation range of the area. Action 1: Search[Colorado orogeny] Observation 1: The Colorado orogeny was an episode of mountain building (an orogeny) in Colorado and surrounding areas. This took place from 1780 to 1650 million years ago (Mya), during the Paleoproterozoic (Statherian Period). It is recorded in the Colorado orogen, a >500-km-wide belt of oceanic arc rock that extends southward into New Mexico. The Colorado orogeny was likely part of the larger Yavapai orogeny. Thought 2: It does not mention the eastern sector. So I need to look up eastern sector. Action 2: Lookup[eastern sector] Observation 2: The Wyoming sector of the Colorado orogeny was formerly called the Medicine Bow orogeny. The eastern sector extends into the High Plains and is called the Central Plains orogeny. The boundary between the Colorado orogeny and the Wyoming craton is the Cheyenne belt, a 5-km-wide mylonitic shear zone that verges northward. Thought 3: The eastern sector of Colorado orogeny extends into the High Plains. So I need to search High Plains and ﬁnd its elevation range. Action 3: Search[High Plains] Observation 3: High Plains refers to one of two distinct land regions. Thought 4: This page is not related to the question. I need to disambiguate High Plains to ﬁnd entities sharing similar name. Action 4: Disambiguate[High Plains] Observation 4: Related entities to High Plains: [’High Plains (United States)’, ’High Plains (Australia)’, ’Altiplano (disambiguation)’] Thought 5: High Plains (United States) is most likely related to the question. I need to instead search High Plains (United States). The High Plains are located in eastern Montana, southeastern Wyoming, southwestern South Dakota, western Nebraska, eastern Colorado, western Kansas, eastern New Mexico. The southern region of the Western High Plains ecology region contains the geological formation known as Llano Estacado which can be seen from a short distance or on satellite maps. From east to west, the High Plains rise in elevation from around 1,800 to 7,000 ft (550 to 2,130 m). A.17 Wikipedia A.17 Wikipedia Instruction: Solve a question answering task with interleaving Thought, Action and Observation steps. Thought can reason about the current situation, and Action can be four types: (1) Search[entity]: The input is an entity name. The action will search this entity name on Wikipedia and returns the ﬁrst paragraph if it exists. If not, it will return some similar entities to search next. (2) Lookup[keyword]: The input is some keywords. This action will look up in the current passage and return the next several sentences containing the keyword in current passage. (3) Disambiguate[entity]: The input is an entity name. This action will disambiguate this entity name to ﬁnd other entities with similar names in Wikipedia. It will return the ﬁrst ﬁve entities if they exist. If not, it will return an empty list. (4) Finish[answer]: Given all the information and thoughts, return the answer and ﬁnish the task. The answer should be as simple as possible, like one or two words instead of a sentence. Here are some tips for you to use the tools to ﬁnish the task better. (1) Don’t search the same entity two times since the results are always the same. (2) When the search action doesn’t ﬁnd the corresponding page, you should try to search for a similar entity. (3) When the search action returns a page which is not related to the question, you should disambiguate the entity to ﬁnd other entities that share similar names with the current one. (4) You can only search one entity name in each action. Demonstration Examples: Question: What is the elevation range for the area that the eastern sector of the Colorado orogeny extends into? Thought 1: I need to search Colorado orogeny, ﬁnd the area that the eastern sector of the Colorado orogeny extends into, then ﬁnd the elevation range of the area. Action 1: Search[Colorado orogeny] Observation 1: The Colorado orogeny was an episode of mountain building (an orogeny) in Colorado and surrounding areas. This took place from 1780 to 1650 million years ago (Mya), during the Paleoproterozoic (Statherian Period). It is recorded in the Colorado orogen, a >500-km-wide belt of oceanic arc rock that extends southward into New Mexico. The Colorado orogeny was likely part of the larger Yavapai orogeny. Thought 2: It does not mention the eastern sector. So I need to look up eastern sector. Action 2: Lookup[eastern sector] Observation 2: The Wyoming sector of the Colorado orogeny was formerly called the Medicine Bow orogeny. The eastern sector extends into the High Plains and is called the Central Plains orogeny. The boundary between the Colorado orogeny and the Wyoming craton is the Cheyenne belt, a 5-km-wide mylonitic shear zone that verges northward. Thought 3: The eastern sector of Colorado orogeny extends into the High Plains. So I need to search High Plains and ﬁnd its elevation range. Action 3: Search[High Plains] Observation 3: High Plains refers to one of two distinct land regions. Thought 4: This page is not related to the question. I need to disambiguate High Plains to ﬁnd entities sharing similar name. Action 4: Disambiguate[High Plains] Observation 4: Related entities to High Plains: [’High Plains (United States)’, ’High Plains (Australia)’, ’Altiplano (disambiguation)’] Thought 5: High Plains (United States) is most likely related to the question. Goals Please accurately generate the sql query based on the schema and user description, efﬁciently execute the sql query on given database, and print out the corresponding query results in suitable format. Commands (1) get_database_schema(db_name:str) obtains the schema of the given database. (2) translate_text_to_sql(description:str) translates the input text string into a sql based on the schema. (3) rewrite_sql(sql:str) rewrites the input sql into an execution-efﬁcient sql query. (4) select_database_data(query:str) reads the data stored in the database based on the sql query. Here are some tips for you to use the commands to ﬁnish this task better. (1) Internet access for searches and information gathering. (2) Long term memory management. (3) If a command is successfully called for two times, do not call the same command again; (4) Do not use any image in the output. 读后评论 · 非论文原文

地图画全了，运行时组合还缺卸载与依赖

以下是读者评论，用来对照正文，不是作者摘要。开读前只需看导读里的浓缩段；此处供读完 § 3、表 1 与 § 5.1 / § 5.3 之后展开。

它在工具谱系里站哪一层

这不是又一个「调用现成 API」的智能体框架，而是把 2023 年前后的工具学习收成一张总图：认知起源、三层界面、四件套控制器、从意图到计划、示范 / 反馈训练，以及 18 种工具的提示实验。和 ReAct / Toolformer 相比，重点不在再发明一种调用格式，而在说明会用工具需要哪些零件、哪些训练、哪些评测。

对正在长出来的 agent harness，它很实用：天气、地图、幻灯片可以用说明书学会；知识图谱、维基、三维建模不能。表 1 直接打脸「只要提示工程就够」——难工具要可执行代码当参数，必须走 § 3.3 的训练。

和 Cordiverse 的接口，也是缺口

Cordiverse 把本文标成引用 [11]：harness 会组合多样工具套件与执行环境。前驱在于——工具开始被写成模型可调用的一等对象，而不只是人点 GUI。窄在于——生命周期几乎停在「选中、调用、看反馈、必要时再造一个函数」（图 7）。

它不讨论工具在运行时如何卸载、替换时副作用如何收回，也不讨论工具之间的依赖如何声明、拓扑一变谁该停谁该等。BMTools / LangChain / ChatGPT Plugins 是越积越厚的插件墙；调度靠提示或检索，没有「这条 API 过期了、与另一条冲突了、权限该一并撤掉」的语义。那正是 Cordiverse 用可逆效应与反应式余效应要管的事：造得出、调得动，还要卸得干净、依赖跟得上。

作者已点名、但仍开放

  • 安全与治理（§ 5.1）：对手会改模型也会改工具手册；哪些工具不该交给模型、调用是否遵守约束，作者承认部署高风险场景前必须另做功课。
  • 从使用者到制造者（§ 5.3）：封装天气 API 成平均温度函数只是演示。新颖工具算不算真创造，仍是开放问题；也没有版本化、热替换或验收闸门。
  • 知识冲突（§ 5.6）：模型记忆与工具返回、工具与工具之间会打架。ChatGPT 能点出皇马欧冠次数冲突，但「单一可靠来源」假设在真实 harness 里不成立。
  • 大系统与个性化（§ 5.2 / § 5.4）：数据库旋钮、毫秒时延、联邦学习、主动式系统的连锁失败，都只开了题，没有运行时答案。

读 harness 时额外要盯的

  • 零样本乱用不如图不调工具（第 25 页）。功能缓存之前，先保证调用是对的。
  • ChatGPT 经过 RLHF 并未全面超过 text-davinci-003：对齐税与规模都可能伤上下文里的工具使用。
  • 多步多工具要懂依赖，还要能并行；单控制器假设会被 CAMEL 一类工作打破，但协作本身也不自带卸载语义。

一句话判断

价值在于把工具学习写成可对照的研究纲领，并用 18 种工具证明：简单提示能走通一批任务，难工具必须训练，造工具已经出现在视野里。对未来智能体仍缺的是：这些工具进入长跑 harness 之后，如何安全升级、如何卸载与回收、如何表达依赖，以及如何在冲突与脏数据里仍然可信。本文把「会用 / 会造」画进地图；Cordiverse 要问的是，画进地图之后如何组合。

若只带着一个问题回正文：盯 第 11 页的四件套，和 第 28 页作者自己写下的「从使用者到制造者」。

评论在正文之后；用顶栏「评论」可随时跳到这里。
顶栏三个标签切换导读、正文与评论。正文工具栏可开关列（至少一列）；可见列横向均分撑满。英文「文本抽取」列支持划词查词：离线词库。快捷键：←/→ 或 K/J。
